| R11.9 | How is a Bog Check made? | D8.2, D8.21, B9.4: a vehicle entering, or changing its VCA in, a hex whose Terrain Chart entry for it carries a B (not by road or VBM), and a halftrack crossing a hedge, makes a Bog DR at the entry; a Final DR of 12 or more bogs it and ends its MPh at once, in the Bog hex (for a hedge, the hex it left, B9.4). DRM (D8.21, p. 209): +1 Normal Ground Pressure (no GP printed on the counter), +2 High, 0 Low (the T-34); +1 towing a Gun; +1 not fully-tracked; +1 truck-type MP; +1 an Abrupt Elevation Change; +1 gaining elevation into woods; +3 woods, wooden building, or rubble at half the allotment; +4 stone building at half the allotment. Mud, snow, and wire do not arise. A halftrack crossing a hedge into a Bog hex takes the hedge's DR first, then the hex's (referee finding). A bogged vehicle is Immobile: it may not leave or change its VCA, and in the MPh it bogged it is a moving target if it entered a new hex or began in Motion (D8.4). |
| R11.10 | How is a vehicle freed? | D8.3, D8.31: at the start of its MPh, as its first expenditure, a bogged vehicle that did not Prep Fire and is otherwise Mobile spends as its Start MP the colored dr times the white dr (doubled for a truck); a Final colored dr of 1 to 4 frees it (even beyond its allotment), 5 Mires it, 6 or more immobilizes it; +1 drm while Mired. A freed vehicle may use any MP left. The Start MP opens the DEFENDER's window, with no Case J (D8.4). Assistance by another AFV and armor leaders are not built. |
| R11.11 | How does a vehicle OVR? | D7.1 to D7.17: a moving vehicle not in Reverse, not in VBM, not marked Bounding Fire, entering an enemy-occupied Location may declare an OVR with the entry, paying a quarter of its printed allotment (FRU) more, never with an ALL entry or a Minimum Move, which leave no MP for it (D2.7; referee finding); not in the hex it began its MPh in unless it left and came back, and a second OVR only after leaving the hex of the first (D7.13, D7.14). Any Bog DR and the DEFENDER's First Fire at that expenditure come first; the ATTACKER then resolves the OVR before any other expenditure. FP: 1 for an unarmored vehicle, 2 for an AFV, 4 for an AFV whose MA is a Gun and is manned and functioning, plus its functioning MG FP tripled and halved (the AAMG only while CE; BMG and CMG count, since their FP is printed); the OVR is halved when the vehicle is immobile or destroyed before it resolves (a Bog, or DEFENDER First Fire; a destroyed vehicle still resolves it, referee finding) and against concealed units (Area Fire), never for Motion. It attacks every non-armored enemy unit in the Location on the IFT with their TEM, FFMO -1 against Infantry in Open Ground, and a wall or hedge TEM only when the vehicle entered across that hexside; no leadership (no Armor Leaders), no Hindrance. An Original DR of 12 malfunctions one weapon that added FP, chosen by Random Selection among them (A9.71), or immobilizes a vehicle with none (a truck). AFVs are not attacked, but a CE crew is, as Vulnerable PRC (D7.12). CC Reaction Fire at the OVRing vehicle waits until the OVR is resolved (D7.2; referee finding). An OVR is declared only when the Fire package would resolve it; one whose attack stops at an owner's option is resolved when the answer resumes it; and a vehicle that bogs entering the Location still resolves it, its move ending after the Reaction window (table-player findings). |
| R11.12 | What happens when a vehicle enters a Location of concealed enemy units? | A12.41 (replacing the R6.8 deviation): the concealed or hidden enemy Personnel there, other than SMC, Fanatic, and berserk units, are either revealed or take one combined PAATC, as their owner chooses (a pending choice, R5.8): a NTC on the lowest Morale Level among them (1TC if any is Inexperienced), modified by the best unpinned Good Order leader present; a Dummy stack uses 7. On failure all are pinned and revealed; on success they stay concealed and can be OVR only as Area Fire. A Location holding only unknown units is OVR only after that choice, by a separate declaration then. |
| R11.13 | How is Reaction Fire made? | D7.2 to D7.22: in the DEFENDER's window after a vehicle's MP expenditure in (or OVR of) a DEFENDER's Location, an unbroken, unpinned, armed Infantry unit there, not in Melee, may make a CC Reaction Fire attack on the vehicle, resolved as CC against a vehicle (R11.14) with no vehicle attack back; against a manned AFV it first passes a PAATC (a failure pins it), once per phase per AFV; a unit marked First or Final Fire reduces its CCV by one; it is then marked with a CC counter (no Non-CC Reaction Fire) and First Fire. Non-CC Reaction Fire is the DEFENDER's ordinary fire from within the Location at the OVRing vehicle, as the Fire and Ordnance packages decide it. The OVR's Reaction Fire window follows its resolution; fire from within a moving vehicle's Location at it is made only then, never on its other MP expenditures there (D7.22; table-player finding). FPF Reaction Fire (D7.212, D7.221), Street Fighting (D7.211, A11.8), and Gun crews' Reaction Fire (D7.23) are not built. |
| R11.14 | How does Infantry attack a vehicle in CC? | A11.5 to A11.612, A11.621: one unit, or one unit with a SMC adding one, attacks a vehicle with the CCV as its Kill Number: squad 5, crew 4, HS 3, SMC 2, -1 Inexperienced, -1 for each halving (pinned, and the Reaction Fire marker). DRM: -3 against an unarmored vehicle; -1 against a vehicle with no manned functioning MG (none, or only a BU AAMG, or Stunned or Shocked); -2 against an OT AFV; -1 against a CE CT AFV; -1 against an Immobile AFV (not a truck; referee finding); +1 per unbroken, unpinned, armed enemy HS or crew and +2 per such squad in the Location; +2 against a Motion or Non-Stopped vehicle; the attacking SMC's leadership (only when combining); CX +1 (A4.51). A Final DR below the CCV eliminates it (a burning wreck at half or less), equal immobilizes it; an Original 2 lets the attacker roll the Unlikely Kill dr (1 burns, 2 eliminates, 3 immobilizes, and an elimination or immobilization the DR gave stands); an Original 12 against a crewed vehicle not Shocked or Stunned Casualty Reduces the attackers. The AF plays no part. |
| R11.15 | How does a vehicle attack Infantry in CC? | A11.62: with its AAMG only while CE, and its CMG (the BMG never), combined or apart, against the defenders' CCV (each MMC's CCV, -1 Inexperienced and -1 pinned, at least one; a SMC adds one defending with a MMC, as in the A11.622 example's 5-6 and 5-16 odds, and has its CCV of 2 defending alone; referee finding), on the CCT's black Kill Numbers with the Infantry CC DRM against its defenders; halved when in Motion and against concealed units; a Stunned or Shocked vehicle has none. Each attack Casualty Reduces or eliminates as Infantry CC does. The Close Defense Weapon System (sN) and Riders are not in the catalog. |
| R11.16 | How is CC in a Location with a vehicle run? | A11.31, A11.7, A11.52: every attack there is declared and resolved one at a time; the non-vehicular side attacks first, then the sides alternate, a side with no attack left passing, until every unit has attacked, passed, or been eliminated; a side that passes passes with every unit it has left there (a reading); with opposing vehicles the ATTACKER begins. Infantry attacks against Infantry there are not built (table-player finding; backlog section 21): such Infantry stay in Melee and may Withdraw from it as usual (A11.2). No Ambush dr is made in a Location holding a vehicle (a deviation: A11.34 would let the ambusher attack first; backlog section 1). A vehicle is never held in Melee; a non-Abandoned vehicle that is not in Motion holds the Known (not concealed) enemy Infantry in its Location in Melee after the CCPh (A11.7). An unarmed vehicle (a truck) not in Motion and without Personnel of its side is captured as a CCPh begins with enemy Infantry in its Location (referee finding): it is marked captured and Abandoned, and neither moves nor fires (A21.2's use of captured vehicles is not built). A squad Reduced to a HS in that CC has made its attack, and so has the HS (table-player finding). |
| R11.17 | May Infantry advance into an enemy vehicle's Location? | A11.6, A4.14: in the APh, yes; a MMC advancing into the Location of a manned, unconcealed enemy AFV must first pass a PAATC (a NTC, 1TC if Inexperienced), modified by a Good Order leader in its Location, or be pinned; SMC, Fanatic, and berserk units are exempt. In the MPh Infantry still do not enter a Location with an unconcealed enemy vehicle. A berserk charge at a vehicle stays undecided and ends in place, as since pass 4 (backlog). |
| R11.18 | What else is out? | Reverse Motion, Motion Attempts, Trail Breaks, a Bypass out of an enemy AFV's LOS, tracing fire to a CAFP, the B23.41 building entry, Armor Leaders, Bog assistance, mud and snow, FPF and Street Fighting Reaction Fire, Gun crews' Reaction Fire, Ambush with a vehicle, the use of captured vehicles, Passengers and Riders in OVR and CC, and A5.132's hits on other vehicles stay in the backlog (section 21). |
| R12.1 | How does Opportunity Fire work? | A7.25, A7.24: in its PFPh, the owner designates Good Order Infantry of his side that have not fired, directed fire, or moved this Player Turn, and are not berserk, in Melee, or prisoners, as Opportunity Firers: each is marked with a Bounding Fire counter (the drawn `asl:bounding-fire` condition), and one in the LOS of a Good Order enemy ground unit within 16 hexes loses its "?" (Concealment Table Case D). Such a unit may not fire in the PFPh or move in the MPh; it may advance (A7.25 does not bar it). In the AFPh, if unbroken, it fires with no AFPh halving (A7.24) and keeps Multiple ROF on its MG (the only unit that may, A7.25; no other AFPh MG keeps it, fixed for SW MGs too); Assault Fire adds nothing to Opportunity Fire (A7.36; referee finding). It is marked Prep Fire once it fires; the Bounding Fire counter stays until the AFPh ends. Ordnance, mortars, and LATW Opportunity Fire are not built (backlog). |
| R12.2 | What does fire at a blocked LOS do? | A6.11, A7.52: an attack declared at a Location to which the firers' LOS is blocked is still fire: a DR is made (Random Events are not built; the DR decides only whether each MG keeps Multiple ROF, and no MG malfunctions on it, a reading of "a DR must still be made ... for the retention of any Multiple ROF"), the firers and their MGs are marked as having fired (Prep, First, or Final Fire as the phase gives), directing leaders are marked as having directed, and a concealed firer loses its "?" as for any fire (Case B). Nothing in the target Location is affected, no DM results, and no Residual FP is left. In a multi-Location fire group, the units whose LOS is blocked make that DR first and drop out; the others must attack as a smaller group, with the rest of the group's facts (A7.52). The Fire package decides a blocked attack as a resolution with no target effects. |
| R12.3 | How are the FPF variants resolved? | A8.31, A7.83: (a) a leader may direct FPF: his leadership DRM modifies the attack and the FPF NMC DR, and he takes that NMC with the FPF firers (Random Selection for a Casualty MC or Heat of Battle among two or more). (b) FPF firers may form a group with Subsequent First Fire firers (First-Fire-marked units, within A8.3's limits), all then marked Final Fire; both kinds are Area Fire, and only the FPF firers (and the directing leader) take the NMC; the FPF firers must be ADJACENT or in the Location, as before. An unmarked unit's First Fire does not join FPF, since its marker would differ (referee finding). (c) One attack on a moving stack mixing pinned and unpinned movers is resolved with one DR: the unpinned units' Final DR includes FFMO and FFNAM, the pinned units' does not (A7.83), as A12.13 resolves concealed and unconcealed units on one DR. |
| R12.4 | How do split fire and SMC fire work? | A7.351 to A7.353, A7.53, A9.12: a squad fires one MG apart from its inherent FP, in either order within the phase: the MG's attack alone (`withoutInherent`) does not mark the squad as having fired its inherent FP, and the squad's inherent-FP attack does not use the MG; a squad firing two MGs, or a HS, crew, or SMC firing any SW, loses its inherent FP (A7.352), as before, and a squad fires no more than two SW in a phase (referee finding). A leader directs one of the two attacks only (A7.53; the pass 9 director record already keeps this). A leader fires a MG alone as Area Fire (halved); two SMC stacked together fire one MG at full FP, named as its operator and his partner; a leader who fires or helps fire a SW gives no leadership DRM that phase, neither to that attack nor to another, so one who has directed fire this phase fires no MG, and a leader fires one MG a phase; its MG is exempt from Cowering (A9.12; referee finding). A hero's own SW use (A15.23) is still not reviewed. |
| R12.5 | How is concealment gained in play? | A12.12, A12.121, A12.122, the Concealment Table (p. 107): at the end of each Player Turn (when the CCPh ends), each non-concealed Good Order Infantry unit of the phasing side not manning a Gun (unbroken, not berserk, not in Melee, not a prisoner) is checked. The game reads the LOS of every unbroken enemy ground unit to it: with one in LOS within 16 hexes, no "?" (Case NA); with one in LOS only at 17 hexes or more, a unit in Concealment Terrain gains "?" on a Final Concealment dr of 5 or less (Case I), one not in it gains none; with none in LOS, a unit in Concealment Terrain gains "?" (Case J), and one not in it gains "?" if no unbroken enemy ground unit is within 16 hexes, or else on a Final Concealment dr of 5 or less (Case K). Concealment Terrain is the terrain the Terrain Chart lists in red (p. 698): brush, woods, orchard, grain in season, marsh, wooden and stone buildings, and rubble. The dr adds the unit's US# (squad 3, HS or crew 2, SMC 1), the Leadership of the best Good Order leader in the Location unless the unit is that leader, and subtracts the Location's TEM (hexside and Height Advantage TEM excluded), 2 for SMOKE in the Location (the only in-hex Hindrance that hinders its own units, A6.7; referee finding), and 1 for a Stealthy unit (none in the catalog; Lax is not built). A unit sharing its Location with enemy units, about to be held in Melee, gains no "?" (a reading). Each unit rolls its own dr. Vehicles, Guns, and Dummy creation are not built (backlog). |
| R12.6 | How does Spraying Fire work? | A9.5 to A9.52: in the PFPh, AFPh, or DFPh, a fire group whose every firer uses a SW MG (no other SW; referee finding) (the MG's holder may add its inherent FP, as in the A9.5 example) may attack two Locations in its LOS that share a hexside, neither its own Location: each Location is attacked as Area Fire (halved), with its own TEM and Hindrance, and both with the same Original IFT DR; the MGs' Multiple ROF and malfunction are decided once, on that DR, and the firers are marked once. Spraying Fire has no effect on an AFV (A9.51). A First-Fire-marked unit sprays in Final Fire only at two ADJACENT Locations (A9.52). Spraying Fire in the MPh and at upper-level Locations of one hex are not built, and Spraying Fire does not count toward an Encirclement (backlog; a limit the referee disputed). |
| R12.7 | How does a Fire Lane work? | A9.22, A9.222, A9.223: when the DEFENDER declares Defensive First Fire (not Subsequent First Fire or FPF, not TPBF) with a Good Order SW MG manned by unpinned Infantry, within its Normal Range at a same-level target, and not already First or Final Fire marked, he may declare a Fire Lane along the Hex Grain through the MG's hex and the target hex, naming the Location where its counter goes (in or beyond the target hex, within the MG's Normal Range); the MG is marked First Fire. If the manning Infantry did not Cower, the lane exerts Fire Lane Residual FP in every same-level Location of the grain from the MG to the counter that is within its Normal Range and in its manning Infantry's LOS: the IFT column to the left of the MG's printed FP, doubled in an ADJACENT hex (PBF). A moving unit is attacked by it in each such Location where it expends MF, after any other Residual FP there: TEM, FFMO and FFNAM apply; a SMOKE, grain, brush, or marsh Hindrance on the LOS from the MG cancels FFMO but applies no DRM (A9.222; referee finding), and a wall or hedge TEM of the lane is not built (backlog); the Residual FP is never reduced (A8.26 does not apply), and no CX, leader, or hero DRM applies; it cannot Cower. An Original DR at least the MG's Breakdown Number malfunctions the MG. The lane is removed when the MG malfunctions, its manning Infantry breaks, is pinned, or is eliminated, or the MPh ends; the MG does not fire again until the DFPh, nor does its manning Infantry use Subsequent First Fire or FPF while the lane stands (referee finding). Alternate Hex Grains, Snap Shots from a lane, intersecting lanes, the TPBF cancellation, and Impulse movement are not built (backlog). |
| R12.8 | May units fire into a Melee? | A11.15, A7.212: fire from outside at a Location holding units in Melee attacks every unit there, of both sides, with one DR; each side's units fail their MC by more than their own side's ELR, and the firing side's units in it are attacked as any other target. Units held in Melee still fire only in CC (A11.15, as before). A concealed unit's TPBF into its own Melee Location waits for concealment in CC (pass 14). |
| R12.9 | May units fire at prisoners, and may a Guard fire? | A20.52, A20.54: fire from outside at a Location holding prisoners attacks the Guard and its prisoners as if in Melee: a prisoner is never broken and suffers Casualty Reduction when it fails a MC; it is never pinned itself but is pinned when its Guard is; a prisoner leader takes no LLMC or LLTC; the Casualty Reduction of a K/# result falls on the Guard or a prisoner by Random Selection. A Guard whose US# (squad 3, HS or crew 2, SMC 1) is less than its prisoners' total US# fires only at its prisoners (Massacre, as before) and uses no SW; any other Guard fires normally. A Guard advancing into CC stays refused (backlog section 11). |
| R12.10 | When may a berserk unit fire? | A15.432: a berserk unit never fires in its PFPh and never uses Opportunity Fire; it fires in its AFPh (the AFPh halving applies, and the TPBF A15.432 describes is the ordinary TPBF of A7.21 at the enemy in its Location) and in the DFPh and the opponent's MPh. It directs no fire (a berserk leader gives no leadership, A15.41, A15.42). |
| R12.11 | How does Encirclement work? | A7.7, A7.71: in a PFPh, DFPh, or AFPh (not the MPh), the attacks of one side on one target Location made consecutively (no attack by that side at another Location between them) by two or more units firing inherent FP or SW at no more than Normal Range can Encircle it. The game reads where each firer's LOS enters the target hex: through a hexspine or a hexside. The Location is Encircled when the entries include two opposite hexspines, two opposite hexsides (exactly three vertices between them both ways), or three hexsides no two of which are adjacent; a fire group's entries all count, so one group can Encircle. Only valid attacks count: one whose FP could inflict at least a NMC on an Original DR of 2 (with its Cowering) or 3; ordnance and Residual FP never count. The attack that completes it Encircles the Location before it is resolved: every non-berserk, non-heroic Personnel unit of the target's side there (and every Melee unit there) has its Morale Level one lower against that attack and every later attack on the Location while it stays Encircled; each such unit's fire has +1 on the IFT DR; and the first Location it enters in a move, advance, withdrawal, or rout (pass 13) costs twice its MF. Only Personnel are Encircled (referee finding). An enemy unit entering the Location is Encircled at once. The Encirclement ends when no such unit is left there (all left, eliminated, captured, or berserk or heroic). Upper-level Encirclement (A7.72), LOS from the Locations directly above and below, ordnance hits as valid fire, the ordnance To Hit +1, Vulnerable PRC of an Immobile vehicle, and the capture effects (A20.21) are not built (backlog). |
| R12.12 | What else is out? | Random Events (A6.11's DR), Sniper, TI units, Impulse movement, pillboxes, and night; the Spotter's wait after Opportunity Fire (C9.3, backlog section 19) stays in the backlog. |
| R13.1 | When is a broken unit under DM, and how long does it keep it? | A10.62: as before, a unit is under DM in the Player Turn it breaks, and a broken unit attacked by FP that could inflict at least a NMC. Pass 13 adds: a broken unit is under DM whenever a Known armed enemy unit (Personnel not a prisoner, or a vehicle not Abandoned) is ADJACENT to it or in its Location; the game reads this after each step of a move, advance, or rout, and at every phase change, so a unit passed by an enemy mid-phase keeps it. At the start of the RtPh a broken unit is also under DM in a Blaze Location or in Open Ground (R13.3) in the LOS and Normal Range of a Known enemy unit. DM leaves at the end of every RPh, except on a unit its owner names to keep it (`retainDm` on the phase advance), which must not be in a woods or building Location; overstacking, pillboxes, and trenches are not built. |
| R13.2 | Where does the -1 terrain DRM for rallying apply? | A10.61: in any woods or building Location (any level); nowhere else on board 01: Open Ground, roads, brush, orchard, grain, marsh, and rubble give 0. Pillboxes and trenches do not arise; rooftops are not built. |
| R13.3 | How does the RtPh work? | A10.5 to A10.533: the ATTACKER's broken units rout first, one at a time, each with one action naming its whole route (`asl.game.rout`); then the DEFENDER's. A broken unit not in Melee must rout when it is in Open Ground in the LOS and Normal Range of a Known unbroken enemy unit not in Melee (one able to fire; table player, pass 13), or ADJACENT to or in the Location of a Known unbroken armed enemy unit, (a Blaze is not built: a Wreck Blaze does not bar movement, B25.141); it may rout when under DM; otherwise it may not. It has 6 MF (a wounded SMC 3), paying the terrain costs of pass 10 without the Road Bonus or Bypass. No step enters a Location holding a Known enemy unit, or ADJACENT to one unless it leaves that unit's Location, or (while in its LOS) decreases the range to a Known armed enemy unit, or, for a unit that started ADJACENT to one, ends ADJACENT to that same unit. Its destination is the nearest woods or building hex in MF over such steps within its MF (computed at the start of its rout; one no farther from a Known enemy unit than the start may be ignored, or chosen if no farther in MF); it must reach it this RtPh, not necessarily by a shortest route, and stops on reaching a woods or building Location not ADJACENT to a Known enemy unit (continuing only directly into another). With none reachable, any legal route. Low Crawl is a route of one Location using all its MF, never out of an enemy-occupied Location, and is never Interdicted. Entering Open Ground (a hex where FFMO could apply: Open Ground or a road hex with no Hindrance on the LOS) in the LOS and Normal Range (at most 16 hexes) of an unbroken enemy Infantry unit able to fire (not CX, pinned, Encircled, in Melee, or a leader without a SW) with no Hindrance on the LOS is Interdiction: a NMC against the unit's broken Morale Level; failed, Casualty Reduction (a HS goes on routing); a Final DR equal to it, pinned, and the rout ends there; an Original 12, eliminated; once per hex. Vehicles do not Interdict (backlog). As the RtPh ends, a broken unit that did not rout from Open Ground in the LOS and Normal Range of a Known enemy unit, or that ends ADJACENT to or in the Location of a Known unbroken armed enemy unit, is eliminated for Failure to Rout, unless it surrenders (A20.21): a broken Infantry unit ADJACENT to Known Good Order armed enemy Infantry (not in Melee, berserk, or vehicular) that can rout away only with Interdiction or Low Crawl, or that is Disrupted or Encircled, may not rout and surrenders instead as the RtPh ends, abandoning its SW, as does any such unit about to be eliminated for Failure to Rout; Fanatic units and units faced with No Quarter never surrender this way; they are eliminated instead (A20.21). Concealed enemy units are ignored; the A10.533 repulse is not built (backlog). **Amended by R35.1 to R35.4 (pass 35): Open Ground is read by the FFMO a given enemy could apply; a unit bound to surrender owes no rout; the A10.533 repulse is built; no voluntary rout without DM.** |
| R13.4 | How do squads Deploy and Recombine? | A1.31, A1.32: in its own side's RPh a Good Order squad (not Disrupted, not in Melee, not TI) with a Good Order leader of its nationality in its Location attempts to Deploy by a NTC modified by that leader (Guards need no leader and take the NTC unmodified); passed, it becomes two HS of its type, the first keeping its SW unless the player names some for the second. A leader directs one Deployment attempt a RPh, a squad makes one, and neither the leader nor the squad, nor the HS it becomes, takes any other RPh action (rally, repair, Recovery, Transfer); a leader who directed a Rally attempt directs none. Two Good Order HS of one definition in a Location with a Good Order leader of their nationality (Guards need none) Recombine automatically into their squad, keeping their SW; a Fanatic HS with another makes an unfanatic squad. It is their sole RPh action, and the new squad's; the leader may permit any number of Recombinations in his Location, and no other RPh action. |
| R13.5 | How are SW transferred, dropped, and Recovered? | A4.43, A4.431, A4.44: a SW is transferred between Good Order unpinned units of one side in one Location in their RPh (not an RPh action; a unit that Deployed, Recombined, or Recovered transfers nothing), or in the ATTACKER's APh before either advances, never in the phase it was Recovered. An unbroken unit drops a SW it possesses in its MPh (at any point in its move; table player, pass 13), its APh (before it advances), or at the start of the CCPh; a broken unit that routs keeps SW up to its IPC and drops only the rest first (A10.4 reading). Recovery of an unpossessed SW, friendly or enemy, in the unit's Location: as its sole RPh action, or in its MPh for one extra MF at any point in its move while it has that MF left (table player, pass 13), once per SW per MPh, by an unpinned Good Order unit in a Location with no armed Known enemy unit, on a Final dr below 6 (+1 CX). A SMC Recovers a SW its friendly broken unit possesses the same way, with no MF. |
| R13.6 | How are SW dismantled and assembled? | A9.8: the German MMG may be dismantled or assembled by its possessor in a PFPh or DFPh in which it has not fired; this counts as its use, and it is marked as having fired; in the PFPh it is also its possessor's use of a SW, so the unit does not move (table player, pass 13). A dismantled weapon is not fired, and portages at half its PP (FRU). The German dm MMG firing as a LMG, mortars, and starting dismantled are not built (backlog); the catalog has no other weapon A9.8 names (Russian MGs are excluded). |
| R13.7 | How are captured SW used? | A21.1 to A21.12: a unit possessing an enemy nationality's MG or ATR (by Recovery) fires it with its B# two lower and its Multiple ROF one lower. Captured ordnance, mortars, and LATW, and captured Guns and vehicles, are not built (backlog). |
| R13.8 | Which catalog units may Self-Rally? | A10.6, A10.63: broken leaders and crews, as before, and one MMC per Player Turn as a Field Promotion attempt (A18.11). A10.6 grants MMC Self-Rally capability to Finns only, so a German or Russian MMC whose catalog definition leaves the capability unrecorded has none; the Rally package decides it instead of leaving the attempt undecided. |
| R14.1 | How is Hand-to-Hand CC played? | J2.31 itself is not in the registered PDF; the CCT prints the red Kill Numbers (A11.11, p. 72) and G1.64, A25.43, and W.6B say Hand-to-Hand may be declared voluntarily only by SSR (or in Deluxe ASL) and never by or against vehicles, PRC, or pillbox occupants. So: where the game's SSRs include `hand-to-hand`, the ATTACKER may declare the CC of a Location Hand-to-Hand with its first round other than the prisoners' (the ambusher's first round after an Ambush) in a CCPh, unless the ATTACKER was ambushed there (A25.43, G1.64; referee, pass 14); every CC attack there in that CCPh, by both sides, then uses the red Kill Number of its odds column. Never in a Location holding a vehicle. The rest of J2.31, and Gurkha and Japanese Hand-to-Hand (A25.43, G1.64), are not built (backlog). |
| R14.2 | How do concealed and hidden units and Dummies take part in CC? | A11.19, A12.14, A11.4: an advance may enter a Location of concealed or hidden enemy units or Dummies, and a concealed unit keeps its "?" advancing into CC. When a Location's CC starts (its Ambush drs, or its first round when none is due), its Dummies are removed and its hidden units are placed beneath a "?" (A11.19; ASOP 8.11B: Dummies go before the Ambush dr). An Ambush can then occur where Infantry advanced into CC in woods or a building, or with or against a concealed unit, in any terrain, or where a hidden unit was placed (A11.4), with -2 to the dr of a force any of whose units is concealed. An attack whose defenders include a concealed unit has its attacking FP halved (A11.19), cumulative with pinned. A unit loses its "?" when it makes or directs a CC attack, unless its side has Ambush status and the attack eliminates or captures every defender; when it is Casualty Reduced or wounded; and, on the ambushed side, at the Ambush (A11.4, A12.14). A Known SMC declared stacked with a concealed MMC, or a concealed SMC with a Known MMC, makes the concealed one forfeit its "?" before the attacks, so attacks on them are not halved (A11.14). A concealed unit is neither held in Melee nor holds an enemy unit in Melee (A11.15 EXC). |
| R14.3 | What do TI units do in CC? | A4.8 and the CCT's "vs CX/TI" row: +1 to a CC attack a TI unit makes (once per attack, however many take part), and -1 to a CC attack against a TI unit, for that defender alone, as the CX DRM (R5.2). Nothing in the game places TI yet (TI tasks are other passes); the refusal of a Location holding a TI unit is removed. |
| R14.4 | How is a capture attempt made? | A20.22, A19.35, A20.2: a CC attack by Infantry none of whose attackers is berserk, against defenders none of which is berserk, may be declared a capture attempt: +1 to its DR, or -1 instead against an Inexperienced defender (for that defender alone). A defender whose Final DR is below the Kill Number is captured, not eliminated; at the Kill Number, one defending unit of the defender's choice among those at it is captured, the rest unharmed: a squad chosen is exchanged for two HS, one captured and one not (the A20.22 EX). The defender's choice is declared with the round (`yield` per attack; a reading: the round is resolved in one action, and the choice cannot change the dice); an attack with several defenders and no declared choice is refused before any roll. A captured unit abandons its SW in the Location (A20.24; no A11.13 loss dr), becomes Unarmed, and is placed with its Guard: the unit the capturing side names, or else the first attacker of the attack left in the Location with Guard capacity (A20.51: its prisoners' US# at most five times its own), or else any unit of that side there with capacity; with none it is freed as an Unarmed unit of its own side (A20.5). A20.221: when a simultaneous round eliminates or captures every non-prisoner unit of a side in the Location, that side's captures in the round are void and the units it would have captured are unchanged. |
| R14.5 | What is an Unarmed unit, and what does a Guard do in CC? | A20.5, A20.51, A20.52, A20.54: a captured unit is Unarmed, a condition it keeps when freed, with a CC FP of one in attack and defense; an Unarmed unit never fires, never uses or Recovers a SW, never Interdicts, and, when not a prisoner, never moves or advances into a Location of a Known enemy unit; it is never broken. US#: squad 3, HS or crew 2 (a crew becomes an Unarmed HS), SMC 1 (A1.6, A20.51). An Unarmed MMC is Inexperienced (A19.3: Lax, and -1 instead of +1 to a capture attempt on it; referee, pass 14). An Unarmed SMC keeps its Morale Level and leadership (A20.5). A Guard may advance into CC, its prisoners going with it (A20.53; R29.19 is withdrawn); its CC FP is halved when it attacks non-prisoners (A20.52, the CCT). Prisoners in a CC Location take no part in its CC but for an escape attempt, and a CC attack against a Guard does not affect them (A20.54). When a Guard is eliminated, captured, or leaves play, another armed unit of its side in the Location with capacity takes the prisoners, the first by id (a reading of "any unit capable ... may immediately assume"); with none, they are freed as Unarmed units of their own side. A Guard Reduced or Replaced keeps them in the unit that takes its place; a Guard is never a berserk unit or one withdrawing (A20.2; referee, pass 14). Overstacking never counts prisoners (A20.51). |
| R14.6 | How do prisoners escape, and how are they rearmed? | A20.55, A11.33, A11.34, A20.551: in the CCPh, prisoners whose Guard is broken, or held in Melee, may attack in CC; each whose Guard is not in Melee first passes a NTC on its Morale Level (an Unarmed MMC keeps its unit's Morale Level: no registered source prints an Unarmed counter's, so under R0.3 it is the unit's own), made in the same action; one that fails does not attack. Their attacks form one round declared at once and resolved before any other CC in the Location (A11.33 takes precedence over an Ambush, A11.34), and every attack includes the Guard (a reading: A20.55 lets later attacks leave out a Guard an earlier one eliminated; backlog). A prisoner that attacks is no longer guarded: it is an Unarmed unit of its side, held in Melee at the end of the CCPh if enemy units remain (a reading of the A20.55 EX); a SMC among them is Armed again once its escape succeeds, with no enemy unit but prisoners left in the Location or by Infiltration ("Escaped SMC are always Armed"; referee, pass 14). An attacking Unarmed MMC is rearmed as a Conscript squad or HS of its size and nationality (the catalog has no Green MMC) for each armed enemy unit of at least its size its attack eliminated or captured, one Unarmed attacker per such unit. Prisoners' Withdrawal from Melee, recapture in the MPh, Scrounging, and the A20.221 exchange of prisoners left alone are not built (backlog). |
| R14.7 | How are prisoners transferred or abandoned? | A20.5: in its side's RPh or APh, a Guard not held in Melee may transfer its prisoners to another armed unit of its side in its Location with Guard capacity (as a SW transfer, A4.431, which costs neither unit its RPh or APh action), or abandon them: they are freed as Unarmed units of their own side, still protected by A20.3 and A20.4. Freed Unarmed units move as their owner's units (A20.5). |
| R14.8 | How is Infiltration offered? | A11.22: a unit's destination is declared with the round (`infiltrations`: a reading, since the round is resolved in one action; a unit with none declines). The ATTACKER's attacks are resolved first, then the DEFENDER's (A11.12), so the order decides which attacks come after an Original 2 or 12. After an attack with an Original 2, each of its attacking Infantry units with a destination that the attacks resolved before it did not eliminate, capture, or pin withdraws, and no later attack affects it; a leader it created withdraws with it, and no other attack is re-figured for him (A18.12: "Unless one or both sides Withdraw due to Infiltration"). After an attack with an Original 12, each attacked unit it did not eliminate or capture, not pinned, that has a destination withdraws: it makes no attack not yet resolved, and no later attack affects it. The destination is one a withdrawal from Melee could reach (A11.21: ADJACENT, Accessible, no Known enemy unit; CX when the advance would need it), whether or not the unit was in Melee. |
| R14.9 | How is Ambush Withdrawal made? | A11.41: after an Ambush, units of the ambushing side that are not pinned, berserk, or Disrupted may withdraw to a Location a withdrawal could reach, either before the Location's first round (declining CC) or once its CC is over this CCPh (before Melee develops). The withdrawal is its own action and record, CX when the advance would need it; when every ambusher has left, the ambushed side's round closes the Location (referee, pass 14). |
| R14.10 | How do overstacking penalties work in CC and on an advance? | A5.1, A5.5, A5.11, A5.12, A5.131: a side's excess in a Location is its squad-equivalents above three, rounded up, a HS or crew counting half and each five SMC a HS (four or fewer SMC count none), prisoners never counted. Each of that side's CC attacks there takes +1 per excess, and each CC attack against its units -1 per excess. An advance into a Location overstacked by it costs one more MF per excess squad-equivalent (A5.11), which may make it an advance into Difficult Terrain (A4.72); overstacked advances are no longer refused. |
| R14.11 | When does a Disrupted unit surrender outside the RtPh? | A19.12: a Disrupted unit, not rallied, in a Location that Good Order armed Known enemy Personnel not in Melee advance into surrenders to them, whether or not it is itself in Melee (referee, pass 14), as a Heat of Battle surrender does (the captor's choice, R30.7, R5.6), unless its side faces No Quarter. The other "during any phase" cases are not built (backlog). |
| R14.12 | Which MMC founds a leader created in CC, and which does he defend with? | A18.2 (with R29.12, confirmed by the user on 2026-09-28: the created leader's leadership takes the director's place, A10.7): the MMC of the highest BPV among the attack's MMC, from the A./G. National Capabilities Chart (p. 109; German 4-6-7 10, 4-4-7 7, 4-3-6 5, 4-6-8 13; Russian 4-4-7 7, 5-2-7 7, 4-2-6 4, 4-5-8 11, 6-2-8 12, NKVD 6-2-8 13; HS 4, 3, 2, 5 and 3, 3, 1, 5, 5, 6), recorded with the Close Combat package; equal BPV in the catalog means equal Leader Creation drm, so the first is used. When an enemy attack takes some but not all of the attack's MMC, the MMC he defends with is chosen by Random Selection (A.9: one dr each, the highest; ties go to the first in the attack's declared order, a reading), with the roll key `leaderStack:<attack>`. Both refusals (R29.12) are removed. |
| R14.13 | Where do odds just above 10 to 1 fall, and which Ambush drm apply to a berserk force? | A11.11: odds are rounded down to the nearest printed ratio ("11 to 2 would be 4-1"), so odds of at least 10 to 1 and below 11 to 1 are 10-1, and "> 10-1" is reached at 11 to 1 (R29.16's refusal is removed; confirmed by the user, 2026-09-28). A11.4 "(each)": a berserk force takes +1 berserk and +1 Lax (A15.432: berserk units are Lax), as R29.9 built; the backlog row is closed as decided. |
| R14.14 | When may the CCPh end while a berserk or reinforcing unit has not attacked? | A15.43, A11.15 (replacing the R29.17 row): a Location whose CC is mandatory must have its round, unless the Close Combat package refuses every attack the unit could make there: the unit's attack alone on each Known enemy unit in turn, its friendly units left out but the Guards of the prisoners there. Then the requirement lapses, as the package cannot decide it. |
| R15.1 | How is a FT fired? | A22.1 to A22.5: a FT (catalog 1.10.0: a German and a Russian FT, 24 FP, range 1, removed on an Original DR of 10 or more; portage 5 manufactured under R0.3) is fired through `asl.game.fire` by one Good Order, unpinned Personnel unit naming it as its only weapon, alone: no other firer, no inherent FP (a squad may fire its inherent FP in a separate attack, A22.3), and no director. Its FP is 24 at range 0 or 1, never raised for PBF or TPBF, and 12 at range 2 (Long Range), which is refused through any Hindrance (A22.32: an obstructed LOS); never beyond two hexes or at a target more than two levels above or below, and 12 (6 at Long Range) at a target two levels away. It is halved as Area Fire (a concealed target, Subsequent First Fire, FPF, Final Fire by a First-Fire-marked unit), never for the AFPh, and shifted by Cowering. No leadership, heroic, or TEM DRM applies (in-hex, wall or hedge, Height Advantage, or an AFV's cover), but the LOS Hindrance, CX, FFMO, and FFNAM do (A22.2). A non-elite user's removal number is two lower (8), except a Finnish 1st Line user's (A25.74); a captured FT's is two lower again (A22.3, A21.11), and an Inexperienced or Conscript user's one lower (A19.32). A unit uses one FT or DC per Player Turn (A22.3): the game records every FT user, DC thrower, and DC placer for the Player Turn and refuses a second use. A unit that fired only its FT in the PFPh has Prep Fired: its FT is marked, it neither moves nor fires in the AFPh. A firer naming a FT fires it apart from its inherent FP without saying so. Every attack on a unit possessing a FT is one lower for that unit, per FT (A22.4), including an ordnance hit. A FT against an AFV and Flame creation are not built (backlog). |
| R15.2 | How is a DC Placed and detonated? | A23.1, A23.3, A23.4: an Infantry unit carrying a DC (catalog 1.10.0: a German and a Russian DC, 30 FP, removed on 12; portage 2 manufactured) may, in its MPh, Place it in an ADJACENT Location in its LOS by a `placeDc` step that spends the MF entering that Location would cost it, unless it fired in the PFPh or is pinned; a Location holding any vehicle, or one whose TEM the game has not reviewed, is refused (a DC's attack on a vehicle and the PAATC are backlog; referee and table player, pass 15), so every operable Placement can be detonated. The DC is operably Placed when the placer leaves the Placement Location or ends its move there neither broken nor pinned; until then a broken, pinned, or eliminated placer keeps it (or leaves it in the Location). In its side's AFPh the DC must be detonated (`asl.game.detonate-dc`) before the phase ends: 30 FP on the IFT, never modified for PBF, TPBF, or the AFPh, halved if every target unit it attacks was concealed when it was operably Placed; the target Location's in-hex TEM applies, not a hexside TEM or a Hindrance; +1 if the placer was CX when it Placed it; no leadership or heroic DRM and no Cowering (A7.9). An Original DR of 12 (10 for a non-elite owner other than a Finnish 1st Line one, two lower again for a captured DC, one lower for an Inexperienced or Conscript owner) removes it without effect (A23.2, A23.4, A25.74, A19.32). If a vehicle enters the Location later and the Fire package refuses the detonation, the AFPh still ends and the Placement lapses. Set DC and a DC against an AFV are not built (backlog). |
| R15.3 | How is a DC Thrown? | A23.6, A23.61 to A23.63: a Good Order unpinned (or berserk) Personnel unit may Throw its DC with `asl.game.throw-dc`, in a friendly fire phase or as Defensive First Fire, into an ADJACENT Location in its LOS at its own level (a DC Thrown to another level is backlog). Two attacks are made, each with its own DR: the target Location on the 30 FP column with +2, and the thrower's Location with +3 (each +1 more in the AFPh unless the thrower is an Opportunity Firer); a thrower two or more levels above the target is not attacked. Everything else is as R15.2. A DEFENDING unit marked First Fire may not Throw a DC (A23.63), nor may one in Subsequent First Fire or FPF. The DC is removed after the attack. Only the first DR, at the target Location, can malfunction it (as R15.2), which ends both attacks; the second DR, at the thrower's Location, never does (A23.4 EXC). A squad whose only fire this phase is its Thrown DC may still fire its inherent FP in that phase (A23.2), as after a FT. |
| R15.4 | How is a MOL used? | A22.6 to A22.62: where the game's SSRs include `mol:<side>`, a Personnel unit of that side declares a MOL (`mol` on `asl.game.fire`, one per attack) in a PBF or TPBF attack, never in Subsequent First Fire or FPF, never in Final Fire after it made a MOL Check in Defensive First Fire, never through a woods or orchard hexside (both hexes woods, or both orchard), and never with an AFV as the target (backlog). The unit must be unpinned and Good Order or berserk. Its MOL Check dr passes at 3 or less, +1 for a HS or crew, +2 for a SMC, +1 when CX, and +1 against a non-AFV (always, here). A pass adds 4 FP after every other modification; a failure leaves the attack without it, and a HS or crew that fails adds no FP of its own. A squad making the Check must use its inherent FP and fires no other SW. An Original colored dr of 6 breaks the MOL user and voids both its FP and the MOL's (the rest of the group still attacks); the Flame on a colored 1 or 6 is not built (backlog). Leadership applies as for any group. |
| R15.5 | When does a Sniper attack, and whom? | A14.1 to A14.3, A14.21 to A14.23: a side's Sniper counter (`asl:sniper`, placed at setup; a side with no counter on the map makes no Sniper attacks) attacks when the other side makes, in a PFPh, MPh, DFPh, or AFPh, an Original DR equal to its SAN: an IFT attack DR, or a MC or TC DR in the fire records, and a To Hit DR in the ordnance records (other DR are backlog). The game resolves the attack in the same commit, after the DR's own effects: a Sniper dr (1 or 2 is effective; the Sniper player never forfeits it, so a Sniper is never repositioned, backlog); a Random Location DR from the Sniper counter's hex (the colored dr a hexside direction, 1 the top hexside and on clockwise; the white dr the number of hexes, stopping at the last hex on the board); the closest hex holding an eligible target (Personnel of the attacked side not hidden or prisoner; an enemy Sniper counter is not a target here, and Interior Building Locations are not excluded; both are backlog), ties to the lowest TEM then the first hex in board order; in a hex with targets at several Locations, the Location with the most targets; Random Selection in the Location, a concealed stack counting as one target; dr 1 eliminates a SMC or Dummy stack, breaks a MMC (Casualty Reduces one that cannot break); dr 2 wounds a SMC (Wound Severity dr as usual), pins a MMC. Broken units in the Location take DM. No Collateral Attack, no Residual FP, no Heat of Battle from the Sniper dr itself; vehicles and Sniper Checks are backlog. |
| R15.6 | How are Commissars built? | A25.22 to A25.224: the Russian 9-0, 10-0, and 8+1 Commissars (catalog 1.10.0) are leaders. A Commissar is first to take an IFT-dictated MC in his Location, is exempt from LLMC and LLTC, and causes a LLMC in every other unit of his Location when he is eliminated or broken, whatever their morale (a LLTC when a TC causes it). A broken Commissar's duty to Self-Rally first is not enforced (backlog). While unpinned and unbroken he raises the Morale Level of every other friendly Infantry unit in his Location by one (not a Commissar or a unit of Morale Level 10) for MC, NTC, PTC, and Rally, and no other leader's DRM applies to their morale checks or rally there. He never takes another leader's DRM, never Disrupts, and is not subject to ELR Replacement or Battle Hardening; a Heat of Battle Surrender is Berserk for him. A broken unit in his Location rallies under his direction only, immune to DM for the attempt; a MMC failing it is Replaced by the next lower quality of its size (a squad already the lowest is Casualty Reduced; a HS, crew, or SMC that cannot be Replaced is eliminated). When he goes berserk, every friendly Infantry unit of his Location subject to Heat of Battle goes berserk too. His duty to rally every broken unit of his Location is enforced: the RPh may not end while such a unit has made no attempt and the Rally package would decide one. The scenario substitution of A25.22 is the setup's choice. |
| R15.7 | What does NKVD Field Promotion create? | A25.25: when a Field Promotion occurs for an NKVD MMC (A18.11 in a Rally, A18.12 in CC), the NKVD table replaces the Leader Creation Table: dr 1 a 10-0, 2 or 3 a 9-0, 4 or 5 an 8+1 Commissar, 6 or more none; drm -1 per odds column below 1-1 (and -1 for CC against an AFV), -1 when the base unit is Fanatic, +1 when it is broken. |
| R15.8 | How do Allied Troops take leadership? | A10.7: a unit of a nationality other than its side's is Allied Troops. A leader influencing Allied Troops of a nationality other than his own, in fire direction (the whole group's DRM), a MC or PTC of theirs, or a Rally, does so with his leadership one worse; they take a LLMC or LLTC based on that reduced modifier. A mixed group of one side may fire together. |
| R15.9 | What does an underscored Morale Factor change? | A1.23, A19.13: a MMC with an underscored Morale Factor has an ELR of 5, whatever its side's (referee, pass 15). An unbroken such squad that fails a MC by more than 5 is Replaced by its two broken HS (of its own class); such a HS is Disrupted instead, unless A19.12 exempts it (a Fanatic unit, or a Commissar). A Casualty MC failure beyond its ELR Reduces the squad to one broken HS of lesser quality, as for any squad. The same holds for an FPF firer's NMC. The SSR case of A19.132 is not built (backlog). |
| R15.10 | How do Green MMC work? | A19.2, A19.3: the Green MMC of catalog 1.10.0 (American 5-3-6, British 4-3-6, French 4-3-7, and their HS) are Inexperienced unless stacked with an unbroken leader, a fact the live game supplies from the stack (the rule was built in pass 1; the counters were missing). |
| R15.11 | How does a hero use a SW? | A15.23, A15.24: a hero fires a MG at full FP with +1 to its IFT DR, which his heroic -1 negates, and forfeits his inherent FP that phase; he uses a FT or DC as any Personnel unit may, with no heroic DRM (A15.24); his use of a LATW, a light mortar, or a Gun is not built (backlog). |
| R15.12 | Is a hero created by a concealed MMC concealed? | A15.21, A12.1: yes. The hero shares his MMC's status at creation, so a hero created by a concealed MMC is placed concealed too, as he is marked with its fire or TI counter. |
| R15.13 | Which nationalities are added, and with which exceptions? | A15.1, A18.2, A25: American, British, Italian, Finnish, and French MMC from the National Capabilities Chart (p. 109: three or four classes each, with their HS), a leader of every grade and a hero of each. Heat of Battle DRM: British and Finnish -1, French +1, Italian +3, American 0; a non-elite Italian surrenders on a Final Heat of Battle DR of 10 or more. Leader Creation drm: American and British -1, Italian +1, French 0; never for the Finns. Replacement and Battle Hardening follow each nationality's classes (A19.13, A15.3); the Italians (elite, 1st Line, line, conscript; A25.61, A25.62) and the Finns (their own ranks 8+1 to 10-1 and classes; A25.71, A25.72) by their own progressions, and a Finnish 1st Line or elite MMC, which has no better class, becomes Fanatic instead. British elite and 1st Line units, and Finns but their Conscripts, never Cower (A25.45, A25.7); Finns but Conscripts may Self-Rally (A25.7). Japanese units, Axis and Allied Minors, and the other A25 national rules are backlog. |
| R15.14 | Which SW does a berserk unit keep? | A15.431: at the start of its MPh, before charging, a berserk unit abandons each SW of more than 1 PP, and then 1 PP SW beyond its IPC; its owner names which 1 PP SW it keeps (`keep` on the charge); with none named the game keeps them in the order it holds them. A DC it keeps may only be Thrown. |
| R16.1 | How does a game declare night, and how does its NVR change? | E1.1, E1.12, E1.15: night is in effect only by SSR (E.1): `night:<n>` gives the Base NVR, 0 to 6; `night-clouds:<none|scattered|overcast>` and `night-moon:<none|half|full>` may name the sky (the DYO NVR Table roll is the scenario cards' work, pass 17). At the start of each RPh after the opening Player Turn the game makes the Wind Change DR (R16.10) itself: a colored 6 changes the Base NVR, a white dr of 3 or less lowering it, 5 or more raising it, and 4 raising it only before the first Starshell of the game; the change is one hex, or, with Scattered clouds and a Half or Full Moon, a further dr divided by three (Half) or two (Full), FRU. The Base NVR stays between 0 and 6, or 2 and 9 with Ground or Deep Snow. |
| R16.2 | What does a unit see at night? | E1.101, E1.13, E1.14, E1.8, E1.81, E1.9: a Location more than the firer's NVR hexes away is out of its LOS, unless it is Illuminated (R16.8) or marked by a Gunflash: a Prep, First, Final, Bounding, or Opportunity Fire counter, or a Melee, in that Location. Fire at a Gunflash beyond NVR, not Illuminated, is Area Fire as at a concealed target, halved once only. An Illuminated unit sees only Illuminated Locations and Gunflashes. A vehicle in Motion is within NVR at up to 1.5 times the NVR (FRU), twice for a tracked one; with an NVR of 0, at 1 hex if wheeled and 2 if tracked (referee, pass 16). A BU AFV's own NVR is halved (FRD), for its MA's fire too. A vehicle changing its VCA without moving is not treated as moving (backlog), and neither is Target Acquisition's night limit (E1.74): an Acquisition applies at night as by day. At night First and Final Fire counters stay until the end of the AFPh (E1.8). Fire at an unconcealed unit beyond NVR is therefore refused; such a unit is not Known to the viewer (E1.33). |
| R16.3 | Which night DRM apply to fire? | E1.7, E1.75: every night attack takes a +1 Low Visibility (LV) Hindrance DRM, cumulative with other Hindrances and never negating FFMO, except Fire Lanes, Snipers, DC, Residual FP, and CC; and except when the target has a positive TEM from Height Advantage, or its hex's terrain tops out at least a full level above the firer, or it is in the firer's hex. A fire group spanning more than one Location is refused at night. |
| R16.4 | How does concealment work at night? | E1.31 to E1.33: an Infantry unit loses "?" by moving only when it uses Non-Assault Movement in a Location already Illuminated, or enters an enemy-occupied Location in the MPh; a Location that becomes Illuminated during the move lets the mover stop there without the loss. Every situation that would call for a concealment dr (A12.122) gains "?" without one, and an enemy unit sees a Location for that purpose only within its NVR or when it is Illuminated. |
| R16.5 | How does movement change at night? | E1.51, E1.52: Infantry pay one more MF per Concealment Terrain Location entered, after every other modification (not across a road hexside); a unit whose NVR is 0 uses no Double Time and no road bonus. A stairwell move into a building Location costs one more MF too. A vehicle pays one more MP per hexside crossed, as if towing; an AFV whose NVR is 0 (a BU AFV at a Base NVR of 1) spends no MP while BU but to Stop (referee, pass 16). Straying, Jitter Fire, Cloaking, and Lax, Normal, and Stealthy units are not built. |
| R16.6 | How do rout, surrender, and DM change at night? | E1.54: a broken unit always Low Crawls in the RPh (never routs normally), is never eliminated for Failure to Rout, may Low Crawl out of an enemy-occupied Location, and still never toward a Known enemy unit; no unit surrenders at night but in CC or to Mopping Up (Mopping Up is not built). A DM unit keeps DM until a Rally or Self-Rally Original DR at most its printed morale. |
| R16.7 | Which other night rules are built? | E1.56, E1.76, E1.77: +1 to every SW Recovery dr; each side's SAN is two higher, to at most 7; the ATTACKER's CC Ambush needs a dr only two lower than the DEFENDER's (the DEFENDER's still three), unless the Location is Illuminated (table player, pass 16). |
| R16.8 | How are Starshells fired, and what do they Illuminate? | E1.91 to E1.923, E1.94: `asl.game.fire-starshell`, in the PFPh by the phasing side or in the DFPh or as Defensive First Fire by the other, one attempt per hex, by a Good Order, unpinned, not TI leader, CE AFV, or MMC not in a pillbox; a leader needs a Usage dr of 4 or less, a CE AFV or MMC 2 or less. The first Starshell of the game needs an enemy unit in the firer's LOS, or a Gunflash from an attack on an enemy unit. The firer names a placement method: in its own hex, then a Random Direction dr of one hex; at a Gunflash or Known enemy unit in its LOS at most six hexes out (a hex along that LOS, and a target seven or eight hexes away, are not built), then a Random Direction DR with the Extent of Error halved (FRU); or in a hex exactly three hexes away, then a full Random Direction DR. The Starshell Illuminates every Location within three hexes of its final hex until the end of the CCPh (a Random Direction that leaves the map stops at its last hex: a Starshell off the map is not built); firing it is not firing (no Gunflash, no fire counter, no loss of "?"). After the Player Turn of the first Starshell, a firer other than a leader fires one only before any fire or movement of the PFPh or the enemy MPh (referee, pass 16). An enemy unit counts as seen, for the first Starshell and method 2, only within the firer's NVR or Illuminated (E1.33). A Blaze (a burning wreck) Illuminates every hex within two hexes; shadows (E1.941), IR, trip flares, and the motorized vehicle trigger of E1.91 are not built. |
| R16.9 | How does a game declare its weather? | E3, E3.2 to E3.74: weather is Clear unless an SSR names it (`weather:<kind>`): `overcast`, `gusty`, `mist`, `rain` (Overcast and raining at the start), `heavy-rain`, `mud`, `falling-snow`, `ground-snow`, `deep-snow`, and `extreme-winter`, which combine as the Weather and Snow Charts combine them (the DYO Weather Chart roll is pass 17's). Fog, Drifts, Winter Camouflage, and the Weather Charts of Chapters F, G, and H are refused. |
| R16.10 | When is the Wind Change DR made, and what does it change? | B25.65, E1.12, E3.4, E3.51, E3.71: when night, Overcast, rain, Falling Snow, or Gusty weather is in effect, the game makes the Wind Change DR at the start of each RPh after the opening Player Turn and records it. With Overcast, a DR of 10 or more starts rain (or makes it heavy if already raining), 3 or less ends it; with Falling Snow, the same starts, intensifies, and stops the snowfall; with Gusty weather a DR of 10 or more is a Gust. Wind Force and Direction, and a DR of 2's wind change, are not built (no rule of this game reads them yet). The game starts in its opening RPh, so that RPh's Wind Change DR (B25.65, E3.51) is not made (a deviation); the NVR change starts after the opening Player Turn as E1.12 says. |
| R16.11 | Which weather Hindrances apply to fire? | E3.1, E3.32, E3.51, E3.52, E3.711, E3.8: Mist (and rain or Falling Snow, which cause Mist) adds a +1 LV Hindrance DRM per six hexes (or fraction) beyond six, to fire and LOS (not Residual FP or Fire Lanes); heavy rain or heavy snowfall adds +1 at six hexes or less and +1 more beyond. The Low Visibility DRM has no cap; with the other Hindrances it blocks the LOS at 6 (A6.2, E3.1; referee, pass 16). LV DRM is cumulative with every other Hindrance and never negates FFMO. In the firer's own hex the weather is Clear (E3.8); the Clear weather between Locations of one building is not built. An ordnance TH DR takes the Low Visibility DRM as a Case R Hindrance of its own (C6.9), which cancels neither FFMO nor the Open Ground cases (referee, pass 16). |
| R16.12 | What do Mud and rain change? | E3.54, E3.6, E3.62, E3.64, E3.65: in rain (and after it) every ground unit pays one more MF or MP per level changed, but on a stairwell or paved road. In Mud, a ground unit pays half an MF (or one MP) more per Open Ground hexside, the road bonus is lost on unpaved roads (a vehicle using one pays the Open Ground COT, whatever the hex holds; referee, pass 16), and HE attacks (not HE Equivalency, mines, Direct Fire at a vehicle or pillbox) in Open Ground take +1 TEM, their Residual FP one IFT column lower; a Bog DR a vehicle makes takes +1 for Mud (D8.21); the Bog DR of D8.23 for a vehicle's whole MPh in Mud is not built. "After rain" is any rain the game has had: the SSR's, or the Wind Change DR's, stopped or not (referee, pass 16). In rain and in Mud, SMOKE grenades are placed only inside a building (E3.53, E3.734). |
| R16.13 | What do Ground and Deep Snow change? | E1.15, E3.72 to E3.7332: Ground Snow: Infantry pay one more MF per level changed (but on a stairwell or plowed road) and lose the road bonus but on plowed roads (SSR `plowed-roads`); a vehicle's road entry costs at least one MP (a road keeps its entry), and non-tracked vehicles pay one more MP per hexside. Marsh and brush as Open Ground (E3.722, E3.73) are not built (referee, pass 16). Deep Snow adds, for Infantry, half an MF more per hexside but into woods, buildings, rubble, or across a plowed road hexside; for vehicles one MP more per hexside if tracked, two if not (one on plowed roads); HE in Open Ground +1 TEM as in Mud; a Bog DR a vehicle makes takes +1 for snow and +1 more for Deep Snow, and for Mud, not in a building (D8.21 notes 2 and 3). No SMOKE grenades in Deep Snow but inside a building (E3.734). Winter Camouflage, frozen streams and Ice, minefields in Deep Snow, and the MPh Bog DR of D8.23 are not built. |
| R16.14 | What does Extreme Winter change? | E3.74 to E3.743: Extreme Winter comes with a Snow Chart condition, which the SSRs must name. B# and X# of every weapon but a DC (MG, ATR, FT, a vehicle's MG, and a Gun) are one lower for Russians before April 1941 and two lower for Axis but Finns (the Japanese included) before April 1942 (the game needs the scenario's month and year); such a unit making an Original Rally DR of 11 outside a building suffers Fate as on 12 (E3.742, A10.64). The ELR advice of E3.741 and the Axis vehicles' immobilization (E3.744) belong to the scenario cards (pass 17). |
| R17.1 | What does a scenario card do in pass 17? | A scenario card is read, validated, and shown; it is not played (user ruling, 2026-09-29). It holds the title, the place and date, the board configuration, the Turn Record Chart, each side's OB groups with ELR, SAN, setup or entry, the Friendly Board Edges, the Scenario Defender, the SSRs, the Balance, and the Victory Conditions. A new game is still set up by hand (R5.16, R16.1, R16.9); setting up a game from a card, reinforcements entering, and evaluating the Victory Conditions are backlog. |
| R17.2 | What may a card rest on? | The registered rulebook only. A legacy card (The General, 1986 to 1994) is a model for the format and for the situation, not a source of rules: every term, rule, and reference on an adapted card is checked against the registered PDF and cited to it (the pass 17 PDF comparison), and the legacy text is paraphrased, never copied. An entry the rulebook cannot support is rewritten in the registered terms, manufactured under R0.3 and labeled, or kept as text marked not enforced, with a backlog row. The Chapter H DYO procedures (H1.28 ELR Chart, H1.29 SAN, H1.8 leaders, A16.1's DYO Integrity Base) are the check on a card's numbers. |
| R17.3 | How does a card give its board configuration? | A2.1: the boards, each with its place in the configuration (rows from north, columns from west) and whether it is turned 180 degrees, and North. The Guards Counterattack uses board 1 alone; Gambit uses board 4 with board 2 below it, turned 180 degrees (its exit hexes 2I1, 2Q1, and 2Y1 lie on the south edge). A card may also name a playable area (hexrows A to P); it is shown and not enforced (backlog). |
| R17.4 | What does the Turn Record Chart give? | A3.9: the number of Game Turns, the side that sets up first, the side that moves first, and whether the last Game Turn has only one Player Turn (a half turn; a half box on another turn is backlog, referee, pass 17). The bracketed number beside each side is its Battlefield Integrity total (A16, p. 84, and A16.1): the BPV of its starting MMC. Validation recomputes it from the catalog's BPV and rejects a card whose bracket differs, whose starting MMC lacks a BPV, or that prints one when either side starts with fewer than ten squad-equivalents (A16). Battlefield Integrity itself is optional and not built (backlog); the page says so. |
| R17.5 | What do ELR and SAN come from? | A19.1: every OB group has an ELR of 0 to 5; A14.1 and H1.29: every side has a SAN of 0 to 7 (a DYO side has 2 unless it buys more). The card's printed values stand; the H1.28 ELR Chart is the DYO check (Guards Counterattack, 10/42: German 4, Russian 3, as printed; Gambit, 5/41: German 3, British 3, where the card gives the German Fallschirmjager 4). Where a side's groups have different ELRs, the game's single ELR per side cannot hold them; the card shows each (backlog). |
| R17.6 | Which side is the Scenario Defender? | Index, Scenario Attacker/Defender: only a side that sets up wholly or partly on board and need not capture terrain beyond its setup boards, facing a side that enters wholly from offboard. The two exist together or not at all. A card names the Scenario Defender or none, and validation rejects a named Defender whose opponent sets up on board. Neither card has one: in The Guards Counterattack both sides set up on board, and in Gambit the Germans set up on board and the British partly. |
| R17.7 | Where are the Friendly Board Edges? | A20.53: unless an SSR names them, the edge a side's forces entered from, or, for a side set up on board with no reinforcements, any edge it set up in front of with no enemy between; a side may have more than one. The card gives one edge per side, as the game uses one (R5.16), with its basis (SSR, entry, setup, or manufactured under R0.3) and says it is one of the side's edges. The Guards Counterattack: German south, Russian north (in every hex column of board 1 the hexes of the German setup buildings lie south of those of the Russian ones; its legacy diagram prints a bare N, read as the map's top). Gambit: British north (entry on Turn 1), German south (the Germans set up in hexrows 8 to 10 of board 4 with nothing between them and the south edge; the east and west edges may qualify too). |
| R17.8 | How does a card give OB, setup, and entry? | Each OB group names its nationality, formation, ELR, and whether it sets up or enters: a setup area (a building, listed by all its hexes, the anchor hex first named; a board with hex-number rows; or a board's hexrows) or an entry (a Game Turn and an edge, with any limit the SSRs set). Each counter line names a catalog definition and a count. A counter the catalog lacks is added from a registered source or manufactured under R0.3 on sheet MFG and labeled as manufactured. Validation checks the hex names, that a building area includes its anchor hex, and that areas lie on the card's boards and entries within its turns; the tests check the registered cards' hexes against the boards' Hex Fact data (each building area is exactly its building, and the exit hexes exist). Checking terrain when a card is read is card-driven setup's (backlog). |
| R17.9 | Which counters do the two cards add, and which are manufactured? | Catalog 1.11.0 adds six. From registered sources: the German circled-E 5-4-8 squad (Assault and Spraying Fire, BPV 13) and its 2-3-8 HS (BPV 5) from the National Capabilities Chart (p. 695, sheet NCC), and the British OML 2-in. mortar (51mm, ROF 2, range 2 to 11, B# 12, 4 PP, s7) from the British Ordnance Listing (p. 426, sheet OLG). Manufactured under R0.3 on sheet MFG: the German HMG (7 FP, range 16, ROF 3 as the legacy card shows the counter; B# 12 and 5 PP chosen), the British LMG (2 FP, range 7, ROF 1 as the legacy card shows it; B# 11 and 1 PP as the other LMG), and the British Boys ATR (as the Russian ATR, 14mm). The legacy Russian 9-0 in The Guards Counterattack is the 9-0 Commissar (its art is the Commissar's; table player, pass 17): the registered leader table (Chapter H, p. 331) has a plain 9-0 only for the Finns, and A25.22 (p. 94) lets the Russians replace an 8-0 with a 9-0 Commissar in a scenario set in or before 10/42 with no SSR. The card uses the catalog's `defender-commissar-9-0`. |
| R17.10 | How are SSRs given? | Each SSR is its text and, where the game reads it, its token (`night:<n>`, `night-clouds:`, `night-moon:`, `weather:<kind>`; R16.1, R16.9), parsed by the game's own SSR parser. An SSR without a token is `game-default` when the game plays it with no token, or `not-enforced`, with a note. The legacy "EC are Moderate, with no wind at start" stays EC and wind (B25.5, B25.63), which the game does not model, with the weather Clear by default (R16.9): not enforced (referee and table player, pass 17). |
| R17.11 | How are Victory Conditions and Balance given? | A26: the Victory Conditions are text in the registered terms (Control, A26.1 to A26.14; Exit VP, A26.23 with the A26.211 values; Avoidance, A26.3), with their kind for display. The Guards Counterattack: the Russians win by Controlling at game end two more of the German setup stone buildings (F5, K5, I7, M7, M9) than the Germans Control of the Russian setup stone buildings (N4, J2, M2, F3; N2 is wooden), or by ending with at least three times as many unbroken squad-equivalents as the Germans; otherwise the Germans win (A26.3). Control is a looser test than the legacy "completely occupy", and the card says so. Gambit: the British win at once when they have exited at least 20 Exit VP off the south edge on or adjacent to 2I1, 2Q1, or 2Y1 (a squad 2 VP, a HS 1, a leader 1 plus 1 per negative leadership modifier; broken Personnel give none, A26.23). Balance (A26.4) is one provision per side, shown as text; the Guards sewers cite B8.1 (sewers need an SSR) and B8.4. National capabilities the card's troops have (Gambit's New Zealanders are ANZAC, Stealthy unless Green, A25.44) go in its adaptation notes. Neither is evaluated (backlog). |
| R17.12 | How does a card give a sequential setup, "?", and a first move decided by a die roll? (pass 17b) | A12.11 and A12.12 (p. 76), A3.9: each OB group may carry its place in the setup order, 1 for the groups that set up first, the side that sets up first owning them, with no gaps; groups that set up at the same time share a number. A group may carry a count of "?" it may set up with (A12.11, A12.12), which may cover real units or be Dummies. The side that moves first may be left open when a note says how it is decided (The Tractor Works: a die roll before play). The Studio shows each group's place (first, second, ..., last), its "?", and the note. |
| R17.13 | How is The Tractor Works adapted? (pass 17b) | The legacy ASL Scenario B (The General, Vol. 22 No. 6, p. 50), on board 1, hexrows O to GG playable, 8 turns, SAN 6 each. The German engineers are the National Capabilities Chart's plain-E 8-3-8 (Smoke exponent 3, Assault and Spraying Fire, underscored morale, BPV 16; p. 695, sheet NCC), as the legacy counter prints the Smoke exponent 3; the AE row (Smoke exponent 5, BPV 17) is not used. With it the catalog's BPV gives the legacy totals exactly: Russian [266] (12 4-4-7 and 26 5-2-7 at 7) and German [226] (6 8-3-8 at 16, 13 4-6-7 at 10). The Russian HMG (6 FP, range 12, and ROF 3 as the legacy counter shows; B# 12, as it prints none; 5 PP) is manufactured under R0.3 on sheet MFG. Each setup area is its whole building on board 1; X3, the Tractor Works, is nine hexes. The Friendly Board Edges follow from the setup under A20.53, as for The Guards Counterattack: in every hex column of board 1 the Russian setup buildings lie north of the German ones, so Russian north and German south (referee, pass 17b; a west or east edge would have enemy between). The Victory Conditions: the Russians Control all of X3 at the start (A26.11) and keep a hex until the Germans gain it; Control (A26.13) of at least six hexes of X3 at game end, a hex in Close Combat Controlled by neither; failing that, a side that alone has an unbroken unit in X3; otherwise a draw, which the legacy card allows and the card keeps. SSRs 4 (Fanaticism in X3, A10.8) and 5 (X3 a Factory, B23.74) are shown, not enforced; SSR 2 never applies in X3, which has no upper levels. The legacy label "295th Infantry Division" on a Russian group is kept, with a note that it is probably a misprint. The card prints no aftermath: the legacy card has none. |
| R18.1 | How does a game start from a scenario card? (Scenario Card Games Plan, pass 18) | Every game is a card (user ruling, 2026-09-29). The Play page's card picker fills and locks the start fields, and the setup's `start` names the card; the planner then takes the start from the card, not the request: the boards and their placement (A2.1), the sides (a side's id and nationality are the card's side), each side's SAN (A14.1) and Friendly Board Edge (A20.53), the Scenario Defender, the month and year, and the SSR tokens (R17.10). Only the label (the card's title unless one is given) and, when the card leaves the first move to a die roll (R17.12), the side the players name come from the request: the page presets no winner and proposes no setup until one is named (table player, pass 18); the roll itself is pass 20. A card that is not embedded, not valid against the catalog, or changed since the page read it (the page keeps the hash from when the card was chosen) is refused. Units are still placed by hand; placing the OB is pass 19. |
| R18.2 | What does a game record of its card? | `game-started` records the card's id, the SHA-256 of its text with LF line endings, and its title, as it records the catalog, so a replay knows its scenario. During play the Play page shows the card: its turns, place and date, Victory Conditions, and SSRs, with a link to the whole card, and says so when the card has changed since the game started. |
| R18.3 | How does a unit get its ELR? | A19.1 (p. 86): each scenario OB lists an ELR "for that group of units". A game from a card gives each side its OB groups (ids `<side>-<n>` in the card's order, with the card's names and ELRs); a unit may name its group at setup; a unit made from others (Replacement, Deployment, Casualty Reduction, Battle Hardening) keeps their group, a Recombination of two groups' HS takes the group with the lower ELR, and a unit created in play (a hero, a created leader, a crew bailing out) joins its creator's group or that of a unit of its side in its Location (referee, pass 18). A unit's ELR is its group's, otherwise its side's. A side's groups have distinct ids and ELRs of 0 to 5. A side's ELR is the one all its groups share, and none when they differ; then each of its units must name its group, but for Dummies, Snipers, and the units ELR never Replaces (heroes, crews, and Commissars, A19.11), which fire needs no ELR for either. Fire and ordnance give the Fire package each target's and each FPF firer's own ELR (a new optional fact; the package's cases do not change), and a side's ELR only for units with no group; only the FPF firers and friendly targets need the firing side's. A Massacre (A20.4) raises the side's ELR and every group's. |
| R19.1 | What does each side set up in a game from a card? (Scenario Card Games Plan, pass 19) | Its OB, and only its OB (A2.9): every counter it places names its OB group (a SW belongs to its holder's), and fills a counter line of that group, the line's area when the card names one (The Guards Counterattack's squads by building), otherwise any of the group's areas. A counter no line is left for is refused. The Sniper is not part of the OB (A14.1). A card changed since the game started is refused, since its OB can no longer be checked. |
| R19.2 | In what order do the groups set up, and when does play start? | The card's order (R17.12): the groups of order 1 first, then 2, and so on; without an order, the side that sets up first (A3.9), then the other. A setup proposal may place only counters of groups whose earlier groups have all finished. A group that sets up on board has finished when its OB is placed, or, where an SSR fixes how many counters set up in an area, when that area has them; a group that only enters is not part of the setup. Until every such group has finished, no action but setup is accepted, so nothing starts play (referee, pass 19); a card changed or gone since the game started stops play from starting too. The Play page shows each group's order, status, what it still has to set up, its "?", and where. Setup is hot-seat on the honor system: both sides see the map (user ruling, 2026-09-29; hidden setup is backlog). |
| R19.3 | Where may a counter set up? | Within one of its group's setup areas (a building's hexes, or a board's hex numbers; R17.8); never off the map (entering is pass 20); never where Infantry could not enter in play, which the game reads as terrain its movement rules give no entry cost for, marsh apart (B16.4; A2.9); and never overstacked: three squad-equivalents and four SMC per Location per side, five SMC counting as a HS once there are more than four (A5.1: "never during initial or offboard setup"; A5.5). Equipment on its own belongs to no group and is refused. |
| R19.4 | How are an SSR's setup limits given? | A setup area may carry the number of counters that set up in it, the fewest of them that are MMC, and whether they may set up under "?" (Gambit's SSR 2: five counters, at least two MMC, none a Concealment counter, in hexes 5 to 7 of board 4; the rest of the group enters). More counters than the number are refused; the group finishes when the area holds exactly that many with its MMC. |
| R19.5 | How does "?" work at setup? | A12.11, A12.12 (p. 76): a group may set up only its OB-designated "?", each a Dummy or the "?" over a stack of its real units, and only in Concealment Terrain (brush, woods, orchard, marsh, buildings, rubble, and grain in June to September), and not in an area whose SSR forbids it. A Dummy keeps its group, and one naming a group not of its side is refused. A "?" over a Location holding concealed units of two groups is charged to each. HIP needs an SSR (A12.3), which no card gives yet. The non-OB "?" that A12.12 lets each side place after both have set up, out of the enemy's LOS or 17 hexes away, is not built (backlog). |
| R19.6 | May squads set up Deployed? | A2.9: up to 10% (FRU) of the squads of a side that set up on board, whole or Deployed, may be Deployed before setup (two of The Guards Counterattack's thirteen German squads; referee, pass 19); each Deployed squad sets up both its HS in one proposal, and they fill that squad's counter line. The separate 10% of the squads entering in a turn is pass 20's. The squads whose HS the Fire package did not know (the pass 17 counters) could not Deploy until pass 19b (R19.7). |
| R19.7 | Which HS, Replacement, Battle Hardening, and CC BPV do the German squads the cards added take? (pass 19b) | The National Capabilities Chart (p. 695, rows rendered to read the underlines): the circled-E 5-4-8 has its FP and range underlined and a plain Morale Factor, and its HS is the 2-3-8; the plain-E 8-3-8 has all three underlined, so an underscored Morale Factor (ELR 5, R15.9), and its HS is the 3-3-8, underscored too. Deployment and Casualty Reduction give these HS, and two of them Recombine into their squad (A1.31, A1.32, A7.302). Replacement (A19.13): the 5-4-8 by the 2nd Line 4-4-7 and the 2-3-8 by the 2-3-7: a 1st Line 4-6-7 or 2-4-7 would raise the range, the chart's squared 2nd Line 5-3-7 is Volksgrenadier, which no Replacement makes of another MMC (A25.13; referee, pass 19b), and a Conscript 4-3-6 or 2-3-6 drops further than the next Class. The 8-3-8 is Replaced by its two broken 3-3-8 and a 3-3-8 is Disrupted (A19.13 EXC).
