A Casualty MC failed beyond an 8-3-8's ELR Reduces it to a broken HS of lesser quality (A19.13 EXC): the 2-3-7, the next Class down, kept in a table of its own so that nothing else Replaces a 3-3-8. Battle Hardening (A15.3): all four are elite MMC, so they become Fanatic. Their CC BPV (A18.2, R14.12) is the chart's: 13, 5, 16, and 6. Nothing is manufactured. |
| R20.1 | When does a game from a card end? (Scenario Card Games Plan, pass 20) | A3.9 (p. 47): when the Turn counter reaches END. Advancing the phase at the end of the last Player Turn of the card's last Game Turn records `game-ended` with the turn instead of a new phase; with a half turn (A3.9: "only the first side to move has a Player Turn"), the game ends after the first side's Player Turn of that Game Turn. After it no action is accepted. The Victory Conditions are evaluated in pass 21. A game with no card never ends. |
| R20.2 | How is the first move decided when a card leaves it to a die roll? | R17.12, A3.9: the card's note says only "a die roll". The first setup proposal of such a game rolls it, before either side sets up (the card says only "before play"), so both sides set up knowing who moves first (referee, pass 20): a dr for each side in the card's order, rerolled while they tie; the higher dr moves first (manufactured under R0.3, as no registered source says how). The roll is recorded (`dice-rolled`, purpose `first-move`), and the start no longer names a winner: a start that names one is refused. This replaces the player-named winner of R18.1. |
| R20.3 | How is the Balance taken? | A26.4 (p. 100): "whenever both players wish to play the same side ... they decide the matter with a dr and the loser of the dr gets to claim the Balance provision ... for his side". The start may say which side each of the two players wishes to play: when both wish the same side, each rolls a dr (the first-named player first), rerolled while they tie; the higher plays the side, and the other plays the other side with its Balance. When they wish different sides, each plays the side wished, with no Balance unless they agree to give one side its Balance without a dr, which they may also do without naming players (referee, pass 20). `game-started` records the Balance side and the players with their sides in its `scenario`; the Balance shows on the card panel. |
| R20.4 | Which Balance provisions does the game apply? | Those a card gives as counters: a side's `balanceUnits` join its OB when it has the Balance, and like the rest of the OB they must be set up before play starts (table player, pass 20). The Guards Counterattack and The Tractor Works: one German Hero (the catalog's `attacker-hero`) in any German group, set up with that group; Gambit: one German LMG (`attacker-lmg`) in the German group. The Russian Sewer Movement of the two Stalingrad cards (B8.1, B8.4) and Gambit's two British foxholes (B27) are not built: the Balance is shown and recorded, and the players apply it by hand (backlog). |
| R20.5 | How do reinforcements wait and enter? | A2.5, A2.51, A2.52 (pp. 46 to 47): an OB line that enters (its line names an entry area, or its group's lines beyond an area whose SSR fixes its counters, as Gambit's British) is set up off board during the setup, by its side at any point of it and outside the setup order, filling its line; play starts only when every entering counter waits off board. A2.51 sets them up at the start of their entry turn's RPh; the game takes them at setup, since offboard units act only to Deploy with a leader (A2.52, backlog). They are never "?" or hidden off board. Up to 10% (FRU) of the squads entering in a given Game Turn may be Deployed (A2.9). In its side's MPh of its entry Game Turn (or a later one after a blocked entry), an off-board stack enters a hex of its entry edge, within the playable area: entering it is the stack's first MF expenditure, at that hex's cost (A2.51: it may have set up in the adjacent offboard hex), and the DEFENDER may fire as at any step; it then moves on normally. A stack enters together or apart as its units choose, but only units of one entry edge together. The MPh does not end while a unit whose entry turn has come waits off board and a hex of its edge within the playable area may be entered (A2.5: otherwise entry is a Game Turn later; the four-hex radius is backlog). A2.5 lets a unit capable of movement in the APh delay its entry until then; the game does not build entry by advance, so it forbids that delay (referee, pass 20; backlog). A Balance counter waits off board only in a group that enters, and vehicles do not enter yet. Entry by advance in the APh, entry into a hex holding concealed enemy units or Residual FP, and Bypass or Minimum Move at entry are not built. |
| R20.6 | What is the playable area? | A2.1: a card may give its playable area a structured form, a board and its hexrows from and to (The Guards Counterattack: hexrows A to P of board 1; The Tractor Works: O to GG). When the card enforces it, setup, movement (Infantry and vehicles), entry, rout, advance, and a Melee withdrawal refuse a Location outside it, and the rout, charge, and withdrawal searches stay within it; fire, LOS, and Hindrances are unaffected. A unit leaves the map (A2.6) only by a map edge within the playable area, never across the area's own edge. Hexrows limit their own board; a card's other boards play whole (referee, pass 20). A card with no structured area plays the whole map. |
| R20.7 | May a card's OB set up broken? (pass 20) | No: a card's counters set up in Good Order (A2.9 and the cards give no broken setup); a counter set up broken is refused (table player, pass 20). A card that sets up broken or DM units needs a token of its own (backlog). |
| R21.1 | How is Control kept? (Scenario Card Games Plan, pass 21) | A26.1, A26.11, A26.13, A26.14, A26.15 (pp. 98 to 99): the game tracks the Control of what the Victory Conditions name: each building (a building setup area of the card, by its id) and, for a hex count, each hex of it. At scenario start a side Controls what lies wholly in its setup areas and in no enemy one, and a hex no area holds on a board where only one side sets up (A26.11). Then, event by event, a side gains a hex by an armed Good Order Infantry MMC at its ground level, and a building by one at any level of any of its hexes, with no armed enemy ground unit there (A.7: not broken, berserk, captured, or in Melee; any Personnel unit is armed but an Unarmed one); never in Bypass; a Dummy neither gains nor prevents it, a concealed real unit does both, and a prisoner neither. Control stays until the enemy gains it. Location (level) Control, vehicles' temporary Control (A26.12), Mopping Up, and Fire forfeiture (A26.16) are not built (backlog). |
| R21.2 | What are units worth? | A26.211, A26.212: a squad or crew 2 VP, a HS 1, a leader 1 plus 1 for each negative leadership modifier (a Commissar 1); a Hero, a SW, and a Dummy none; Gun and vehicle VP (A26.212) are backlog, no card fielding either (referee, pass 21). A unit's VP change with it (A26.21): a squad Reduced to a HS (A7.302: Reduced, not eliminated) gives no CVP, and its HS gives 1 when eliminated (referee, pass 21, ruled on A26.21). CVP (A26.22, A26.221, A26.222): the VP of enemy units eliminated, or that left the map other than by their own exit condition, and of enemy units held prisoner, double once the game has ended. Exit VP (A26.23): the VP of a side's units that left by its exit condition's edge from a hex on or adjacent to one it names, none for broken Personnel. |
| R21.3 | How are Victory Conditions given? | A card's Victory Conditions keep their text and kind (R17.11) and gain `outcomes`, in order, each a winner (a side or a draw) with conditions of which any may hold and whether it is checked at once, and `otherwise`, the result when none holds at game end (A26.3's Avoidance, or a draw). The conditions: `control-margin` (buildings a side Controls against those the other side Controls of another set), `control-count` (hexes of a building, a hex in Melee counting for neither when the card says so), `squad-ratio` (unbroken squad-equivalents, a squad one and a HS or crew half, against the other side's), `sole-unbroken` (the only side with an unbroken unit in a building), `exit-vp`, and `cvp`. The Guards Counterattack: the Russians by a margin of two buildings, or three times the Germans' unbroken squad-equivalents; otherwise the Germans. Gambit: the British at once with 20 Exit VP off the south edge on or adjacent to 2I1, 2Q1, or 2Y1; otherwise the Germans. The Tractor Works: six hexes of X3 for either side, then the side alone with an unbroken unit in X3; otherwise a draw. Validation checks every side, building, edge, and hex they name. |
| R21.4 | When is the result decided and recorded? | At the game's end (R20.1) the outcomes are read in order and the first that holds gives the result; none holding gives `otherwise`. After every action in play, the outcomes marked immediate are read at the VP of play (captured units not yet doubled, A26.222), and one that holds ends the game at once (Gambit's Exit VP), once nothing is left open in the action (an entry attempt, a choice, a surrender, or a CC); the check runs again after the next action (referee, pass 21). A game whose card has changed since it started, or no longer validates, is not judged. `game-ended` records the winner (none for a draw), the reason, and the facts behind it (each building's and hex's Control, each side's CVP, Exit VP, and unbroken squad-equivalents); the Play page shows them during play as what the result would be now, and the result once the game has ended; a hex a card counts for neither while it is in Melee is shown so (table player, pass 21). |
| R21.5 | How does Infantry leave the map? | A2.6 (p. 47): in its side's MPh a Good Order stack in a ground-level hex on a map edge, within the playable area (R20.6), leaves across that edge as if entering the mirror-image hex beyond, for its own hex's MF; the whole moving stack leaves together, never from Bypass, and never a crew manning a Gun or a Guard with prisoners (not built; referee, pass 21); the units, and the SW they carry, are Exited, never to return (table player, pass 21). The DEFENDER cannot fire at units off the map. Leaving in the APh, by Bypass (A2.6's extra MF), and at the road rate are not built (backlog). An exit that meets no exit condition of its side counts as elimination for the enemy's CVP (A26.221). |
| R22.1 | What does the card editor edit? (Scenario Card Games Plan, pass 22) | Every card field: the id, title, place, date, introduction, boards (a board, or boards placed by column and row and turned), North, the playable area and its hexrows, the Turn Record Chart, the Scenario Defender, each side's SAN, ELR (a minimal card's), edge and its basis, and Balance; the OB groups, SSRs, and Victory Conditions as JSON in the card format, with a counter picker that adds a catalog counter line to a group; and the source. The card is validated against the catalog as it is edited (R17.1 to R21.3), and only a valid card is saved. Picking setup areas on the map is not built: areas are typed as hexes or rows, and the setup checks read the board's Hex Facts when the game sets up (R19.3; backlog). |
| R22.2 | Where are the user's cards kept? | Under `src/ASL/boards/cards`, beside the saved maps and like them untracked live data, one file per card named by its id (lower-case letters, digits, and hyphens), never a built-in card's name. The built-in cards stay embedded and read-only: the editor copies one under a new id to change it. The Play page, the Scenarios page, and the planner read one library of both, so a game from a user card records its id and SHA-256 as any card (R18.2) and is refused when the card has changed since (R18.1, R19.1). A user card may be deleted; a game from it then shows its card as missing. A save replaces a user card only when that card is the one being edited, and writes the file whole (referee, pass 22). A game whose card has changed or gone follows none of it: its setup, OB, and Victory Conditions are not read from another text, and the refusal says whether the card changed or is gone (referee and table player, pass 22). |
| R22.3 | What is a minimal card? | A card for a game with no scenario: boards and two sides with no OB groups. A side may carry its own ELR (A19.1); the day, month, and year may be 0 (not recorded), and a side's edge may be unnamed (basis `none`); a SAN of 0 records none. Its units set up by hand as before, with no OB, order, or area checks (R19.1 to R19.6 apply only to a card with an OB), and the players judge the result (its Victory Conditions name no outcomes). Its Turn Record Chart still ends the game (R20.1). A named edge with no printed basis is manufactured (R0.3); a side ELR on a card with an OB is refused, the groups giving theirs; SSR tokens are checked against the date as the game sees it (a month or year of 0 is none), so Extreme Winter needs both (R16.14; table player, pass 22). |
| R22.4 | Does every game start from a card? | In the Studio, yes (user ruling, 2026-09-29): the Play page's new-game form is gone; a new game names a card (built-in, the user's, or a minimal card from the editor), its id and label, and the Balance, and shows the start the card gives. The setup action still accepts a start without a card, so the recorded games and the planner's tests replay as before; the page tests start from minimal cards. The user's live games are left as they are. |
| R23.1 | What does a side's view show? (Card Play and Map Studio Redesign Plan, pass 23) | A12.11, A12.3, A26.15: its own units whole; the enemy's Known units whole; each concealed enemy counter as a "?" at its Location, a Dummy always (referee, pass 23), so a stack's height shows as on the board (A12.11); an enemy's hidden units, and what they hold, not at all, nor the setup events that created what the side may not see (referee, pass 23). Every Play panel reads the view: the map, the unit table, and the unit, weapon, CC, and entry pickers; the Move panel is the moving side's and the Fire panel the firing side's, each told to the other side in a line (table player, pass 23). The adjudicator sees everything. |
| R23.2 | How do the sides share one screen? | The user's answer of 2026-09-30 (plan section 3, decision 1): a change of view in the picker shows a hand-over screen instead of the map and the panels, which are not rendered at all until the next viewer confirms being at the screen; choosing the view shown again drops it. Opening a game or the page shows the view at once. A proposal under review, a drawn LOS, and every draft of setup and of the actions are dropped when the view changes (plan section 13.4); the CC declarations stay, since each side declares its attacks openly into one proposal (A11.1, R5.20). Play is hot-seat on the honor system: nothing stops a player choosing the other view. |
| R23.3 | What does a side see of the other's setup, and where does the board link lead? | A12.12, A2.9 (pp. 77, 47; the user's answer of 2026-09-30: the rulebook, not the plan's first text): "The player setting up first in a scenario does so out of vision of his opponent": in a game from a card, while an order of groups sets up, the other side's view leaves those groups' units out entirely, and the setup table shows only their status; a group of an earlier order, already seen, stays seen (referee, pass 23). Once that order has finished, the other side sees its setup as on the board: "No enemy stack ... may be inspected prior to the start of play", so until play starts each enemy stack not under "?" shows its top counter (the first listed) and counts the rest as uninspected counters, and a Gun or other equipment beneath them is withheld; a fortification holds its units beneath it, so it shows. From the start of play Known units are seen whole. A game with no OB (a minimal card) has no order, so its setup is on the honor system. The Play page's board link opens the board viewer as the page's view, the adjudicator only when the page shows the adjudicator (plan section 15.4). |
| R23.4 | What Control and standing does a side see? | A26.15 (p. 99): "control ... by a concealed or HIP armed unit need not be declared until game end". A side reads the Victory standing with the enemy's concealed and hidden units neither gaining nor preventing Control, event by event, and with the enemy's unbroken squad-equivalents counted over its Known units only; what the result would be now rests on all of it, so only the adjudicator sees that line. Its own units count whole. Once the game has ended, Control is declared and both sides read the recorded standing. |
| R23.5 | How does a card give HIP, and what may a hidden unit do? | A12.3, A12.31, A12.32 (p. 80): an SSR token `hip:<side>:<n>` lets that side set up up to n squad-equivalents of MMC hidden (a squad one, a HS or crew half), with the SMC set up hidden in a Location where one of its hidden MMC is; Infantry only (hidden Guns and vehicles are refused), only in Concealment Terrain, never a Dummy, never a unit also under "?", never off board (R20.5); a token names a side of the card (referee, pass 23). The largest n of a side's tokens holds; a malformed token is refused where the card is checked. A hidden unit is recorded seen by its side alone and revealed by the triggers already built for concealment (A12.14, A12.15, fire). A12.32: a hidden unit "may not move ... or advance and remain hidden"; it is first placed beneath a "?" (the action `asl.game.place-hidden`, from the start of play, whenever its side chooses), after which the concealment rules apply; the move of a hidden unit is refused with that message. Hidden Guns (A12.34) and fortifications (A12.33) are backlog. |
| R23.6 | How is the non-OB "?" placed? | A12.12 (p. 77): once every group of a card that sets up on board has set up and before play starts (the setup action's `conceal` argument: a side and its Locations), each side may place one "?" on each of its stacks with no "?", no hidden unit, and no broken or berserk unit ("A unit can never gain '?' while broken or berserk"; referee, pass 23), if every unbroken enemy ground unit (not a Dummy, captured, or hidden: hidden units are not on the board) is 17 hexes or more away or has no LOS to it within 16 hexes; a LOS the board cannot decide refuses it, and a refusal names no enemy Location (table player, pass 23). Each unit of the stack gains "?" in a setup event seen by its side, which does not start play and is not counted against the OB's "?" (R19.5). A minimal card's sides may place them at any time during setup. The "?" a unit may take before entering the map (A12.12) and a HIP unit giving up its status to stop an enemy "?" (A12.32) are backlog. |
| R24.1 | What Control is kept per Location, and what does an unentered Location count for? (Card Play and Map Studio Redesign Plan, pass 24) | A26.1, A26.11, A26.14, the A26.16 EX (pp. 98 to 99); the user's answers of 2026-09-30, both the rulebook's. Each Location of every building the Victory Conditions name is tracked: each level of each of its hexes as the board gives them (ground level and upper levels; rooftops left out, A26.14, and cellars, which "have no other use in the game", B23.41; table player, pass 24). A Location starts with its hex's side (ruling R24.4); during play an armed Good Order Infantry MMC in that Location, not in Bypass, gains it when no armed enemy ground unit is in that Location (A26.11). An upper level or cellar no one enters keeps its own Control until an enemy MMC enters it or Mopping Up secures the building (R24.2); it does not follow its hex or building. A building's Control does not need every Location (A26.14): it is gained as R21.1 says, by one MMC at any level with no armed enemy ground unit in the building. No Victory Condition names a Location yet, so Location Control is shown and used by Mopping Up but decides no card. |
| R24.2 | How is Mopping Up played? | A12.153, A12.154 (p. 79), A26.11; the user's answer of 2026-09-30: with the Search casualties. The action `asl.game.mop-up` names a building of the card and the units: in its side's PFPh, armed, unpinned, Good Order Infantry MMC (not broken, berserk, captured, Unarmed, in Melee, TI, or marked Prep Fire) in a building of the card of more than one hex or level, with no unconcealed unbroken enemy unit inside (a prisoner of the side does not count, nor an enemy vehicle in Bypass, which is not inside the building; referee and table player, pass 24), each ground-level Location the side does not Control within two hexes of one of them, once per building per Player Turn, and never once the side has employed No Quarter or Massacre (A20.3, A20.4: the enemy is in `NoQuarter`). The units become TI. Hidden enemy units in the building are placed beneath "?" (seen by their side) and enemy Dummies removed. With no concealed enemy unit left, the building is secured: the side Controls the building and every Location of it (A26.11), but the ground level holding an armed enemy vehicle in Bypass, and every broken enemy unit there surrenders to the unit the side names inside the building (the first Mopping-Up unit when it names none), moving to it and leaving its SW; the guard is any armed Personnel unit of the side, even a broken SMC, never a berserk or Unarmed one (A20.5, A20.4; referee, pass 24), and one guard takes them all where A12.153 lets the ATTACKER choose several (a simplification). Otherwise, when an armed Good Order concealed enemy unit is there, the DEFENDER's Casualty dr (A12.154) is rolled at once, since it costs him nothing: -1 per Stealthy unit (a Good Order hero or heroic leader, as for the Ambush, A11.17), -1 per HS-equivalent beyond one, the best concealed leader's leadership unless he is the only concealed defending unit in the building (the referee's reading of A10.7 here), +1 per Lax (Inexperienced) unit, all among the concealed defending units in the building; a Final dr of 1 or less Reduces a Mopping-Up unit (A7.302), chosen by Random Selection when there are several (ties all). A refusal says why (pinned, fired in this PFPh, and so on), and the result says how many hidden units were placed beneath "?" and Dummies removed (table player, pass 24). The range check names a ground-level Location the side does not Control, which may tell it of Control a concealed enemy holds; A26.15 lets Mopping Up verify Control, so this stands (referee, pass 24). Only buildings the card names are Mopped Up; Rowhouses hex by hex, rubbled or Blazing Locations, Fortified Locations, Casualty drs from minefields, Residual FP, FFE, or Booby Traps, and nationality Stealth are backlog. |
| R24.3 | What are Guns and vehicles worth? | A26.212, A26.213, A26.22, A26.222 (p. 100). A non-vehicular Gun two VP, even dismantled. A vehicle one, plus one for a MA not malfunctioned (a Gun MA, read by its type, or a MG the catalog names as the MA, such as a SPW 251/1's AAMG, which also loses it when disabled; referee, pass 24), plus one per multiple of five of its single strongest AF, rounded up (hull or turret, front or side; a 0 AF one; none unarmored), plus its inherent crew's two (A26.211) unless the crew has left it (Abandoned, or a crew counter of its own); a vehicle with no MA and no MG has only an Inherent Driver (D5.1), so no crew VP (the A26.212 EX's truck, one VP; referee, pass 24). CVP: the side's opponent gets a Gun's VP when it is eliminated, and a vehicle's when it is wrecked or eliminated, at the value it then has (A26.21); a Gun is captured while the side that last held it (possessed or manned) is the enemy's, even unheld, and a vehicle while it is captured (A11.52), each worth its normal VP during play and double at the end (A26.222). Exit VP for Guns and vehicles wait for their exits (plan passes 25 and 26). No card fields a Gun or a vehicle yet (plan pass 26), so the tests use constructed games. |
| R24.4 | Where does Control start in the edge cases? | A26.11 (p. 98). Each hex is resolved on its own: the side whose setup areas alone hold it, or, when no area holds it, the only side setting up on its board; a hex in both sides' areas starts with neither. A building, hex, or Location starts with a side when every hex of it resolves to that side. A building the Victory Conditions name is a building setup area of the card, so it never lies partly outside every area; and a card validates only when each area names a board of the card or the card has one board, so no area is without a board (referee, pass 21: both recorded, neither reachable from a valid card). |
| R24.5 | How do vehicles gain and prevent Control? | A26.11, A26.12 (p. 98). An armed vehicle (the Index's Armed: one with an inherent crew, so a MA or a MG, not Abandoned; referee, pass 24) not in Bypass temporarily holds the Location it occupies while no armed enemy unit is there, and its hex when that hex has only one Location; it never gains a building. The hold is read from the present state only: when the vehicle leaves, the Location's Control is what it was. An armed vehicle prevents the enemy from gaining the Location, hex, and building it occupies, but one in Bypass does not prevent the building's. A captured vehicle neither holds nor prevents Control, though A21.22 gives it a Temporary Crew (a deviation: captured vehicles are not used in play; backlog). The vehicle's PRC are not built. |
| R24.6 | How is the Control fold kept? | Referee, pass 21 (backlog section 31). Each game's fold is cached per view: the Control and the side that last held each Gun after the stored events last read, checked by the last event's id and time, so a later reading folds only the states added since. A plan's events (the immediate Victory check) are read but never cached. |
| R24.7 | What does the Victory standing show? | Plan task 24.5 (K08 `VictoryStandingTable`): the buildings and hexes as before; a row for each Location whose Control differs from its building's (such as "bd01:H5 ground level of building F5"), with a vehicle's temporary hold named, and several of one building and side as one row whose Locations open in a disclosure (table player, pass 24); and each side's CVP with the part from Guns and vehicles. The game's recorded facts list the same Locations. A side reads it as it knows it (ruling R23.4). The Play page's Mopping Up panel is the phasing side's in its PFPh (and the adjudicator's), listing only the buildings that may be Mopped Up and only units the view sees, none checked, since each becomes TI. |
| R25.1 | When and how may units waiting off board enter by advance? (Card Play and Map Studio Redesign Plan, pass 25) | A2.5, A4.7, A4.72, A4.14 (pp. 46, 49 to 52); the user's answer of 2026-10-01: the APh holds them. "All forces scheduled to arrive on a certain turn must enter the mapboard on that turn--although if capable of movement in the APh, entry may be delayed until then." A stack whose entry turn has come (or a later one, R25.2) and that did not enter in its MPh enters in its side's APh by advance (`asl.game.advance` from off board): into a hex of its entry edge open to it this Game Turn, within the playable area, at that hex's entry cost from off board (R25.3), becoming CX when the hex is Difficult Terrain (A4.72); units of one edge together, as one advance. Like any advance it may enter a hex holding enemy units, concealed, hidden, or Known (A4.14: "but may do so during the APh"), and CC follows. The MPh may therefore end while due units wait off board (replacing R20.5's MPh hold); the APh may not end while one waits that can still advance into a hex open to it: one no Known enemy unit or enemy vehicle holds (R25.2), not marsh (B16.4), across a hexside whose cost is decided, and not Difficult Terrain for a CX unit; a pinned unit, or one with no MF after portage, is "not capable of movement in the APh", so it does not hold the phase and enters a Game Turn later (table player and referee, pass 25). The refusal names every unit still waiting and hexes open to them. The move and advance panels name the units waiting, the hexes a named entry may use, and the stacks forced back. |
| R25.2 | What blocks an entry, and how is a blocked entry delayed? | A2.5 (p. 46); the user's answer of 2026-10-01: named entry hexes. An entry area of a card may name its entry hexes (`hexes`, on its board); a card's entry along a whole edge stays as R20.5 built it. In the friendly MPh a hex is obstructed for entry when a Known enemy unit or an enemy vehicle holds it (A4.14); concealed and hidden units do not obstruct it (R25.3). When every named hex is obstructed, the entry is a Game Turn later in a hex of the edge within four hexes of a named hex, and four more for each further Game Turn ("the radius of permissible entry is extended another four hexes"), never past a river or canal hex on the edge ("can never be used to appear on the other side of a river/canal"; a hex whose terrain is Water, River, or Canal: VASL names rivers and ponds both Water, so a pond on the edge also stops the radius, a deviation in the backlog). An entry naming no hex of its edge within the playable area is refused with a message saying so (referee, pass 25). The radius counts the Game Turns since the entry turn, so a stack forced back off board (R25.3) also enters within it the next turn, a reading recorded in the backlog. For Infantry, rubble is ordinary terrain entered at its cost, and the game has no terrain Blaze; rubble or Blaze cutting a hex off from the board concerns vehicles, which enter in plan pass 26 (backlog). Delayed entry is never voluntary: a hex that can be entered must be. |
| R25.3 | How does an entering stack meet what is in its first hex? | A2.51, A2.52, A12.15, A8.22, A9.22, A4.3, A4.134, A2.6 EX (pp. 46 to 47, 49, 60, 78); the user's answer of 2026-10-01: forced back off board. Entering is a step like any other, from the mirror-image hex beyond the edge at the hex's own level: the hex's cost, at the road rate across a road hexside of the edge, one MF more across a wall or hedge on it (B9.4); the leader's MF bonus, Assault Movement, Double Time, Bypass along one or two hexsides starting at a vertex of the edge hexside (A4.3), and Minimum Move (A4.134) all apply, and the stack's Bypass continues from there. Residual FP and Fire Lanes in the hex attack it first (A8.22, A9.22). A hex holding only concealed or hidden enemy units is attempted as A12.15 says: hidden units are placed beneath "?", one real unit is revealed by Random Selection, and the stack is forced back off board with its MF spent and its MPh over; nothing fires at it there (A2.51: no fire between the map and an offboard setup), and it may still enter by advance in the APh. Only Dummies there are removed and the stack enters. An entering stack keeps to the stacking limits, which offboard setup never exceeds: at most three squad-equivalents and four SMC (A5.1; referee, pass 25). SMOKE grenades and a DC Placement from off board are refused: "No action is allowed by units offboard awaiting entry" (A2.52); once on the map the stack places them as at any step. |
| R25.4 | What may units waiting off board do, and which entry do they use? | A2.52, A2.51, A2.9 (pp. 46 to 47). A squad waiting off board may attempt to Deploy in its side's RPh, as on the map (A1.31), from the RPh of its entry Game Turn (A2.51 sets it up off board then), "if stacked with a leader": a Good Order leader of its nationality waiting to enter along the same edge, his entry turn come too (off-board stacks are not recorded, so this is manufactured under R0.3); its HS keep the squad's group and entry. A counter set up off board may name one of its group's entry areas (`position.entry`), for a group with several entry areas or a Balance counter in a group that enters; a name its group does not have is refused, and a counter that names none enters by its line's entry, else its group's first (R20.5). A setup naming another side's OB group is refused with a plain message (`play.setup-group`) before the projector sees it. |
| R25.5 | How do units leave the map, and what does a Guard's exit count? | A2.6, A2.6 EX, A20.53, A26.221, A26.222, A26.23 (pp. 46 to 47, 87, 100). A stack leaves "using normal MPh/APh capabilities": in its MPh as R21.5 says, or by advance in its APh from an edge hex (`asl.game.advance` naming an edge), CX when the hex is Difficult Terrain (A4.72, with a leader's two MF and IPC as any advance; referee, pass 25), never in the RtPh. The cost is the edge hex's own terrain across the edge hexside (the cheapest edge hexside of a half hex), at the road rate across a road hexside ("exit of 2Y1 can be done at the road movement rate"). A stack in Bypass of an edge hex leaves when the far vertex of its last Bypass hexside lies on the edge, for one MF of Open Ground beyond its Bypass (the A2.6 EX's X0). A Guard leaves with its prisoners by any edge, and they and their SW leave with it; off its side's Friendly Board Edge as the card names it (A20.53) or the edge of its side's exit condition (A26.23) the Guard is not eliminated for CVP (A26.221's escort exception), otherwise it is (referee, pass 25: A20.53 does not bar other edges). Either way its prisoners stay captured, worth their VP to the captor during play and double once the game has ended (A26.222), and off the edge of the captor's exit condition, from a hex it names, they also count Exit VP for the captor, normal during play and double at the end (A26.23). Each exit says what it counts for: Exit VP, or elimination for the enemy's CVP, or an escort's exemption; the edges read with their compass direction (table player, pass 25). A crew leaving with its Gun waits for Guns on a card (plan pass 26). |
| R25.6 | Which Play components does the pass extract? | Plan task 25.6 and section 16.17: A01 `BuildingEntryAction`, A11 `MovementStatus`, A13 `InfantryMovementAction`, A14 `SmokeGrenadeAction`, A15 `MovementWindowActions`, A16 `ReactionFireAction`, A23 `AdvanceActionPanel`, C19 `OpenEntryDeclaration`, K09 `MapExitAction`, N03 `PlaceDemolitionChargeAction`, N04 `BerserkRetainedWeaponsField`, S11 `LocationField`, and S12 `UnitSelectionList`, all thirteen. Each holds no draft: the page keeps the drafts and their reset rules (plan section 15.3; the SMOKE, DC, and advance drafts now reset with the game and phase and are pruned to the choices offered), reads every list for the view (rulings R23.1 to R23.4; the CC Reaction Fire panel is now the DEFENDER's and the adjudicator's only, and the Deploy lists a side's own squads; UI review, pass 25), and proposes; the components render what they are given and raise what the player does, keeping the element ids page tests use. K09 serves both the move (`#move-exit`) and the advance (`#advance-exit`). K08 `VictoryStandingTable` shows `#play-victory-locations` only when a Location row is listed (the user, after the pass 24 demo). |
| R25.7 | Which messages and small fixes does the pass make? | Plan task 25.7. The SMOKE panel offers only squads with a Smoke Placement Exponent, and as places the movers' own Location (1 MF) and the ADJACENT ones (2 MF) (backlog section 19). Once the DEFENDER's fire has broken, pinned, or eliminated every member of the moving stack, passing also ends its move, and the end-move refusal says the window is open and that passing ends it; ending a move whose movers were eliminated or Replaced no longer fails in the projector (backlog section 25, both rows). A setup naming another side's OB group is refused by the planner (`play.setup-group`), and an offboard Deployment refusal says what the squad needs (backlog section 30). An exit refusal from a hex beside the edge names the edge hexes next to it (backlog section 31). A TI unit is no longer offered as a mover (the visual check). An exit of a fresh stack while another moves names the moving one (table player, pass 25). |
| R26.1 | How do vehicles wait off board and enter, and what of their towed Guns? (Card Play and Map Studio Redesign Plan, pass 26) | A2.52, D2.4, D2.11, A2.5, C10.1, C10.2, C10.13 (pp. 46, 198, 180); the user's answers of 2026-10-01: the MPh holds vehicles; limbering and en portee wait (backlog). A vehicle of an OB line that enters waits off board, CE or BU as its side chooses, "assumed to be in Motion" (A2.52); it is never "?" or hidden off board (R20.5). In its side's MPh of its entry Game Turn or later, its first MP expenditure is its entry (`asl.game.move-vehicle`, kind `enter`, from off board): with no Start MP, from the mirror-image hex beyond the edge into a hex of its entry open to it this Game Turn (R25.2, the named hexes and the four-hex radius included), at that hex's own cost for its movement type across the edge hexside (a road hexside's rate, a wall or hedge as for any entry), with a VCA holding the hex: one of the two hexspines 30 degrees either side of its travel (D2.11). A hex a Known enemy unit or an enemy vehicle holds is not open (A4.14); concealed enemy units there take their A12.41 choice; Residual FP attacks it as at any step. Its Passengers and towed Gun enter with it. Vehicles cannot advance, so the MPh, not the APh, does not end while a vehicle whose entry turn has come waits off board and an edge hex is open to it (A2.5: "if capable of movement in the APh"); a vehicle that can enter no hex is delayed a Game Turn. A Gun waits in tow of a vehicle whose T# does not exceed its M# (C10.1), one per vehicle; only a QSU Gun is towed, hooked up, unhooked, or pushed, since limbering (C10.2) is not built: both catalog Guns are QSU, and a Gun that is not is refused with that message. The ammunition takes four PP of the vehicle's Passenger capacity, eight at 100mm or more (C10.13). En portee (C10.5) is not built (backlog). |
| R26.2 | How do Passengers ride, board, and get off? | D6.1, D6.4, D6.5, A5.5, C10.11, C10.12 (pp. 204 to 205, 180); the user's answer of 2026-10-01: loading and unloading both. A vehicle's Passenger capacity is its printed PP (the catalog's `passenger-capacity`), less a towed Gun's ammunition (R26.1); a squad takes ten PP, a HS or crew five, up to four SMC none, plus the SW each carries (D6.1). At setup, Personnel of the vehicle's side and OB group ride it as Passengers (`position.in`), within its capacity, neither "?" nor hidden; off board they wait and enter with it. Infantry board a vehicle (`kind: load`) that is stopped, not in Motion or Bypass, and has spent no MP this MPh (D6.4): Good Order, unpinned Personnel of its side on foot in its Location that have not moved this MPh (boarding as a step of the Infantry move, as the D6.4 EX does, is in the backlog), each spending one MF and riding with its move ended; the vehicle keeps a quarter of its printed MP for each MF left to the unit that spent the most (FRD to the half MP). Passengers disembark (`kind: unload`) beneath a vehicle stopped in its own MPh, not in Bypass, that has spent no more than three-fourths of its printed MP: the vehicle spends a quarter of its MP allotment (FRU) for the stack, and each unit spends one MF plus one per quarter (FRU) the vehicle spent before, added to what it spent boarding (D6.5); it may then move on with the MF left. A vehicle that Prep Fired, is immobilized, or is Abandoned lets its Passengers off (D6.5, D6.1; referee, pass 26); those of a vehicle that Prep Fired stay in its Location that MPh, and an immobilization ends the vehicle's move at once, so its Passengers get off in its next MPh (backlog). A broken Passenger may stay aboard, free of rout requirements (D6.1). Up to four SMC ride as zero PP; a fifth is refused (backlog). A crew may board as it hooks up its Gun at no MP (C10.11), and a crew aboard disembarks to man the Gun as the vehicle unhooks it (C10.12). Passengers are reached through their vehicle: they are not units in its Location for fire, Control, CC, stacking, rout, or spotting a firing Gun (A12.34), and no page list offers them (D6.61 for the halftrack; the Passengers' fire, rout, OVR, and CC are plan pass 65, and fire at an unarmored vehicle's Passengers plan passes 60 and 65), and an action naming one is refused until it unloads. Passengers of a vehicle eliminated or wrecked are eliminated with it, since Crew Survival for Passengers (D5.6) is not built (backlog). Riders (D6.2) are not built. |
| R26.3 | How does a Gun set up, and how does its crew stack? | A5.5, C10, C11.2 (pp. 53, 180, 181); plan task 26.2. In a game from a card a Gun on the map sets up manned by a crew or HS of its OB group in its hex, or in tow (R26.1); never alone, since no card names an unmanned Gun. A crew or HS manning a Gun counts as a squad for stacking at setup (A5.5: "considered equal to a squad for stacking purposes"), fixing the half squad pass 19 counted (backlog section 29). A Gun set up manned, not in tow, and never moved is Emplaced (C11.2), as pass 8 built. |
| R26.4 | How do vehicles and Guns leave the map, and what do they count? | A2.6, C10.3, A26.211, A26.212, A26.221, A26.23 (pp. 46 to 47, 181, 100). A vehicle exits as pass 11 built, and its exit is now recorded with the edge (`vehicle-step` `edge`) for itself, its Passengers, and a Gun it tows, so each counts its VP toward its side's Exit VP conditions (the vehicle's VP as R24.3 reads them, a Gun's two), broken Personnel none; an exit that meets no exit condition counts as elimination for the enemy's CVP (A26.221). This fixes the pass 25 bug: a vehicle's exit recorded no `UnitExit`, so it scored no Exit VP and gave the enemy CVP. A crew or HS manning a QSU Gun leaves the map only pushing it (`pushGun`), alone, in its MPh, not from Bypass: a push into the mirror-image hex, across Open Ground or grain, at double the MF, with its Manhandling DR (+ the MF spent, -2 across a road hexside); a DR above the M# leaves both in place, TI; otherwise both are Exited and the Gun's exit recorded. A crew still pushing its Gun this MPh may push it on, off the map, though its last push made it TI (referee, pass 26). A Gun's exit counts for the side last holding it, so a captor's exit keeps the Gun captured (A26.222). A vehicle leaving under Recall (D5.341) counts neither Exit VP nor CVP (A26.23, A26.221; `UnitExit.Recalled`; referee, pass 26). Leaving a Gun behind to exit (abandoning it), and more than one unit pushing it off, are not built. |
| R26.5 | How do Guns hide, and when are they revealed? | A12.34, A12.2, A12.14 (pp. 78, 80); the user's answer of 2026-10-01: Concealment Terrain only. An Emplaced Gun (manned, not in tow) and its manning crew may set up hidden in Concealment Terrain with no SSR, together, and do not count against a `hip:` token; the crew hidden without its Gun, a Gun hidden without its crew, or either outside Concealment Terrain is refused. A Gun's hidden flag at setup is its own and is now checked (the loophole by which a manned Gun's hidden flag escaped ruling R23.5 is closed). HIP out of every enemy ground unit's LOS outside Concealment Terrain waits for a LOS check after every enemy step (backlog). An Emplaced hidden or concealed Gun that fires is revealed, with its crew, when the colored dr of its Original To Hit DR is 5 or more and the nearest Good Order enemy ground unit with a LOS to it is within 16 hexes, or the dr is a 6 and that unit is 17 hexes or more away; otherwise both are placed, or stay, beneath "?"; with no such unit in LOS a hidden Gun keeps HIP (A12.34). A Gun that is not Emplaced, and a vehicle, lose concealment by A12.14 as before; a hidden crew that loses its "?" so loses HIP too. The RCL, zero-range, IFE, and FB/DB exceptions are not reached by the catalog's Guns; a crew possessing a non-inherent SW, which A12.34 bars from HIP, and C8.9's "not fired" are not checked (backlog). Passengers do not spot for the reveal (ruling R26.2). |
| R26.6 | How is a Gun's Covered Arc read across boards and on a reversed board? | C3.2, C3.21 (pp. 168 to 169); plan task 26.6. Checked first against what the game did: the bearing already came from the composed map's grid across boards and on a reversed board (ruling R8.7), so a Gun's facing and its Covered Arc are read in the map's frame on every board, reversed or not; a test pins it (a Gun facing north-east on reversed board 2 covers the hex above it on the map). The comments that said "one unreversed board" are corrected. Pass 115 reuses it for pillboxes. |
| R26.7 | Which card fields vehicles and Guns? | R0.3; plan task 26.4. The built-in card Armor Test (`armor-test`) is manufactured under R0.3 and adapts no legacy card: the Russians set up in hexrows 5 to 10 of board 4 with three squads, a leader, a 45mm AT Gun and its crew, and a T-34; the Germans enter along the north edge on Turn 1 with a halftrack (its squad and leader as Passengers), a PzKpfw IIIH, and a truck towing the infantry gun with its crew aboard. The Germans win at once with 8 Exit VP off the south edge near 4G10, 4Q10, or 4Y10. Its SSRs cite the rulings, since the card's citable rule fragments do not yet list A2.52, D6, or A12.34. |
| R26.8 | Which Play components does the pass extract? | Plan task 26.5 and section 16.17: P08 `SetupPlacementEditor`, P09 `SetupPlacementList`, A17 `VehicleMovementPanel`, A18 `VehicleMovementStatus`, A19 `VehicleStepChoices`, A20 `VehicleTowingActions`, A21 `BoundingFireAction`, A22 `CrewExposureActions`, C18 `GunArcAction`, and S13 `FacingPicker`, all ten, keeping the element ids page tests use. Each holds no draft: the page keeps the drafts and their reset rules (plan section 15.3; the vehicle's Passenger choices, the hex it wished to enter next, and the crew boarding reset with the vehicle chosen and the phase) and reads every list for the view (rulings R23.1 to R23.4). P08 names an off-board counter's entry area (`#place-entry`; backlog section 39) and a Passenger's vehicle or a Gun's tower in its holder field; A19 offers an off-board vehicle's entries with their VCAs, the Passengers to load and unload, and the hex wished to enter next as buttons for the VCA's hexes (backlog section 21). After the reviews: each placement has its own key; a holder not yet placed is refused with a note; the entry area, the Passengers chosen, the entry, and the crew boarding reset with the card, game, view, vehicle, and commit; towing and unloading are offered only when the planner allows them; cargo shows only for the viewer's own side (UI and table player reviews, pass 26). |
| R27.1 | Which Axis Minor exceptions are built, and with which counters? (Card Play and Map Studio Redesign Plan, pass 27) | A15.1, A15.3 (p. 83), A18.2 (p. 694), A19.13, A25.8, A25.82, A25.84 (pp. 97 to 98), the National Capabilities Chart (p. 695); the user's answers of 2026-10-01: one shared counter set with the nation named per side, the counter values now and the other A25.8 rules to the backlog, catalog 1.13.0. Vocabulary 1.16.0 adds the side `axis-minor`; catalog 1.13.0 adds 17 counters transcribed from the chart, none manufactured: the elite 4-4-7 and 2-4-7, the boxed 1st Line 5-3-7 SMG squad (A25.84, Assault Fire) and its 2-2-7, the 1st Line 3-4-7 and 1-3-7, the Conscript 3-3-6 and 1-2-6 (a non-elite squad's broken Morale Level one less, A25.8), a leader of every grade (sheet LGR), and a hero (sheet CEX); the case matrices, packages, cards, and tests are re-pinned. An Axis Minor side names its nation (Romanian, Hungarian, Slovakian, Croatian, or Bulgarian), and no other side names one; a card side may name it too. Heat of Battle: +3, and a non-elite Axis Minor MMC surrenders on a Final DR of 10 or more (A25.82). Hungarians fighting Romanians start with No Quarter for both sides (A25.8), which already turns their Surrender of 10 or 11 into Berserk (A25.82). Leader Creation: no drm (the table lists none); a nationality the table and the game have not reviewed is now refused rather than given no drm. Replacement and Battle Hardening follow A25.84: the 5-3-7 and its 2-2-7 HS are Replaced by Conscripts and become Fanatic when they Battle Harden (referee, pass 27); a Conscript hardens to a 3-4-7 and a 3-4-7 to the elite 4-4-7, with their HS; the elite are Replaced by the 3-4-7 and its HS. Left out (backlog section 41): 1PAATC, no escape, red TH#, PF, the broken Morale Level one higher for Hungarians against Romanians and within their own borders, a card holding two Axis Minor sides, and the Japanese (Chapter G). |
| R27.2 | How does a berserk charge find its route, and what may it charge? | A15.43, A15.431, A4.3, A4.31, A4.32, B23.4, B23.421 (pp. 84, 49 to 50, 135); the user's answer of 2026-10-01: a level-aware graph for the charge only. The route is the shortest in MF by a search from the charging stack over the entries the movement rules allow (R10.1 to R10.3), the stairwell levels above and below in the same hex, ADJACENT hexes at the same upper level, and the Bypass of an ADJACENT woods or building hex along one or two of its hexsides with the step out through the far vertex (R10.7); a stack already in Bypass starts from its lane. Every first move of a shortest route is allowed, and one into Bypass only in the lane the route takes, which the page's charge notice names in the order a move gives it (A4.31). No Wire is on any map yet, so its one MF has nothing to apply to. A charge at an enemy vehicle enters its Location in the MPh (no PAATC, A11.6) unless enemy Infantry is there too, since CC between Infantry beside a vehicle is not built (R11.16; the charge ends in place, R30.5); an Abandoned vehicle is no target (table player, pass 27); in the CCPh the berserk unit attacks the vehicle in the Location's sequential CC (A11.31): the CCPh does not end, and its side may not pass, until it has. An unarmed vehicle alone with the berserk Infantry is captured as before (A11.52). A Gun crew's Location stays barred: CC with a crew is backlog row 120. |
| R27.3 | What happens when a charge makes contact? | A.9 (p. 43), A12.15, A4.15, A4.151, A4.152, A15.432, A15.41 (pp. 78, 49, 84); the user's answer of 2026-10-01: the companions' TCs shown beside the CC record. A charge into a Location of concealed units draws among every counter there, Dummies too, one dr each (A.9): each Dummy drawn above the first real unit is eliminated, the real units of the highest dr drawn are revealed, and the Dummies drawn below stay concealed. A charge by a stack with a berserk MMC (a SMC alone does not OVR, A4.15; referee and table player, pass 27) onto a lone Known SMC not held in Melee is an Infantry OVR with no NTC and no doubled MF, and the SMC may not move away (A15.432); once the DEFENDER's window on the entry closes, the CC comes at once in the MPh, before any action but CC, an answer to a choice, or taking a prisoner (A4.152): every berserk unit there attacks the SMC together, and the SMC attacks them back whenever it may attack in CC, since that never costs it anything, with no capture, withdrawal, Infiltration, or Ambush; if both survive they are held in Melee for the CCPh. When the Close Combat package cannot resolve that CC, the units are simply left together for the CCPh. Every view sees the notice; the phasing side's view proposes the CC. The first CC record after a leader went berserk names the TCs taken with it, by attacked units and companions alike (each fire record's Berserk TC names its leader), read from the fire or Rally record the viewer may see; the CC has no Heat of Battle. |
| R27.4 | What becomes of an attack's follow-ups when an owner's option stops it? | A15.3, A7.309, A9.5, A7.7, A9.22, A23.4, A23.6 (pp. 83, 59, 62, 59, 61, 89). Spraying Fire's second Location on the same Original DR, the Encirclement the attack seals, the Fire Lane it lays, and a Thrown DC's attack on its thrower's Location and its removal are carried in the pending choice's resume, and done once the option is answered (or after a later option, in the same way). A DC's attack now asks its owners' options (Battle Hardening, Unlikely Kill, and the rest) like any other attack, instead of taking every option. |
| R27.5 | Which Play components does the pass extract? | Plan task 27.5 and section 16.17; the user's answer of 2026-10-01: one pass, C01 included. The 14 P1 roots: C01 `VehicleCloseCombatPanel`, C02 `CloseCombatPanel`, C04 `CloseCombatStacking`, C05 `CloseCombatWithdrawals`, C06 `CloseCombatAttackBuilder`, C08 `PendingChoicePanel`, C09 `PendingSurrenderPanel`, C12 `OpportunityFireAction`, C13 `SmallArmsFirePanel`, N06 `AmbushWithdrawalActions`, N08 `CloseCombatInfiltrationChoices`, N09 `PrisonerCustodyActions`, N11 `ThrowDemolitionChargeAction`, N12 `PlacedChargeDetonationActions`; the ten P2 children stay inline in them (the review records why); and the new `BerserkOverrunCloseCombat` for R27.3. |
| R28.1 | What does the card editor edit, and how? (Card Play and Map Studio Redesign Plan, pass 28) | A19.1, A26, R17.1 to R17.13, R22.1; the user's answer of 2026-10-01: every field as a form, the JSON read only. Every field of `asl-scenario-card/1` is a form: the id, title, place, date, introduction, source, adaptation notes, and aftermath; the boards, North, and playable area; the Turn Record Chart and Scenario Defender; each side's nationality, an Axis Minor's nation (R27.1; the JSON editor's rebuild dropped it), SAN, a minimal card's ELR, edge, basis, and note, Integrity, Balance, and the counters the Balance adds (R20.4); each OB group's name, ELR, setup order, and "?" (R17.12), its areas by kind (a building's hexes, a board's hex numbers, or an entry's Game Turn, edge, and hexes, with an SSR's limit, counters, MMC, and "?" for a setup area, R19.4), and its counter lines; each SSR's text, status, tokens, cited rules, and note; the Victory Conditions' kind, text, and rules and, when the game evaluates them, each outcome's winner, whether it is checked at once, and its conditions with the fields of their type (R21.3), and the result when none holds. The card is shown as read-only JSON. A number is kept as typed, and one that is not a number is named in plain words; an SSR's tokens are written only while the game reads it; a group added to a minimal card takes the side's ELR (R22.3). Every built-in card reads into the forms and builds back to the same text. |
| R28.2 | How are a card's boards chosen, and its areas picked? | A2.1, A2.5, A26.23, R17.3, R17.8, R20.5, R21.5; the user's answer of 2026-10-01: compose or take a saved map. A card's boards are rows of a board, its column and row, and whether it is turned 180 degrees, or the boards of a map saved on the Maps page (its scenario LOS rules are left out, since a card names none, and the page says so). The card's boards are drawn as one map, as a game's map is. Picking on it: a click on a hex while a building area is picked takes the whole building, joined across hexsides of building terrain on the same board, the clicked hex its anchor and id (R17.8); a click while an entry area or an exit condition is picked adds or removes that hex. An area's new id, typed or picked, is followed by its group's counter lines and the Victory Conditions naming it. A board's hex numbers and every edge stay form fields. |
| R28.3 | What is checked as the card is edited? | R17.8, R19.3, A2.5; the card is validated against the catalog as before (R17.1 to R27.1), and, when its boards can be drawn, against them: every hex an area or an exit condition names is a hex of its board; a building area is exactly one whole building; an entry's named hexes lie on its edge (no hex beyond them on that side of the map). When the boards cannot be drawn, the page says why and the card is still checked at setup (R19.3). A field that is not a number names the field and what was typed instead of the JSON parser's message. |
| R28.4 | How are user cards renamed and deleted, and what of the games that use them? | R18.1, R19.1, R22.2; the user's answer of 2026-10-01: warn, and keep a built-in card's earlier hashes. Saving a user card under another id keeps both; Rename saves it under the new id and deletes the old file, nothing being deleted when the save is refused. Rename and Delete ask first and name the games that started from the card: the game-to-card index, read on request from each live game's `game-started` record, with nothing stored beside the games. Those games then show their card as missing (R22.2 unchanged). The Guards Counterattack's SSR 3 is played by the game now (each line sets up in its building, R19.1), so its status is `game-default` and its note says so; a built-in card revised only in a note or status lists its earlier SHA-256 in `ScenarioCards.EarlierRevisions`, and a game that recorded it plays on from the current text (the six Studio games from that card recorded hashes from before the catalog 1.13.0 re-pin and stay refused). A side id apart from its nationality stays in the backlog (section 42). |
| R28.5 | Which card components does the pass extract? | Plan task 28.5 and section 16.17. K10 `ScenarioCardView`, K11 `CardSideSection` (now naming an Axis Minor side's nation), K12 `CardSpecialRulesList` (detailed on the Scenarios page, plain on Play's card panel), K13 `CardEditorForm`, K14 `CardIdentityFields` (the Source label names `#edit-source-basis`), K15 `CardMapFields` with the new `CardMapPicker` on B06 `BoardViewport`, K16 `CardTurnRecordFields`, K17 `CardSideFields` with the new `CardGroupFields` and `CardAreaFields`, K18 `ObCounterPicker` (one per group, offering the group's areas, and one for a side's Balance), the new `CardRulesFields` and `CardVictoryFields`, and K20 `CardSaveActions`. K19 `CardJsonField` is retired: the forms replace the editable JSON, and S14 `JsonDisclosure` shows the card read only. |
| R31.1 | What does the Defensive First Fire limit count? | A8.3 (p. 60): "The same unit/weapon can never fire on a moving unit in the same Location more times than the number of MF/MP expended (FRD, but a minimum of once per hex) in that Location during that MPh"; A9.2 (p. 61) says the same of a MG. The count is of the attacks made on this moving stack since its first step (an Infantry stack's or a vehicle's), in the Location of its present step, by each unit that used its own FP and by each weapon, apart. The MF are rounded down, at least once. Before the pass the count read every attack of the game at the same step number, and step numbers start again for each stack (play test P-05). |
| R31.2 | Do units in Melee take or cause a Leader Loss check under fire from outside? | A10.2 (p. 65), A11.141 (p. 70): "Units in CC never take LLMC/LLTC", and units in CC "neither take or cause LLMC/LLTC". An attack planned from pass 31 on records whether its target Location holds a unit in Melee (`targetsInMelee`), and then makes no Leader Loss check. An attack recorded before has no such fact and replays as it was resolved (referee, pass 31). |
| R31.3 | How does a pinned unit fire its MG? | A7.81 (p. 58): "Pinned Infantry fires MG/IFE/Canister as Area Fire ... and cannot ... declare a Fire Lane ... or use Intensive Fire or a Multiple ROF". A pinned unit's MG is halved as its own FP is (A7.8: in addition to any other halving), and keeps no Multiple ROF; a Fire Lane, a FT, MOL, and a thrown DC were already refused. Recorded with every attack from pass 31 on (`pinnedMgAreaFire`); an older record has none and replays as it was resolved (play test R-05). |
| R31.4 | Who Deploys and Recombines without a leader, and may Russian squads Deploy? | A25.2 (p. 93): "Russian squads may not Deploy [EXC: 20.5 & 21.22], although they can take HS losses and Recombine into squads again". A20.5 (p. 87): "An unbroken Guard squad can Deploy into HS automatically at any time, regardless of nationality or leader requirements". So the "Guards" that A1.31 and A1.32 free from the leader are the Guards of prisoners, not the Russian Guards squads, as R13.4 read them. This corrects R13.4: a Russian squad's Deploy is refused; no squad Deploys or Recombines without a leader for being of a Guards formation; Russian HS Recombine with a leader. A Guard of prisoners Deploying at will, a temporary crew (A21.22), and Russian squads set up Deployed (A2.9, which setup still accepts) are in the backlog (play test R-01). |
| R31.5 | How is fire at a Location with several leaders decided? | A10.2 (p. 65): leaders check first, "with higher Morale Level leaders checking before lower Morale Level leaders"; "For each leader eliminated ... all other friendly Personnel units with a currently lower Morale Level and in the same Location ... must take a LLMC". A10.21 (p. 66): one unbroken leader's modifier applies, not cumulative, and "the owner must choose which". A10.22: a leader takes only that of "an unbroken leader of higher morale". A10.72 (p. 68): a non-zero modifier is not declined unless another leader's is substituted. The game takes the most favorable modifier among the leaders who may give it, as the owner would. Each lost leader is taken in turn, the higher Morale Level first, one lost to another's LLMC included; a unit's second and later Leader Loss checks in one attack have their own rolls (keyed `<unit>#2`). A Commissar stays as R15.6 has him. A leader whose broken Morale Level the catalog lacks is still not decided. Before the pass any attack with two leaders among its targets was refused (play test P-02). |
| R31.6 | Who may propose what? | The user's answer of 2026-10-04. A proposal may name the view it comes from (`proposedBy`: a side, or `adjudicator`). A side's view proposes only an action of its own units (a weapon counts as its holder's), the DEFENDER's pass, the answer to a choice or a surrender put to it, and the end of a phase its side ends: the ATTACKER the PFPh, MPh, AFPh, and APh; the DEFENDER the DFPh; either side the RPh, RtPh, and CCPh, where both act (the design gave these to the phasing side; the page's hand-overs follow the side that has just acted, so either may end them). A side may not end the RtPh while the other side has a unit that must rout (A10.5): it hands over first. The adjudicator, and a caller that names no view (the tests, and every game recorded before), may propose anything. Setup, Close Combat rounds, Ambush, and a placed DC's detonation are not checked. A refusal names the side that may act and the id the proposer gave, and nothing else (play test P-07). |
| R31.7 | What may the hand-over screen show? | Rulings R23.1 to R23.6; the user's answer of 2026-10-04. Both the side leaving and the side arriving read it, so it holds only what both know: the turn and phase; why the game waits; what the arriving side will be asked to do; what has happened since that side last had the screen, read only from events no view is kept from and worded without a unit's name (a phase, a stack's move into a Location, an advance, a rout, a fire's Locations and IFT result, a Close Combat round's Location, a side's unit eliminated or captured; a Dummy gives no line); and each side's CVP and Exit VP, which do not depend on the view (A26.2). Control and the unbroken squad counts are left out (A26.15; R23.4). |
| R31.8 | Is a freed SMC Armed? | A20.551 (p. 88): "Escaped SMC are always Armed." The user's ruling of 2026-10-04: a SMC freed in any way is Armed. R14.6 armed a SMC only when his own escape succeeded; one freed by his side eliminating his Guard, abandoned by his Guard, or freed because no captor could guard him stayed Unarmed (R14.5). The planner now follows any action that leaves a SMC free and Unarmed with the event that arms him, so a game recorded before the ruling replays as it was played (in the played game the 9-2 was freed in Turn 4 and stayed Unarmed to the end). |
| R31b.1 | Whose view may read a game's replay? | Rulings R23.1 to R23.6; the user's answer of 2026-10-04. A game that has ended may be read from any view, chosen freely, and opens in the adjudicator's: nothing is left to hide, since Control is declared at the end (A26.15; R23.4) and the result is public. A side's view is then what that side knew at each step. A game still played keeps the Play page's views: a view opens behind the hand-over screen and changes through it (R23.2), the adjudicator's too. In every case the state shown is the view's (`GameLibrary.ViewOf`), and the steps a view sees, their count, their titles, the timeline, the marks, and the address read the view, never the full state. A side's view of a game still played is shown no revision and no link that carries one. "Play on from here" is offered only where the whole game may be known: an ended game, or the adjudicator's view. |
| R31b.2 | What is a step of a side's view? | A12.11, A12.12, A12.14, A12.32, A2.9; the referee's review of pass 31b. A step is one confirmed proposal. A side has a step for each one of which it may read an event, worded from those events alone, naming sides and Locations and never a unit. Two cases are decided by what the side's view sees. An attempt the side reads nothing of is still a step when its view of the map changes over it: at the table the other side sees a "?" placed where a hidden unit stood (A12.32) or placed after setup (A12.12), though not what it is; the step says only what the map showed. And an attempt whose public events say only that something minor was done (a weapon changing hands, a marker changing) is a side's step only when its view sees a change: a SW passed between two units under "?" is not the other side's to know, nor when it was done. A fire whose record is the target side's reads as its public report (A12.14) and alone is marked as read in part. |
| R31c.1 | What is a unit's name, and what may its tag tell? | Rulings R23.1, R19.8; plan section 15.9; the user's answers of 2026-10-04. A unit reads as its printed values, its kind, and a tag that never changes ("4-6-7 squad G4"; with its hex, "4-6-7 squad G4 in [G4]", where the brackets tell the hex from the tag: ruling R31c.6). A side's own units are numbered within the side in the order they entered the game, squads, half-squads, and crews in one count, leaders and heroes in another. The other side's units are numbered in the order this view first held them by name, so a tag counts only what the side has seen and never says how many the other side has. The adjudicator reads each unit's own tag. A unit that takes another's place keeps its tag; a Deployed HS adds "a" or "b"; two HS of one squad give its tag back, and two of different squads make a squad with a new number, so no tag is given twice. The other side's unit takes its parent's tag only when the view held the parent by name just before the unit was made, so a tag tells of nothing done under "?". A unit Known again after going under "?" keeps its tag. A SW has no tag. In a record a name is given only when the view could name the unit just before the event; otherwise it reads "a concealed unit", and a record whose facts are a concealed unit's own (a Deployment, a Recovery, a dismantling) is not given at all. |
| R31c.2 | Which views of a game may be chosen at will? | Rulings R23.2, R31b.1; the user's answers of 2026-10-04. A game that has ended, and a synthetic fixture: any view, on the Game inspector, the board viewer, and Replay. A game still being played: only through the hand-over, on Play and on Replay. The Game inspector does not list it. The board viewer does not list it and shows it only from Play's or Replay's link, in that page's view, with no view picker and no revision stepping; a link that names no view of the game shows no game. A side is shown no revision of a game still played, on Play, on Replay, in the board viewer, or in a stale Confirm's words, since each hidden placement adds one (A12.3). An address typed by hand is the honor system of R23.2. |
| R31c.3 | What may the hand-over screen's map show? (amends R31.7) | A12.11, A12.3, A12.12, A2.9; rulings R23.1, R23.3; the user, 2026-10-04. Both the side leaving and the side arriving read the screen, so its map shows what both know: each side's units as the other side's view holds them. A Known unit is its counter, a concealed one its "?", a hidden one is not drawn; before play starts a stack shows its top counter alone. What a view contributes is chosen by who owns the unit, never by the nationality on its counter. The Locations the screen's own lines name are outlined. During setup, when a side sets up out of the other's sight, no map is drawn. Ruling R31.7's "draws no counter" gives way to this; its other terms stand. |
| R31c.5 | When is a Location's Close Combat resolved after an Ambush? | A11.12, A11.3, A11.32, read in the PDF; the third play test. The ambusher resolves all of its attacks first (A11.32), and a unit eliminated before it attacks forfeits its attack (A11.3). So when the ambusher's attacks leave no unit of the ambushed side in the Location that is not a prisoner, nothing is left to resolve, and that Location does not hold the phase's end. The check lets a phase end that it refused before; it changes no recorded game. Approved by the user, 2026-10-04. |
| R31c.6 | How is a hex written, and where does a unit carry its hex? | The user, 2026-10-04. A hex is written in brackets wherever a player reads it: "[G4]", "[G4], level 1", "building [X3]"; a unit's tag is never bracketed. A record says the hex of the units that act and of their targets. A counter's tooltip begins with its hex and uses the unit's name. A unit named in an action's label or a picker with no Location beside it carries its hex ("8-3-8 squad G3 in [Y5]"), for a unit the view places; a unit it does not place reads as ruling R31c.1 has it. |
| R31c.7 | What may a side read of range? | A6.7, A6.11, A7.21, A7.22, A8.3, A8.31, A8.4, read in the PDF; the user's answers of 2026-10-04; design section 14. The hexes between two Locations are the map's and every view reads them, without the LOS (A6.7). The fire panel reads its targets by range and never by LOS, which is checked only once an attack is declared (A6.11). A unit's Normal Range is printed on its counter: a view reads where a range falls for its own units and for enemy units it holds by name, never for a unit under "?" or hidden. A refusal for range names the proposer's own firer or weapon, its range, and how far it fires; those lines rest on the proposer's units and the map alone, so they are told even where the target Location holds nothing the firing side may see, and then nothing else is told. `FireRange` resolves nothing; the Fire package decides every attack. |
| R31c.4 | What may a fire's arithmetic show before the dice? | A12.3, A12.11, A12.14, A7.5, A7.52, A8.4; the referee's review of pass 31c. The firing side and the adjudicator read the Fire package's own preview: each firer's FP and multipliers, the total and its column, and the DRM; no dice, no result, no Cowering. For a side it is made from what that view may know: a hidden unit in the target Location is left out of its facts, so nothing differs from what the firer would work out at the table. Where the view sees a "?" in the target Location, the lines worked out for what lies beneath it, the multipliers and DRM that rest on those units (the concealed target's halving, FFNAM, FFMO, Hazardous Movement, a gunshield, an emplacement), and the total and its column are not shown, and the preview says so. The preview is not recorded and changes no result. |
| R31d.1 | What does a broken unit leave before it routs, and who does the leaving? (corrects R13.5's check) | A4.42, A4.43, A4.431 (p. 50), A4.51, A4.52 (p. 51), A10.4 (p. 66), read in the PDF; the user's answers of 2026-10-04 (pass 31d, questions 3 and 4). A broken unit has an IPC of three PP (a MMC), one (a SMC), or none (a wounded SMC); it is not CX (A4.51), and no leader adds his IPC (A4.42). Carrying more, it leaves SW in its Location before it routs and routs with a best load: a set of its SW with the most PP that is not over its IPC ("exactly equal to its IPC or, failing that, ... the highest number of PP it can portage"). The choice is its owner's only among best loads that differ to a player; two that differ only in which of two like counters is kept are one. The rout does the leaving: a rout proposed for a laden unit names the load it keeps, or the game takes the one there is; the SW left are recorded as drops before the rout's first step, in one attempt; and the review says what is left before Confirm. A drop on its own, in the RtPh before the rout, is allowed for a SW that some best load leaves out. Before the pass the check paired the PP in the order the SW were created with the SW in the order of their ids, and in The Tractor Works a squad with a FT (5 PP) and a DC (2 PP) was made to leave the DC and then the FT; it keeps the DC. A game recorded before replays its drops as they were recorded. |
| R31d.2 | When does a concealed unit that fires lose its "?"? | A12.14 (p. 77), read in the PDF: a concealed unit loses its "?" when it fires or directs fire "in LOS of a Good Order enemy ground unit within 16 hexes"; the user's answer of 2026-10-04 (question 5). The planner reads, for each concealed unit that fires or directs, whether a Good Order enemy ground unit within 16 hexes has a LOS to its Location, or stands in it, and records the read with the attack. With the read for every concealed unit of the group, the read decides for each: seen, the "?" is lost; not seen, it is kept. The units of the target Location are no measure of it (the referee, pass 31d): one may be hidden, a prisoner of the firing side, or a unit of it in a Melee. The loss is taken as forced whenever such a unit sees; A12.14 lets a concealed viewer keep its own "?" by not forcing it, which is an owner's option in the backlog. A hidden unit does not force it, since it would have to show itself. At night the read is not given (E1.31), and an attack recorded without the read is decided from the target Location as it was. Before the pass, fire by concealed units at a Location with no unbroken unit was refused as undecided; a refusal marks no firer, so a side could try a "?" stack with a concealed unit and read from the refusal that it held Dummies. What the firing side now learns is what the table tells: after the attack is made, whether its "?" was lost. |
| R31d.3 | What is said of a Dummy stack's removal, and to whom? (amends R31.7) | A12.11 (p. 76), A12.14 (p. 77), A12.15 (p. 78), A11.19 (p. 73), read in the PDF; the user's answer of 2026-10-04 (question 6). Both players see the counters leave the map, so once it has happened both sides' records say it: one line for a stack, whatever its count of counters, when its move removes it (A12.11) or a unit tries to enter its Location (A12.15); a line for each Location as the CCPh begins (A11.19), read from the states on each side of the phase change, since the game records no event for it; and "the Dummies there are removed" in a fire's line. The hand-over screen says the same, which R31.7 left out ("a Dummy gives no line"). Before Confirm only the proposer's own Dummies are spoken of. A stack of Dummies alone that is moved without Assault Movement, or into Open Ground, is said to be removed "if a Good Order enemy unit within 16 hexes has a LOS to it", whatever the game knows: whether an enemy "?" that sees the hex is a real unit is not the mover's to learn before the move. Dummies that advance into a Location with an enemy counter on the map are said to go as the CCPh begins. An advance's summary names Known enemy units, says every other counter on the map as "a concealed stack" whatever its count, and says nothing of a hidden unit; before the pass it gave a name or "a concealed unit" for each counter. |
| R31d.4 | May a side check a LOS before it declares an attack? | A6.11 (p. 53), read in the PDF: "During play, neither player may make potential LOS checks to determine if a LOS exists for an attack until after that attack has been declared"; the user's answer of 2026-10-04 (question 7). The game is played with free LOS checks, a departure from A6.11 that the user chose for now: the LOS tab reads any LOS at any time in any view, and a fire proposal that shows its LOS may be cancelled. R31c.7's "the fire panel reads its targets by range and never by LOS" stands for the Target list. A confirmed attack whose LOS is blocked says so in its record, for both sides: "the LOS is blocked, so the attack has no effect and its firers have fired (A6.11)". A6.11 kept for a side's view of a game still played (no LOS tab for a side, nothing of the LOS in a fire proposal before Confirm) is a backlog row. |
| R31d.5 | Which of a fire refusal's reasons may the firing side read? (extends R31c.7) | Rulings R23.1 and R31c.7; A22.31 (p. 89); the user's answer of 2026-10-04 (question 8). Where the target Location holds a unit the firing side cannot see, or nothing it can see, a refusal by the Fire package's pre-check is told to that side by one rule: when any of its reasons rests on the firing side's own group and the map alone, those reasons are told and nothing else; when none does, the one sentence is told ("the Fire package does not decide every outcome ..."). So what a side reads depends only on its own group, and it never reads that there is something more. The reasons that rest on its own group and the map alone: a FT that does not fire alone, a firer that may not fire or uses no weapon, a firer that has fired, a weapon that may not fire, firers of two sides, a director who may not direct, a fire kind its phase does not have, and the range lines of R31c.7. Every reason about a target, an ELR, a missing fact, a definition, leaders, or concealment is left out, as are Subsequent First Fire's closest Known enemy and MOL's vehicle. |
| R31d.6 | May the Close Combat Phase end with a Location in which no CC was fought? | A11.1, A11.12, A11.15 (p. 72), read in the PDF; ruling R31c.5; the user's answer of 2026-10-04 (question 9). Yes, and the side that ends the phase is told first. Ending the CCPh lists, as a consequence before Confirm, each Location that holds units of both sides, prisoners and Dummies apart, in which no round was recorded this phase: "No Close Combat was fought in [Y5] this phase; the units of both sides stay there, held in Melee unless they keep their "?" (A11.15)". It is a consequence and not a refusal: where the Close Combat package does not decide the Location (in The Tractor Works, a prisoner whose Guard was not in the Location), a refusal would leave the phase with no way to end. The panel's due list has no line for a Location with nothing left after an Ambush (R31c.5), and gives the package's reason, in its sentence, where it would refuse the Ambush drs. |
| R35.1 | What is Open Ground for a rout and for Interdiction? (amends R13.3) | A10.53, A10.531, A10.532 (p. 67), B1.14, B1.16, B1.17 (p. 113), read in the PDF. Open Ground is a hex in which the particular enemy unit could apply the FFMO DRM in a hypothetical Defensive First Fire shot: a clear LOS within its range, no Hindrance of any kind along it, and no TEM the routing unit could claim against that enemy. One Rules verdict (`CouldApplyFfmo`) answers for the must-rout test and for Interdiction, over the cover fire reads: the map Hindrance by terrain and season, the Hindrance of vehicles, wrecks, and SMOKE along the LOS, the wall or hedge TEM of the hexside crossed, Height Advantage, and a wreck's or AFV's cover in the Location. This replaces R13.3's test by the terrain's name. A SMC with no other unbroken SMC of his side in his Location Interdicts with his own range alone, which for a leader is none (A10.532: a unit whose FP is halved may not Interdict); the must-rout test still counts his SW's range, since A10.5 names the SW itself. A Fanatic unit's Interdiction NMC is against its broken Morale Level raised by one (A10.8, p. 69), never above 10 (R35.5). |
