| R35.2 | Does a unit that will surrender still owe a rout, and who may end the RtPh? (amends R13.3) | A10.5 (p. 66), A19.12 (p. 86), A20.21 (pp. 86 to 87), A25.22 (p. 94), read in the PDF; the user's answers of 2026-10-09 (the pass 35 design, section 7.5, questions 1 to 3). The surrender stays where R13.3 put it, as the RtPh ends: the page gives no other moment but for a Disrupted unit. A unit bound to surrender owes no rout, so the other side's units do not wait for it in the rout order, the Rout panel says it surrenders and offers it no route, and either side may end the RtPh while the other's unit is bound to surrender or has no legal step; the end still shows each surrender and elimination as a consequence to confirm. For the other side's rout routes, though, a unit bound to surrender is a prisoner from the moment the page has it surrender, and no longer a Known enemy unit that bars a step (A10.51): a Disrupted unit from the start of the RtPh (A19.12), and an ATTACKER's unit before the DEFENDER routs, as the Comprehensive Rout Example has both (p. 69); a DEFENDER's unit that is not Disrupted still stands while the ATTACKER routs (added 2026-10-10 after the referee's review). A Disrupted unit routs only when it must and never by Low Crawl (A19.12). A Commissar never surrenders this way: it routs through the Interdiction, and one that ends the RtPh ADJACENT to its enemy is eliminated, as a Fanatic unit and a unit faced with No Quarter are. |
| R35.3 | What happens to a rout that enters a Location of concealed enemy units? (amends R13.3) | A10.533 (p. 68), A12.15 (p. 78), A.9, read in the PDF; ruling R10.11; the user's answer of 2026-10-09 (section 7.5, question 6). Concealed units are ignored when the route is planned, and the plan a side confirms says nothing of them. On entry: with a real unit among those the routing side does not know, hidden units first go beneath a "?", one real unit loses its "?" (the only one with no dice, among several by Random Selection as an ordinary entry draws, ties all), and the routing unit is repulsed: it stays in the Location it came from, pays the step's MF, and its rout ends there. With Dummies alone, they are removed and the rout goes in. The record is a rout step that names the Location attempted. A repulsed unit left ADJACENT to a Known enemy unit is eliminated for Failure to Rout as the RtPh ends and is never taken prisoner: A10.533's example (p. 68) says its enemy would have had to give up its "?" by choice before the rout entered to take it prisoner, and that keeping the "?" brings no No Quarter penalty (corrected 2026-10-09 after the referee's review; as first built it surrendered when a captor could take it). A concealed unit giving up its "?" by choice to Interdict or to change the route is not built (backlog). |
| R35.4 | May a broken unit that is not under DM rout by choice? | A10.5 (p. 66), read in the PDF: "A broken unit may rout if currently under DM", and otherwise only when it must. The refusal stands, and the backlog row of section 52 (raised in the user's game `cd-guards-5`) is closed. The user's answer of 2026-10-09 (section 7.5, question 4). |
| R35.5 | What is Good Order, who forces a concealed mover's loss of "?", and how high may a Morale Level go? (amends R10.10; withdraws the 11 of the berserk Fanatic) | A.7 (p. 43), A.18 (p. 44), A12.14 (p. 77), read in the PDF; the user's answers of 2026-10-09 (section 6, F5; section 8.4, questions 1 and 2). Good Order is a unit that is not broken, berserk, captured, stunned, shocked, or held in Melee; a pinned, CX, TI, or unarmed unit is still in Good Order. One definition in Rules (`GoodOrderOf`) replaces the planner's, which left a TI unit out and let a berserk unit in. The SW and Deployment actions keep their bar on a TI unit under its own name (`FreeToActAsPlanned`): A.7 says what Good Order is, not what a TI unit may do, and the TI rules for those actions were not read (backlog). The enemy that forces a concealed mover's loss of "?" is a Good Order enemy ground unit within 16 hexes that is not hidden and not a Passenger, as fire reads it (R31d.2); R10.10's "Good Order" was tested as "not broken". No Morale Level is raised beyond 10, "even if the unit is Fanatic, heroic, with a Commissar, and/or part of a Human Wave": the 11 a berserk Fanatic unit was given (the code named it ruling R30.3; the Deviations Pass 2 design has the clause) is withdrawn, and the ceiling is applied wherever a Morale Level is raised. The Close Combat calculator's own Good Order, with no Melee clause, stays: it chooses the leader who directs a Close Combat, where every unit is in the Melee. |
| R35.6 | Is ordnance HE halved into a marsh? (amends R10.1) | C3.53 (p. 170), B16.31 (p. 130), read in the PDF. Yes: a hit's HE FP is halved for a marsh target, beside any halving of the Area Target Type, and after a Critical Hit's doubling. R10.1's leave-out (full FP) ends. Fire from a marsh hex stays refused (B16.32; R10.1). |
| R35.7 | Are the weather's and the night's movement costs multiplied when a vehicle reverses? | D2.21 (p. 196), E3.9 (p. 231), E1.52 (p. 224), C10.1 (p. 180), read in the PDF; the user's answer of 2026-10-09. No. D2.21 puts the Reverse multiplier on "the total cost of entry of that hex, including any penalties/multipliers"; E3.9 adds the weather's MF and MP "per hexside crossed (or Bypassed), after calculating total cost"; E1.52 adds the night's MP "as if towing a Gun", and C10.1 adds the tow's "after all other modifications". So both are added once, after the multiplier, in an entry, in VBM, in Infantry Bypass, and on the Recall exit. The two sentences pull against each other only if D2.21's "total cost" is read to reach forward into E3.9; the later, narrower rule is taken. A reading. |
| R35.8 | When does a LOS "touch" the hexside a vehicle Bypasses? | D9.4 (p. 210), D2.37, D2.38 (p. 198), read in the PDF; the user's answer of 2026-10-09. A Bypassing AFV or wreck is a Hindrance only to a LOS that touches the hexside it Bypasses. The LOS is read as touching that hexside when it passes through both hexes the hexside lies between, or starts or ends in the hex across it. The LOS to the vehicle's CAFP is not built (backlog). A vehicle in Bypass takes no beneficial TEM of the woods or building it Bypasses. |
| R35.9 | When are two Locations ADJACENT, and is a vehicle that has left its Bog Non-Stopped? | A.8 (p. 43), B23.25 (p. 136), A10.5's example (p. 67), C6.3 (p. 174), D2.13 (p. 196), D8.3 and D8.4 (p. 209), read in the PDF; the user's word of 2026-10-09 for ADJACENT, which was pass 40's row. Locations are ADJACENT when there is a LOS between them (SMOKE Hindrance and NVR left out of it) and a hypothetical Infantry unit could move from the one into the other in the APh, enemy presence ignored. As built: a LOS and an Infantry step the movement rules allow, in either direction, so a hex one level up a hill, a hex across a wall or hedge, and the next level of a stairwell hex are ADJACENT; the upper level of the next building hex is not ADJACENT to the ground beside it; terrain the movement rules refuse stays not ADJACENT. "Either direction" is a reading: the rule speaks of moving from that Location into the other, and no step the game admits is one-way. A vehicle is Non-Stopped when it bears a Motion counter or has started in its MPh and not stopped since, never while bogged or immobilized; one freed by its Bog Removal is Non-Stopped from then on, since Bog Removal is its Start MP. This is a reading: C.8 (p. 162) defines a Non-Stopped vehicle as "a moving, Mobile (D.7) vehicle that has not expended a Stop MP" since its last Start MP, and says that a Bog Removal attempt does not by itself make a moving target; the reading takes "moving" there as having started, by D8.3, and not as a moving target, which D8.4 denies the vehicle until it leaves its Bog hex. The referee's review of 2026-10-09 found the page silent between the two; no code was changed for it. No rule read says the last outright; it is a reading, and the referee's review of pass 35 is asked to check it. |
| R35.10 | What does the game do with a counter whose rules are not built? | B23.9, B24, B25.15, B26 to B30; the coverage document's two notes; the user's word of 2026-10-09. A game that would place, or holds, a fortification (wire, foxhole, trench, minefield, roadblock, pillbox, Fortified Building Location), a rubble counter, or a Flame is refused whole, at setup and at every action, with the kinds named; until now it played as if the counter were absent. A stopgap, kept blunt at the user's word: passes 115 and 120 build the counters and take the refusal away kind by kind. A recorded game with such a counter still replays; only the planner refuses. The backlog's section 55.1 lists what each counter's rules are and where the inventory is silent on them. |
| R40.1 | Are a marsh hex and dry ground ADJACENT? (with R35.9) | Pass 40; approved by the user on 2026-10-11. A.8 (p. 43), B16.4 (p. 130); question F3, answered yes by the user on 2026-10-11. A marsh hex and the dry ground beside it are ADJACENT, as pass 35 built it: an advance out of the marsh is possible, and ruling R35.9 reads the step in either direction. No official answer was found either way. |
| R40.2 | How is a stack counted? (amends R26.3 and R8.10) | Pass 40; approved by the user on 2026-10-11. A stack is counted one way in every phase, by A5.5 as the page has it. A crew or HS manning a Gun is a squad in play as at setup. Five SMC are a HS for the To Hit DR too. |
| R40.3 | What do vehicles above one in a Location cost their side? (carries out R11.6) | Pass 40; approved by the user on 2026-10-11. A side's vehicles above one in a Location add to its attack penalty on the IFT, To Hit, and CC DR. The MP cost of D2.14 is unchanged, and no entry is refused for vehicles. |
| R40.4 | Which hexes are woods-road hexes? | Pass 40; approved by the user on 2026-10-11. A woods-road hex is named as VASL names it. Orchard, brush, and grain beside a road at the center dot are not read this way (none is on boards 1 to 5); a backlog row. |
| R40.5 | Who chooses the road's rate? (amends R10.1) | Pass 40; approved by the user on 2026-10-11. The road's cost is the default across a road hexside and the other terrain's cost the mover's choice, declared with the step. |
| R40.6 | Where is the point a road-rate mover is fired on at? | Pass 40; approved by the user on 2026-10-11. The point "where the hexside crossed intersects the road" is the edge point of that hexside, which VASL keeps for every hexside. On the example it gives the page's answer for all twelve cases. |
| R40.7 | What is the other side of the road for a Dash? | Pass 40; approved by the user on 2026-10-11. "on the other side of the road" is a ground-level Location beside the road Location that is neither the Location left nor beside the Location left. The page gives no geometry beyond its example ([CC8], [BB7], [AA7] of board 4, which this admits). |
| R40.8 | Is a Dash refused for its terrain? | Pass 40; approved by the user on 2026-10-11. A declared Dash is not refused for its terrain. Whether it qualifies is decided against each firer, as the page says; a Dash between two Locations that are Open Ground to every firer is legal and changes nothing but the stack's freedom to do anything else. |
| R40.9 | Does a Fire Group of several Locations halve its FP against a Dash? | Pass 40; approved by the user on 2026-10-11. A Fire Group whose Locations differ takes the Dash's halving if it holds against any one of them (A7.52). |
| R40.10 | How long does Hazardous Movement hold? (amends R10.8) | Pass 40; approved by the user on 2026-10-11. Hazardous Movement holds from the push until the unit is pinned or broken, or the Player Turn ends, in every fire phase between. The page gives the pin alone; the break and the turn's end are the ruling's, since a broken crew is no longer "so engaged" and nothing on the page carries the act into the next Player Turn. Only pushing a Gun exists in the game; each other act arrives with its own pass and joins the same list. |
| R40.11 | Which MMC takes the leader's IPC? (amends R10.8) | Pass 40; approved by the user on 2026-10-11. The leader's IPC goes to the laden MMC the mover names, for the whole move; unnamed, to the only laden MMC of his stack. Only a leader lends it (the page says "one SMC"; a hero's IPC is a backlog row). In the APh it still goes to the only laden MMC, since the advance has no field for it. |
| R40.12 | How is the NTC of an Infantry OVR taken? | Pass 40; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-11 ("Complete Pass 40 without my intervention"), approved by the user on 2026-10-12. The NTC is an act of its own, taken before the step it allows, for one stack against one Location; it is taken once in a phase by those units against that Location. Its DRM is the TEM of the Location: the hex's, or that of a wall or hedge the stack would cross when it is the larger, never both (B9.31). Hindrance within the Location is not counted. A passed NTC covers a MMC only while its taker enters with it. |
| R40.13 | What does a MMC lose by failing its own NTC? | Pass 40; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-11 ("Complete Pass 40 without my intervention"), approved by the user on 2026-10-12. A MMC that fails its own NTC loses only the OVR; it may still move elsewhere. The page gives the heavier penalty to the leader's stack alone. |
| R40.14 | Where may the SMC of an Infantry OVR be sent? | Pass 40; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-11 ("Complete Pass 40 without my intervention"), approved by the user on 2026-10-12. The SMC's destinations are the adjacent ground-level Locations it could enter by an ordinary step, in the playable area, that hold no unit of the other side. Minefields, FFE, and Wire are not read. The hex the stack came from is among them when it stands empty. |
| R40.15 | Who makes the CC attack of an Infantry OVR? | Pass 40; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-11 ("Complete Pass 40 without my intervention"), approved by the user on 2026-10-12. The prefilled CC attack of an Infantry OVR is by the stack's MMC; a leader who came with them is not joined to it by the page (the player may add him). The recapture's prefilled attack is a plain CC attack, not a capture attempt. |
| R40.16 | How is Good Order read where a record states only some conditions? | Pass 40; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-11 ("Complete Pass 40 without my intervention"), approved by the user on 2026-10-12. A unit's Good Order, where the record does not state every condition, is read as it is elsewhere in the move: barred only when a condition says no. |
| R40.17 | What if the units that owe the attack of A8.312 cannot fire as one group? | Pass 40; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-11 ("Complete Pass 40 without my intervention"), approved by the user on 2026-10-12. The units that owe the attack fire as one group where the fire rules form one. The fire package forms no group of a unit with no counter and a unit marked First Fire (ruling R12.3: one kind of Defensive fire to an attack), and it lets a Location fire once at a MF expenditure (A7.55). In that case the units that owe it and can fire as one group with those named must all be named, no one else fires with them, and the rest are released; the page says so and cites this ruling. A group of mixed kinds is a backlog row. |
| R40.18 | Has a crew that fired its Gun any FP of its own? (amends R8.4) | Pass 40; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-11 ("Complete Pass 40 without my intervention"), approved by the user on 2026-10-12. A crew that fired its Gun this Player Turn has no inherent FP, but in an attack of the kind FPF, where it adds it, whatever its own counter. A7.353's halved FP in Subsequent First Fire and Final Fire stays a backlog row (pass 45). |
| R40.19 | Which of the DEFENDER's acts wait behind the attack owed? | Pass 40; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-11 ("Complete Pass 40 without my intervention"), approved by the user on 2026-10-12. The bar is on the DEFENDER's small-arms and MG fire (`asl.game.fire`). A Gun's shot, a thrown DC, and a Starshell are not held behind the attack owed. |
| R40.20 | Which step owes the attack of A8.312? | Pass 40; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-11 ("Complete Pass 40 without my intervention"), approved by the user on 2026-10-12. The step that owes the attack is one that changed the stack's Location. Cavalry is not in the catalog. Residual FP, minefield, and OBA attacks, which the rule puts first, are resolved with the step as before. |
| R40.21 | Who Deploys and Recombines without a leader? (with R31.4) | Pass 40; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-11 ("Complete Pass 40 without my intervention"), approved by the user on 2026-10-12. The Guards of A1.31 and A1.32 are the Guards of prisoners. Two HS Recombine with no leader only when both are exempt. A Guard squad's Deployment at will is no RPh action outside the RPh. |
| R40.22 | When is the start of the RPh for Recombining? | Pass 40; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-11 ("Complete Pass 40 without my intervention"), approved by the user on 2026-10-12. "the start of any RPh" is before any other RPh action by a unit of the HS's own side; the other side's actions do not end it. Recombining keeps no side check: the page says "any RPh", where Deployment says "their RPh". |
| R40.23 | Which board names a hex two boards share? (A2.2) | Pass 40; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-11 ("Complete Pass 40 without my intervention"), approved by the user on 2026-10-12. A hex that two boards share keeps the name of the board placed later, as VASL names it, because the boards' fixtures come from VASL; the rule names it by the northeasternmost board. The setup reads both names (A2.3). |
| R40.24 | When are provisional turns built? (A3.9) | Pass 40; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-11 ("Complete Pass 40 without my intervention"), approved by the user on 2026-10-12. Provisional (gray) turns and a chart that runs past END are built with the first card that prints one; the search of the library's eight cards found none (`cards_survey.py`). |
| R40.25 | What does the US# answer for a vehicle or a Gun? | Pass 40; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-11 ("Complete Pass 40 without my intervention"), approved by the user on 2026-10-12. The member answers 4 and 5 for a 5/8 inch counter, but no caller hands it a vehicle or a Gun yet: the rules that read a vehicle's US# (its Concealment dr) are other passes'. |
| R40.26 | Whose overstacking is the target's penalty, and over which units is it counted? | Pass 40; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-11 ("Complete Pass 40 without my intervention"), approved by the user on 2026-10-12. The target's overstacking penalty is read for Personnel targets only, in the MPh for the moving side's units only, and over the units the firing side knows (neither hidden nor concealed), so that a proposal's DRM tells the firer of no unit it may not know. The firer's own penalty counts all its units. |
| R45.1 | By whose counter does a firer of a Defensive fire attack fire? (replaces part of R12.3; closes R40.17) | Pass 45; approved by the user on 2026-10-12 (question F4). Each firer of a Defensive fire attack fires by its own counter, and the attack's recorded kind is the heaviest. The Mandatory FG of A8.312 is every unit that owes the attack. |
| R45.2 | Which of the DEFENDER's acts wait behind the attack owed? (replaces R40.19) | Pass 45; approved by the user on 2026-10-12 (question F4). While the Mandatory FG's attack is owed, a Gun's shot, a thrown DC, and a Starshell by the DEFENDER wait for it. The bar is on a proposal that names a unit or a weapon of the side that is not moving, and stands only while the attack can be made at all. |
| R45.3 | When does a MG take the Bore Sighting DRM against Infantry? | Pass 45; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-12 ("Implement all parts of Pass 45 with out my intervention"), open to the user's veto. Against Infantry the minus 2 is given in an attack of the kind First Fire only (C6.44 cites A8.1), and only where every element of the attack has Bore Sighted the Location: a MG fired with its squad's FP has none. |
| R45.4 | Which attacks take the pass's rules for Cowering, Residual FP, the X#, and Mandatory Fire Direction? | Pass 45; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-12 ("Implement all parts of Pass 45 with out my intervention"), open to the user's veto. Those planned from pass 45 on. Each records the fact `coweringMarksAndWeaponResidual`; an attack recorded before the pass has none and resolves as it was recorded, so no recorded game changes (the pattern of ruling R31.3). |
| R45.5 | May a squad with no Spraying Fire option add its FP to its MG's Spraying Fire? (replaces the leave of R12.6) | Pass 45; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-12 ("Implement all parts of Pass 45 with out my intervention"), open to the user's veto. No: its MG sprays alone. A firer that adds no FP of its own (a HS with a MG, a squad with two SW) sprays with its MG as before. An attack recorded before the pass keeps R12.6. |
| R45.6 | What may a TI unit do with its SW and its HS? | Pass 45; approved by the user on 2026-10-12 (question F8). Good Order is enough for Deployment, Recombining, a Transfer, a Drop, and Recovery in the RPh (A.7, A4.8). Recovery in the MPh, which costs a MF, and dismantling, which is the weapon's use, stay barred. |
| R50.1 | How is a vehicle's weapon named and remembered? | Pass 50; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-12 ("work independently as much as possible up to merge gate and then pause"), approved by the user on 2026-10-13 ("All open proposals are approved"). A vehicle's entry in the fire request's `weapons` names its BMG and CMG; the state keeps, until the Player Turn ends, which weapons fired and in which fire phase; the vehicle carries one fire counter for all of them. A record made before the pass names none and is checked as it was. |
| R50.2 | What is "the same fire phase" of D3.5? | Pass 50; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-12 ("work independently as much as possible up to merge gate and then pause"), approved by the user on 2026-10-13 ("All open proposals are approved"). For the side that is not phasing, Defensive First Fire in the MPh and Final Fire in the DFPh are one (A.15's example). For the phasing side the PFPh, the MPh, and the AFPh are three, with D3.32's and D3.31's leave in the AFPh after a MA-only Bounding First Fire that left no counter. |
| R50.3 | What is D3.51's "same CA Change penalty as the first weapon which fired"? | Pass 50; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-12 ("work independently as much as possible up to merge gate and then pause"), approved by the user on 2026-10-13 ("All open proposals are approved"). The Case A of the shot that turned the turret. The CMG's shot within the TCA as it stands pays the first turning shot's of the fire phase; the MA pays the CMG's on its first shot after the CMG's turn. A weapon that turns the turret again pays for its own turn alone. After two MA shots that each turned, the CMG pays the first's: the page does not settle it. |
| R50.4 | May a BMG fire outside the VCA? | Pass 50; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-12 ("work independently as much as possible up to merge gate and then pause"), approved by the user on 2026-10-13 ("All open proposals are approved"). No: the hull's pivot for a shot is not built. D3.51's example stacks the NT penalty of the pivot on a turret weapon's own, with the turret carried round by the hull, which the state does not do. |
| R50.5 | May a vehicle's MG fire at another level of its own hex? | Pass 50; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-12 ("work independently as much as possible up to merge gate and then pause"), approved by the user on 2026-10-13 ("All open proposals are approved"). No: refused as out of range, as before the pass. |
| R50.6 | What is the Recall of a tank whose MA is disabled? | Pass 50; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-12 ("work independently as much as possible up to merge gate and then pause"), approved by the user on 2026-10-13 ("All open proposals are approved"). The Recall of D5.341 as the game has it: treated as a Stun until the Player Turn ends, then the counter's Recall side. A crew so Recalled repairs nothing more in that RPh. |
| R50.7 | Who repairs a tank's weapon? | Pass 50; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-12 ("work independently as much as possible up to merge gate and then pause"), approved by the user on 2026-10-13 ("All open proposals are approved"). A crew that is not shocked or stunned, BU or CE, by one Rules member the planner and the projector both read. The halftrack's MA AAMG keeps the CE test and its two copies, so that a recorded repair checks as it did. |
| R50.8 | How are the MP of Cases C1 and C2 counted? | Pass 50; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-12 ("work independently as much as possible up to merge gate and then pause"), approved by the user on 2026-10-13 ("All open proposals are approved"). As those of Cases J1 and J2 are: back from the vehicle's latest expenditure to its last one out of LOS of the target's Location, rounded up to whole MP; a LOS held since before the MPh is Case C. |
| R50.9 | What does Case C4's doubled lower dr change? | Pass 50; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-12 ("work independently as much as possible up to merge gate and then pause"), approved by the user on 2026-10-13 ("All open proposals are approved"). The Final DR alone. The Original DR, which decides the ROF, a malfunction, and an Original 2, is unchanged; the lowest Final DR that C3.6 asks about is one higher. |
| R50.10 | When does a vehicle's AFPh shot take Case C? | Pass 50; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-12 ("work independently as much as possible up to merge gate and then pause"), approved by the user on 2026-10-13 ("All open proposals are approved"). When the vehicle entered a new hex this Player Turn, is in Motion, or fired its MA in the MPh. One that only started and stopped, or only spent Delay MP, and did not fire, takes Case B (C5.2). |
| R50.11 | When may the MA fire once more in the AFPh (D3.32)? | Pass 50; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-12 ("work independently as much as possible up to merge gate and then pause"), approved by the user on 2026-10-13 ("All open proposals are approved"). When the vehicle carries no Bounding Fire counter and its MA was the only weapon that fired in the MPh. |
| R50.12 | What does the colored dr's facing apply to in a same-hex shot? | Pass 50; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-12 ("work independently as much as possible up to merge gate and then pause"), approved by the user on 2026-10-13 ("All open proposals are approved"). The hull and the turret alike (D3.2 gives one Target Facing for the hit). The AP To Kill Table's Case D is read in its first column, "0-1". |
| R50.13 | May a Bounding First Fire or Motion Fire shot claim an Acquisition? | Pass 50; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-12 ("work independently as much as possible up to merge gate and then pause"), approved by the user on 2026-10-13 ("All open proposals are approved"). No: C6.55 gives it to a Stabilized Gun alone, and the catalog has none. Such a shot takes no Case N, and what it acquires starts from nothing. Found by the reviews as a BLOCKER. |
| R50.14 | Is the Case B part of Case C raised in woods, a building, or rubble? | Pass 50; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-12 ("work independently as much as possible up to merge gate and then pause"), approved by the user on 2026-10-13 ("All open proposals are approved"). No: C5.3 says "the +2 DRM of Case B". The chart of p. 189 prints Case C as Case B plus the mount's figure and Case B as +3 there, so it can be read to make Case C one more; the text is followed. |
| R55.1 | Is a pass of about twice the plan's size split? | Pass 55; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-12 ("I want you to run without my intervention up to the Gate"), approved by the user on 2026-10-13 ("All open proposals are approved"). No: it stays one pass and is built in the order of the design's section 2, Gun Duels last, so that whatever a session's end leaves out is the task that was carried in. The plan gives six tasks, 20 rows, and 3:40 of building; the inventory names 35 rows and the frame's estimate is 9:50. |
| R55.2 | Where do the 35 rows of the inventory go? | Pass 55; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-12 ("I want you to run without my intervention up to the Gate"), approved by the user on 2026-10-13 ("All open proposals are approved"). By the design's section 3. What is moved on goes to the pass whose ground it is: A5.132 to pass 60, the rest of a vehicle's handling of its weapons and arcs to pass 105, the duel's remainder to pass 110 beside OVR Prevention. |
| R55.3 | Which counters does the pass add? | Pass 55; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-12 ("I want you to run without my intervention up to the Gate"), approved by the user on 2026-10-13 ("All open proposals are approved"). Four, every value printed in the book and none manufactured: the OQF 6-Pounder (p. 426, note 6) for APDS, a start month, and an HE Depletion Number; the M3A1 37mm AT Gun (p. 392, Note C) for Canister; a British 2-2-8 and an American 2-2-7 crew (p. 695). The catalog becomes 1.14.0; the tests, cards, and fixtures that name 1.13.0 are left as they are. |
| R55.4 | How is a counter's special ammunition written? | Pass 55; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-12 ("I want you to run without my intervention up to the Gate"), approved by the user on 2026-10-13 ("All open proposals are approved"). `<type><number>[^<year digit>[m<month>][+][E]]`, with the types A, D, H, HE, AP, and C. The month is a number, since the book's month letters are ambiguous. `+` is "from that year on". `E`, the ETO only, is kept and not read: every game is taken as the ETO, and the PTO's three more is never added. |
| R55.5 | Does the game read the Supply Chart of C8.12? | Pass 55; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-12 ("I want you to run without my intervention up to the Gate"), approved by the user on 2026-10-13 ("All open proposals are approved"). No. C8.11 (p. 177) has the availability on the counter and C8.12 lets a counter's own number differ. The chart is a test's data: each catalog counter with A or D agrees with it or the test names the difference. |
| R55.6 | How is a force Elite for C8.2? | Pass 55; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-12 ("I want you to run without my intervention up to the Gate"), approved by the user on 2026-10-13 ("All open proposals are approved"). By a special rule of the game, `elite-ammunition:<side>`, which a card's rule can carry; the shot records it, and a record without it is not Elite. No card is changed. It supersedes R7.6. |
| R55.7 | Which ammunition follows C8.9? | Pass 55; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-12 ("I want you to run without my intervention up to the Gate"), approved by the user on 2026-10-13 ("All open proposals are approved"). Every ammunition with a Depletion Number, on all three Target Types (C8.9, p. 179); on the Area Target Type nothing is built, since no mortar lists a number. An Original DR above the number is "not to have fired yet": the ROF kept, no fire counter, the Acquisition as it was. A Gun whose HE has a Depletion Number never fires HE undeclared (C2.21, p. 167). |
| R55.8 | Where may Canister be aimed? | Pass 55; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-12 ("I want you to run without my intervention up to the Gate"), approved by the user on 2026-10-13 ("All open proposals are approved"). At the five vertices of a CA whose three hexes all lie in the CA at range 2 or 3: the five circles of the figure on p. 178. |
| R55.9 | How is a Canister shot resolved? | Pass 55; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-12 ("I want you to run without my intervention up to the Gate"), approved by the user on 2026-10-13 ("All open proposals are approved"). As the ordnance shot with the ammunition `canister` and an aim of 1 to 5 (C8.4, p. 178). No To Hit DR: one IFT DR attacks every Location of the cluster and of the adjacent hex that is at the firer's elevation and in its LOS, each as a fire record on that DR with its own TEM and Hindrance. The same DR is the Depletion, the malfunction, and the ROF check. The FP is 12, 20, or 24 by caliber, halved for each cause the rule names. No leader, no FG, no Cowering; the Acquisition is lost (C6.5); friendly units are not attacked (A7.4). |
| R55.10 | Do FFMO and FFNAM apply to Canister, and is an AFPh shot halved? | Pass 55; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-12 ("I want you to run without my intervention up to the Gate"), approved by the user on 2026-10-13 ("All open proposals are approved"). FFMO and FFNAM apply to Canister in Defensive First Fire as to any IFT attack that is not an ordnance hit; this half is not built, since Canister is refused in the MPh (design 10.1). An AFPh shot by a Gun that did not move is not halved: C8.4 halves it only for Infantry that moved to man the Gun. |
| R55.11 | What is a Motion attempt, and how is its number counted? | Pass 55; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-12 ("I want you to run without my intervention up to the Gate"), approved by the user on 2026-10-13 ("All open proposals are approved"). A new action, `asl.game.motion-attempt`, for the side that is not phasing while the window on an enemy ground unit's expenditure is open (D2.401, p. 198). The dr must be at most the MF or MP the mover has spent in the vehicle's LOS in this MPh, counted as a sum over its steps and not as an unbroken LOS. |
| R55.12 | When had the mover "not been in the vehicle's LOS during that Player Turn"? | Pass 55; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-12 ("I want you to run without my intervention up to the Gate"), approved by the user on 2026-10-13 ("All open proposals are approved"). The LOS to the Location the unit began its move in decides it. The game makes that check first and refuses, which costs no attempt, where the rule has the attempt fail after a later LOS Check. |
| R55.13 | Which vehicle may attempt Motion, and what follows a pass? | Pass 55; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-12 ("I want you to run without my intervention up to the Gate"), approved by the user on 2026-10-13 ("All open proposals are approved"). A Mobile vehicle that is not stunned, shocked, or Abandoned, carries no First, Final, or Intensive Fire counter, and has made no attempt in this MPh; one already in Motion may, for the free change of CA. A VCA change in woods, a building, or rubble takes its Bog Check, and a red-MP AFV makes its Mechanical Reliability DR (D2.51); either may leave it with neither Motion nor the change. The event is `motion-attempted`. |
| R55.14 | Which Case C does a DEFENDER's vehicle in Motion fire its MA with? | Pass 55; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-12 ("I want you to run without my intervention up to the Gate"), approved by the user on 2026-10-13 ("All open proposals are approved"). Case C alone, with the lower dr doubled (C5.35, p. 172). C5.31 and C5.32 define C1 and C2 only for a vehicle First Firing in its own MPh. No sentence says so outright; it is a reading. |
| R55.15 | How is an Armor Leader held? | Pass 55; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-12 ("I want you to run without my intervention up to the Gate"), approved by the user on 2026-10-13 ("All open proposals are approved"). As a morale and a leadership on his vehicle, of the grades 8-1, 9-1, 9-2, and 10-2, with no catalog definition: D3.41 (p. 200) keeps the counter out of play. The enemy's view does not show him until the first record that carries his modifier or his morale. |
| R55.16 | Where is his modifier applied? | Pass 55; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-12 ("I want you to run without my intervention up to the Gate"), approved by the user on 2026-10-13 ("All open proposals are approved"). Always, where D3.44 lets it be: the MA's To Hit DR, the IFT DR of a MA that is a MG, the OVR DR (D7.16), the vehicle's own CC attack, and the colored dr of Bog Removal (D8.3). The leave to withhold it (A10.72) is not built. |
| R55.17 | What morale does his crew check at, and when is he lost? | Pass 55; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-12 ("I want you to run without my intervention up to the Gate"), approved by the user on 2026-10-13 ("All open proposals are approved"). At his (D3.42), in the Shock and Immobilization checks and the CE crew's MC, without his modifier. He is lost with a Recall (D5.341) or a wreck, and when the crew takes counter form (D3.43); his return when that crew re-crews is left out. |
| R55.18 | What is an Inexperienced Crew? | Pass 55; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-12 ("I want you to run without my intervention up to the Gate"), approved by the user on 2026-10-13 ("All open proposals are approved"). A condition of the vehicle, `asl:inexperienced-crew`, given at setup (D3.45, p. 200): plus 1 on the rolls an Armor Leader modifies, a crew morale of 6, never lost. |
| R55.19 | When and how is a Gun Duel offered? | Pass 55; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-12 ("I want you to run without my intervention up to the Gate"), approved by the user on 2026-10-13 ("All open proposals are approved"). When the side that is not phasing proposes a Defensive First Fire ordnance shot at the moving vehicle, the firer is not concealed, the vehicle is not conducting an OVR, its MA could Bounding First Fire at the firer without a change of CA, and its Gun Duel DRM is lower (C2.2401, p. 167). The commit is the declaration: a pending choice of the kind `gun-duel` for the ATTACKER. On `decline` the declared shot resolves; on `duel` one commit holds the vehicle's shot and then the declared shot, which is void when its firer can no longer fire. The Gun Duel DRM is the firer-based To Hit DRM and the Acquisition, without Case C4's doubled dr. |
| R55.20 | What of the Gun Duel is left out? | Pass 55; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-12 ("I want you to run without my intervention up to the Gate"), approved by the user on 2026-10-13 ("All open proposals are approved"). The equal case, in which the Final DRs decide the order; fire that is not ordnance on either side; the second duel with the ROF as a DRM; a duel declared by the DEFENDER (C5.33); a Gyrostabilizer. Each to pass 110. When the totals are equal no duel is offered, though the rule gives one. |
| R55.21 | In what order is the pass built? | Pass 55; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-12 ("I want you to run without my intervention up to the Gate"), approved by the user on 2026-10-13 ("All open proposals are approved"). The counters first, with the notation they are written in; then fire at another level, the ammunition, Canister, Armor Leaders, Motion attempts, and the duel last, which sums the firer's DRM of all the others (the design's section 2). |
| R55.22 | Whose To Hit numbers take the color of the chart of p. 695? | Pass 55; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-12 ("I want you to run without my intervention up to the Gate"), approved by the user on 2026-10-13 ("All open proposals are approved"). A Gun's and a vehicle's MA's: British black, American red before 1944 and black from 1944 (C3.3, p. 169). A light mortar and a LATW keep the color they had before the pass, red for every nationality but the German, so that no recorded shot changes. |
| R55.23 | Is a vehicle's MA AAMG held to C2.6's limit? | Pass 55; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-12 ("I want you to run without my intervention up to the Gate"), approved by the user on 2026-10-13 ("All open proposals are approved"). Yes, as its BMG and CMG are, though the rule names "non-AA vehicular MG" (C2.6, p. 168). What an AA weapon may do is pass 105's. |
| R55.24 | Does a target behind a wall or hedge claim Height Advantage on a To Hit DR? | Pass 55; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-12 ("I want you to run without my intervention up to the Gate"), approved by the user on 2026-10-13 ("All open proposals are approved"). No: it has another positive TEM to claim (B10.31, p. 125). That TEM itself is not given on an ordnance To Hit DR, which is older than the pass and is left as found. |
| R55.25 | Does a shot that had no round leave an Intensive Fire counter? | Pass 55; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-12 ("I want you to run without my intervention up to the Gate"), approved by the user on 2026-10-13 ("All open proposals are approved"). No: it leaves no fire counter of any kind (C8.9: "not to have fired yet for any purpose"). C8.92 only says the number is not changed by Intensive Fire. |
| R55.26 | What is "barring obstacles in the first hex of the cluster" (C8.4)? | Pass 55; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-12 ("I want you to run without my intervention up to the Gate"), approved by the user on 2026-10-13 ("All open proposals are approved"). The LOS to each Location: a Location of the aim is attacked when the firer has a LOS to it, and no further test is made of the cluster's first hex. |
| R55.27 | When does a record carry the morale of a target's crew? | Pass 55; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-12 ("I want you to run without my intervention up to the Gate"), approved by the user on 2026-10-13 ("All open proposals are approved"). Only when that crew took a check in it, so that a miss tells the enemy nothing of an Armor Leader. |
| R55.28 | Is a Motion attempt that no dr could pass an attempt? | Pass 55; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-12 ("I want you to run without my intervention up to the Gate"), approved by the user on 2026-10-13 ("All open proposals are approved"). No: against a mover that has spent less than one MF or MP in the vehicle's LOS it is refused and costs nothing. |
| R55.29 | Is an Armor Leader who is not yet revealed counted in a Gun Duel DRM? | Pass 55; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-12 ("I want you to run without my intervention up to the Gate"), approved by the user on 2026-10-13 ("All open proposals are approved"). No: he is left out when a duel is offered and in what is shown of it, for the moving vehicle and, since the reviews, for a declaring vehicle too. The shot each then fires uses him and reveals him. This can deny the ATTACKER a duel his modifier would earn. |
| R55.30 | Does a Canister shot call a Sniper? | Pass 55; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-12 ("I want you to run without my intervention up to the Gate"), approved by the user on 2026-10-13 ("All open proposals are approved"). No: it follows ordnance, which calls none. Found by the reviews: the plain shot called none and a shot resumed after an owner's choice did. Superseded by R71.13 (pass 71, 2026-10-26): ordnance's To Hit DR, and a Canister shot's, call a Sniper as A14.1 names them; this ruling recorded the code rather than the rule. |
| R55.31 | Is a number that starts in a month of the game's year available when the game gives no month? | Pass 55; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-12 ("I want you to run without my intervention up to the Gate"), approved by the user on 2026-10-13 ("All open proposals are approved"). No (C8.11, p. 177). The page's note and the refusal say why. Undated HEAT behaved so before the pass. |
| R55.32 | May a Hidden vehicle attempt Motion? | Pass 55; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-12 ("I want you to run without my intervention up to the Gate"), approved by the user on 2026-10-13 ("All open proposals are approved"). No (A12.32, p. 80: "Hidden units may not move (even within their hex or to change a CA)"). A record of such an attempt is refused. Found by the reviews as a BLOCKER: the attempt was written where the enemy reads it. |
| R55.33 | Does gaining Motion by a Motion attempt cost a concealed vehicle its "?"? | Pass 55; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-12 ("I want you to run without my intervention up to the Gate"), approved by the user on 2026-10-13 ("All open proposals are approved"). Yes, whether or not its CA changes (A12.141, p. 78, "any other activity"), as a vehicle's own step does. A failed attempt costs none (D2.401). |
| R60.1 | Is a pass larger than the plan's entry split, and in what order is it built? | Pass 60; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-15 ("this is my word to start pass 60, run without my intervention up to the gate"), approved by the user on 2026-10-15 ("All rulings approved"). No: it stays one pass. The plan names 37 rows and 4:40 of building; the inventory names 49 rows and the prompt carries in task 50.5 (MG fire against armor), and the frame's estimate is 6:40 of building. The rows are placed by the design's section 3 and built in the order of its section 2: HE Equivalency first, since D.8A's attack takes its FP; the Specific Collateral Attack; the mortar; FT, MOL, and DC; the Guns; the carried-in MG last, so that whatever a session's end leaves out is the task carried in. |
| R60.2 | Where do the pass's tables go? | Pass 60; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-15 ("this is my word to start pass 60, run without my intervention up to the gate"), approved by the user on 2026-10-15 ("All rulings approved"). Into the Rules project as small tables with their page, not into a transcription, so that no case matrix or package is changed and the four packages are not pinned again: C8.31's HE Equivalency (p. 178: AP below 37mm 1; AP 37mm and up 2; every APCR, APDS, and ATR 1; BAZ and PIAT 8; PSK, PFk, and H#[9] 12; PF 16; other HEAT one IFT column left of the Gun's HE FP), C7.34's Basic TK# (p. 190: DC 16, FT 8, MOL 6), the DC and FT/MOL columns of C7.7 (p. 190), and C11.6 (p. 182). `BOTH_SKIP` keeps `GameEvent.RulePackage` in case a later task must pin. |
| R60.3 | How does a rule that changes an attack that could be made before the pass reach a recorded game? | Pass 60; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-15 ("this is my word to start pass 60, run without my intervention up to the gate"), approved by the user on 2026-10-15 ("All rulings approved"). Only through a fact on the record: A9.74, C3.71's reversal of Height Advantage, and D.8A after a Vehicle Target Type hit are applied only where the record carries the pass's fact for them, taken from the record at replay and never recomputed from the state. The planner sets the fact on every new attack, so an attack recorded before the pass resolves as it did. The replay digest is where a miss shows. |
| R60.4 | How do AP, APCR, APDS, HEAT, and an ATR fire at an unarmored target? | Pass 60; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-15 ("this is my word to start pass 60, run without my intervention up to the gate"), approved by the user on 2026-10-15 ("All rulings approved"). On the Infantry Target Type at their HE Equivalency FP (C8.31, p. 178), by the HE path that exists: TEM as Case Q on the To Hit DR, one IFT DR for every unit hit, a Critical Hit doubling it and reversing positive TEM. HEAT is fired at Infantry only where the target Location gives a wall, building, rubble, or pillbox TEM; an ordnance HEAT round needs an H# and a printed HE FP to step left from, and a Gun with no HE is refused it. None leaves Residual FP. An ATR fires at Personnel on the Infantry Target Type only if it is 20L; the catalog's two ATRs are not, so they keep firing at Personnel as Small Arms. |
| R60.5 | How do a PF and a PSK fire at Personnel? | Pass 60; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-15 ("this is my word to start pass 60, run without my intervention up to the gate"), approved by the user on 2026-10-15 ("All rulings approved"). Under C8.31's bar, at 16 and 12 FP (C13.31, C13.9). The PF Check takes +1 at a target that is not an AFV and +1 in August and September 1943; a PF's hit affects one unit by Random Selection. The firer's choice of a Known unit manning a Gun or SW after the hit is left out (backlog). |
| R60.6 | How do AP, HEAT, and an ATR fire at a Gun? | Pass 60; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-15 ("this is my word to start pass 60, run without my intervention up to the gate"), approved by the user on 2026-10-15 ("All rulings approved"). AP, APCR, APDS, HEAT, and any ATR fire at a Gun on the Infantry Target Type at their equivalency FP, and the Gun's fate is C11.4's with C11.6's note 5: a K eliminates the Gun when AP is fired (C11.52, C13.23, p. 183). A non-20L ATR attacks only the Gun and its manning crew. |
| R60.7 | When is the Specific Collateral Attack made? | Pass 60; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-15 ("this is my word to start pass 60, run without my intervention up to the gate"), approved by the user on 2026-10-15 ("All rulings approved"). After a Vehicle Target Type hit on an AFV that does not destroy it (a TK DR missed, an Immobilization, an Unconfirmed Kill), its CE crew takes an IFT attack at the round's IFT FP (HE, or its HE Equivalency) on the Original TK DR, with the CE DRM alone (D.8A, p. 192). None after a Dud, a Shock this hit causes (C7.42), a crew already stunned or shocked, a buttoned-up crew, or a vehicle destroyed. One attack for a Multiple Hit, and no Residual FP. Passengers and Riders: pass 65. |
| R60.8 | May an Infantry Target Type shot name a Location that holds a vehicle? | Pass 60; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-15 ("this is my word to start pass 60, run without my intervention up to the gate"), approved by the user on 2026-10-15 ("All rulings approved"). Yes, one that holds an unarmored vehicle (C3.32, C3.41, p. 169). Each vehicle and each unit is hit on its own Final TH# (the vehicle by its size; a concealed unit with Case K); every unit and unarmored vehicle hit shares one Effects DR on the HE FP column, the vehicle on the Vehicle line (A7.308, A7.309). An AFV is immune, and its CE crew takes a General Collateral Attack on the same DR when the AFV's own TH# is reached. A Location holding only AFVs stays the Vehicle Target Type's (C3.41, D.6). |
| R60.9 | How is a Location of Known and concealed units hit? | Pass 60; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-15 ("this is my word to start pass 60, run without my intervention up to the gate"), approved by the user on 2026-10-15 ("All rulings approved"). By one To Hit DR judged per unit (C3.4, p. 170); a concealed unit hit loses its "?" as A12.14 has it, and one missed keeps it. Units out of LOS or too close: backlog. |
| R60.10 | How does a mortar's hit attack a vehicle? | Pass 60; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-15 ("this is my word to start pass 60, run without my intervention up to the gate"), approved by the user on 2026-10-15 ("All rulings approved"). On the IFT at half the HE FP, never To Kill (C1.55, p. 165; C3.332, C7.345, C9.1): -1 if every AF is 4 or less, -1 if OT, +1 if every AF is 8 or more, with the TEM that gives no HD; a KIA destroys it, a DR at most half the K/# DR burns it, and the K/# DR and one above Shock a turret hit or Immobilize a hull hit. An unarmored vehicle is on the Vehicle line. The CE crew takes a General Collateral Attack on the same DR. A Critical Hit is at double the full FP with no TEM, the AF and OT modifiers kept (C3.72). **Corrected by R60.23 (pass 60): the hit's Location is read from the Original IFT DR's dice, not from the To Hit DR's, as the frame first said.** |
| R60.11 | May a mortar fire at a hex whose units are on several levels of a building? | Pass 60; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-15 ("this is my word to start pass 60, run without my intervention up to the gate"), approved by the user on 2026-10-15 ("All rulings approved"). Yes, outside an Interior Building Hex (B23.32, p. 136): each Location is hit on its own To Hit DR modifiers, and one Effects DR attacks every Location hit, each with its building TEM and +1 for each level above it; a Critical Hit goes to one Location by Random Selection. Friendly units in the target hex stay refused (backlog). **Corrected by R60.24 (pass 60): the frame said a Location out of the firer's LOS is not attacked; C3.33 attacks it when the hardest target in the firer's LOS is hit.** |
| R60.12 | What do Air Bursts do in full? | Pass 60; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-15 ("this is my word to start pass 60, run without my intervention up to the gate"), approved by the user on 2026-10-15 ("All rulings approved"). -1 against an unarmored, CE, or OT target in woods, combined with an entrenchment or Emplacement, and kept on a Critical Hit (B13.3, p. 128); an OT AFV so hit is attacked as unarmored (D5.311, p. 203). None against a unit in Bypass. |
| R60.13 | What does a mortar's Area Acquisition cover? | Pass 60; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-15 ("this is my word to start pass 60, run without my intervention up to the gate"), approved by the user on 2026-10-15 ("All rulings approved"). Every Location of the hex in its LOS (C6.521, p. 174). |
| R60.14 | What do a Critical Hit and Indirect Fire do to Height Advantage and a gunshield? | Pass 60; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-15 ("this is my word to start pass 60, run without my intervention up to the gate"), approved by the user on 2026-10-15 ("All rulings approved"). A Critical Hit on a Direct Fire target that claimed Height Advantage reverses it to -1 (C3.71, p. 170). The gunshield gives +1, not +2, against Indirect Fire (C11.4, C11.5, p. 182). |
| R60.15 | How do a FT and a MOL attack an AFV? | Pass 60; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-15 ("this is my word to start pass 60, run without my intervention up to the gate"), approved by the user on 2026-10-15 ("All rulings approved"). A fire request names an AFV in the target Location as the FT's or MOL's target (predesignation; A22.34, A22.612, p. 89). A FT's Basic TK# is 8, halved at Long Range before +1 CE and +2 OT; a MOL's is 6 with +2 OT or +1 CE (closed-topped), -2 against a moving vehicle (C.8), and +1 or +2 for an elevation advantage; both take +1 through the rear, no AF, and the FT/MOL column (below the TK# burns; equal eliminates with Crew Survival). A MOL's DR also attacks the CE crew at the Small Arms FP plus 4 and every other unarmored unit at the Small Arms FP; a failed MOL Check is an IFT attack on them all without the 4. A FT attacks only the AFV and, if it survives, its CE crew. |
| R60.16 | How does a DC attack an AFV? | Pass 60; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-15 ("this is my word to start pass 60, run without my intervention up to the gate"), approved by the user on 2026-10-15 ("All rulings approved"). By the Position DR (C7.346, p. 177) with its modifiers whose facts exist: 5 or less uses the Aerial AF, 6 to 8 the AF, 9 to 11 attacks the CE crew only on a new DR, 12 or more attacks the other unarmored units of the Location. Its TK# is 16 less the AF, +1 through the rear, on the DC column. A Placed DC needs the PAATC that exists (A23.3, A23.5). A same-hex attack is through the rear; otherwise the hexside crossed. A MOL at an unarmored vehicle, a MOL's Flame, ATMM, a vehicle as the thrower, and a throw down a level: backlog. |
| R60.17 | Which KIA destroys the weapons its victims held? | Pass 60; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-15 ("this is my word to start pass 60, run without my intervention up to the gate"), approved by the user on 2026-10-15 ("All rulings approved"). Every Final KIA of an IFT attack, a Canister KIA included (A9.74, p. 65; C8.42, p. 178): each SW and Gun held by a unit it eliminated rolls a dr on the same column; a KIA destroys it, a K malfunctions it, or eliminates it if it was malfunctioned or has an X#. Ordnance, DC, and OBA at the Gun itself follow C11.4 instead. Unpossessed weapons checked by Indirect Fire and by an OVR, the OVR's end-of-MPh check, and an OVR of an empty Location: backlog. |
| R60.18 | May an unattended Gun be a target, and what ends Emplacement? | Pass 60; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-15 ("this is my word to start pass 60, run without my intervention up to the gate"), approved by the user on 2026-10-15 ("All rulings approved"). An unattended Gun may be the target of the Infantry and Area Target Types: a KIA destroys it and a K malfunctions it (C3.41, C11.4). A Gun manned by a non-crew loses Emplacement for the game. An attack by a MG, small arms, a FT, or a MOL destroys a Gun only through A9.74 (C11.51, C11.6). **Corrected by R60.26 (pass 60): only a Gun that starts the scenario manned by a non-crew unit loses its Emplacement for the game.** |
| R60.19 | How does a MG make its To Kill attack on an AFV? | Pass 60; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-15 ("this is my word to start pass 60, run without my intervention up to the gate"), approved by the user on 2026-10-15 ("All rulings approved"). A SW MG fired alone at a named AFV within Normal Range in the PFPh or DFPh makes a To Hit DR on the Vehicle Target Type with black numbers, then a To Kill DR with Basic TK# 4 (12.7mm and 15mm by their rows), Case A, the 25mm row of Case D, less the AF (A9.61, p. 64); below the number eliminates (at most half burns), equal Stuns, and there is no Shock, Immobilization, Critical Hit, or Multiple Hit; the ROF is kept as ordnance; R60.7's attack follows. A vehicle's MA that is a MG may make it (D3.54, p. 201); its other MGs may not. Its Residual FP and the AFPh: backlog. |
| R60.20 | When does CC Reaction Fire cancel a Fire Lane, and what does its CC counter bar? | Pass 60; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-15 ("this is my word to start pass 60, run without my intervention up to the gate"), approved by the user on 2026-10-15 ("All rulings approved"). A9.223's narrow reading (p. 63): a Fire Lane is cancelled when its MG fires CC Reaction Fire. D7.21's CC counter (p. 207) bars its SW and Guns from Non-CC Reaction Fire. |
| R60.21 | Where does A5.132 go? | Pass 60; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-15 ("this is my word to start pass 60, run without my intervention up to the gate"), approved by the user on 2026-10-15 ("All rulings approved"). To pass 110, beside OVR Prevention: a hit on another vehicle of a vehicle-overstacked hex needs a Random Selection by size among vehicles and a target choice the To Hit does not have (A5.132, p. 52). |
| R60.22 | Is the upper superstructure of a non-turreted AFV built? | Pass 60; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-15 ("this is my word to start pass 60, run without my intervention up to the gate"), approved by the user on 2026-10-15 ("All rulings approved"). With a fact on the record if 2f has time, else moved to pass 105 (C3.9, p. 171; the end of ruling R7.4). 2f had no time: it is moved to pass 105. |
| R60.23 | From which DR does a mortar's hit on an AFV take its hit Location? (corrects R60.10) | Pass 60; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-15 ("this is my word to start pass 60, run without my intervention up to the gate"), approved by the user on 2026-10-15 ("All rulings approved"). From the Original IFT DR's dice, not the To Hit DR's: the C9.5 example and the C7.7 chart's note E ("Indirect Fire uses the Original IFT DR for hit location", p. 190). The frame's reading was built first and sent back. |
| R60.24 | Does a mortar's hit attack the target hex's units out of its LOS? (corrects R60.11) | Pass 60; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-15 ("this is my word to start pass 60, run without my intervention up to the gate"), approved by the user on 2026-10-15 ("All rulings approved"). Yes, when the shot hit the non-hidden enemy target in its LOS that was the hardest to hit (the highest net To Hit DRM): they are attacked on the same Effects DR with their own TEM and levels above (C3.33, p. 169; the C3.331 example, p. 170). Found by the first referee. |
| R60.25 | Does a Collateral Attack follow a Stun the hit causes? | Pass 60; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-15 ("this is my word to start pass 60, run without my intervention up to the gate"), approved by the user on 2026-10-15 ("All rulings approved"). No: a Stun buttons the crew up "immediately" (D5.34, p. 203), so no Vulnerable crew is left, as after a Shock (R60.7). The record says so. Found by the table player. |
| R60.26 | Which non-crew manning ends a Gun's Emplacement? (corrects R60.18) | Pass 60; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-15 ("this is my word to start pass 60, run without my intervention up to the gate"), approved by the user on 2026-10-15 ("All rulings approved"). Only a Gun that "starts a scenario hooked up or manned by a non-crew unit" loses its Emplacement for the scenario, with a move and a forfeit of Wall Advantage (C11.3, p. 182). A non-crew unit that mans a Gun later does not end it; C11.2 only withholds the +2 while a squad mans it. Found by the second referee. |
| R60.27 | May a MG make its To Kill attack at a concealed AFV? | Pass 60; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-15 ("this is my word to start pass 60, run without my intervention up to the gate"), approved by the user on 2026-10-15 ("All rulings approved"). No: A9.61 asks for no halved FP, and on the IFT a concealed target would halve it (A7.23). Whether Case K replaces the halving is left to the backlog. Found by the second referee. |
| R65.1 | Is a pass twice the plan's size split, and in what order is it built? | Pass 65; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-23 ("start on Pass 65 without my intervention"), open to a veto. No: it stays one pass. The plan gives 7:00 of building; the frame's estimates from the code sum to about 15:00, since no rule reached a Passenger at all (`GameState.At` left them out of their Location, and `KeepPassengers` eliminated them with their vehicle). Built in the design's section 2 order: the foundations (the crew kind, the catalog, the index), the Passengers under fire, their action, loading, then Riders, Carriers, the crew's own acts, the TC and Survival, and last the captured vehicles and the wrecks, so that whatever a session's end leaves out is theirs. |
| R65.2 | How does the code tell a crew when a vehicle crew is a kind of its own? | Pass 65; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-23 ("start on Pass 65 without my intervention"), open to a veto. One Rules member, `ScenarioA1Definitions.IsCrew(kind)`, true for `asl:crew` and `asl:vehicle-crew`, replaces each literal comparison whose rule speaks of any crew (A1.123, D5.1: a vehicle crew has every capability of an infantry crew but its Strength Factor). A site whose rule is for an infantry crew alone keeps the literal with a comment; none was found. |
| R65.3 | What does catalog 1.18.0 add? | Pass 65; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-23 ("start on Pass 65 without my intervention"), open to a veto. Eight `asl:vehicle-crew` definitions from the National Capabilities Chart (physical page 695, sheet NCC, read on two renders): German, Russian, British, and Finnish 1-2-7 with broken morale 7; American 1-2-6, 7; Italian and Axis Minor 1-2-6, 5; French 1-2-7, 6; BPV 7, 5, 7, 6, 7, 5, 5, 5. A vehicle class (`asl:vehicle-class`, vocabulary 1.19.0) on the 17 vehicles, from the Chapter H Type column or manufactured under R0.3 on sheet MFG. The Bren carrier's Passenger capacity corrected from 5 to 4 (D6.81). The red CS# trait `asl:cs-red` (D5.7), printed on none of the five listed vehicles. Rider capacity is derived in Rules from the class, and the Carrier's HS is the nationality's 2-4-7 HS, so neither is an attribute. |
| R65.4 | May a game recorded at an older catalog version fire? | Pass 65; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-23 ("start on Pass 65 without my intervention"), open to a veto. Yes: the catalog checks accept `asl-scenario-a1` at the code's major version and a minor version no later than the code's, as the vocabulary's `Serves` does, through one Rules member (`ScenarioA1Definitions.CatalogServes`) for the five checks (fire twice, rally, CC, ordnance). A catalog only adds and corrects. Since the projector reads the loaded catalog by name, this changes nothing visible today. |
| R65.5 | Which fact gates the pass's effects on an attack, since records since pass 60 carry `specificCollateral`? | Pass 65; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-23 ("start on Pass 65 without my intervention"), open to a veto. A nullable list of each vehicle's PRC (`prc`) on `FireVehicle` and on the ordnance vehicle target, built from the index of R65.25 and written by every plan since the pass, empty when nobody is aboard; its absence resolves as before. A destroying event carries each Passenger's and Rider's Survival; where it is missing, they are eliminated with the vehicle as before. |
| R65.6 | When are PRC Vulnerable? | Pass 65; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-23 ("start on Pass 65 without my intervention"), open to a veto. A halftrack's Passengers are CE or BU with its crew, read from the vehicle's `asl:bu` (D5.33, D6.61). A broken, pinned, or shocked Passenger is BU, and a Passenger is never Stunned: a failed MC makes it BU and broken (D6.62, D9.1). A BU halftrack's Passengers are attacked only by D6.61's elevation exception or by TPBF (A7.211). A truck's PRC are always Vulnerable, with no CE DRM (D6.7). Riders are always Vulnerable (D6.23). |
| R65.7 | How are the Collateral Attacks on PRC made? | Pass 65; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-23 ("start on Pass 65 without my intervention"), open to a veto. The General (D.8, D.8B) takes the attack's column and DR against every Vulnerable PRC, with TEM or the CE DRM, never both. The Specific (D.8A, D6.71) takes the IFT FP or HE Equivalency of the weapon, with the CE DRM alone. Neither applies when the vehicle is destroyed, after a Dud or a Deliberate Immobilization, or to shocked or stunned PRC (then only Riders). D5.31 (the CE DRM not cumulative with a positive TEM or Height Advantage under Infantry fire) applies where the attack carries `prc`, which lifts ruling R25.6's refusal of an AFV in positive TEM. D5.311's crew under Air Bursts is attacked as unarmored. |
| R65.8 | What of more vehicles in a Location than the column's highest KIA#? | Pass 65; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-23 ("start on Pass 65 without my intervention"), open to a veto. A7.308's Random Selection of the vehicles affected replaces the refusal `vehicles-beyond-kia`, with the selection's dr on the record. Built in increment 2k for the IFT (ruling R65.26); the ordnance Infantry Target Type and C1.55's mortar keep the refusal and move to pass 110. |
| R65.9 | How do PRC check morale and survive? | Pass 65; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-23 ("start on Pass 65 without my intervention"), open to a veto. Passengers and Riders check at their own Morale Level and take Infantry results, with DM on a broken Passenger (D6.66). Survival (D6.9) is one DR per unit against the CS# or cs#, +1 if broken, no Shock or Stun DRM, none in CC or when the vehicle burns. Survivors are placed beneath the wreck; a SW not held by a survivor is eliminated. |
| R65.10 | How do Passengers fire? | Pass 65; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-23 ("start on Pass 65 without my intervention"), open to a veto. `PassengerBar` is lifted for fire, with LOS from the vehicle's Location. A truck's and other Passengers fire at half, halved again for Bounding, Motion, or Area fire; an armored halftrack's CE Passengers fire at half only when it moved. The SW allowed are a LMG, a PIAT, and a DC (D6.72). Firing bars unloading in that phase (D6.5), and Prep Fire freezes the vehicle (D.3). Fire from a vehicle in Bypass stays refused (pass 100). A SW passes to PRC or Infantry in the Location (D6.31). |
| R65.11 | When does a broken Passenger rout? | Pass 65; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-23 ("start on Pass 65 without my intervention"), open to a veto. It may rout beneath a Stopped vehicle, spending all its initial RtPh MF, and must when the crew is eliminated, broken, or has Abandoned. No fact is needed. |
| R65.12 | Whom does a leader aboard direct? | Pass 65; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-23 ("start on Pass 65 without my intervention"), open to a veto. A CE Passenger leader directs his halftrack's Passengers, and the hex's other units only if the halftrack has not moved that phase. An Armor Leader directs Passengers only inside his halftrack's FG. MC, Rally, and LLMC reach are confined to the same vehicle. This is D6.64's makeup alone, built apart from the general FG code. |
| R65.13 | What do Passengers do in an OVR and in CC? | Pass 65; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-23 ("start on Pass 65 without my intervention"), open to a veto. In an OVR a CE armored halftrack's Passengers add half their printed FP and other Passengers a quarter, then tripled, with one leadership modifier from the Armor or the Passenger leader (D7.11, D7.16); results against them after the declaration do not change the OVR's FP; an OVR may attack an enemy's Vulnerable PRC (D7.12). In CC BU Passengers do not attack, Riders are apart with +1 (A11.611), and Passengers of a Mobile vehicle leave Melee after the first round (A11.71); decided in Rules and not wired into Play (pass 70). A11.52's capture of Passengers moves to pass 75. |
| R65.14 | Are (un)loaders attacked as they board or leave? | Pass 65; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-23 ("start on Pass 65 without my intervention"), open to a veto. The step's (un)loaders are Infantry targets of Defensive First Fire with FFNAM, and with FFMO only when unloading from an unarmored conveyance in Open Ground (D6.5, A4.6), by the fact `embarking`. A load fails if the loader ends pinned, broken, or eliminated, or the vehicle is destroyed (D5.43 and its EX), and an event puts it back on foot. A pinned unloader stays in the Dismount Location (A7.821). |
| R65.15 | What completes loading and unloading? | Pass 65; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-23 ("start on Pass 65 without my intervention"), open to a veto. A unit boards as the last step of its own move, with a leader's IPC (A4.42); a SW may be left aboard a halftrack or Carrier. After unloading the four-MF cap holds and Double Time is barred. Unloading into an enemy-occupied Location was to place a CC counter; none exists, and the proposal says the units are not held in Melee (backlog 71.1, pass 70). Between moves only the vehicle and its disembarking PRC move (`interim`). D2.401's time aboard counts toward a later Motion attempt. Load and unload get records of their own, and the move-vehicle schema gains its missing kinds and arguments. |
| R65.16 | How do units board as Riders? | Pass 65; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-23 ("start on Pass 65 without my intervention"), open to a veto. A load names `riders`, recorded as `riders: true` on the step; its absence means Passengers, so older games replay as before. Rider capacity follows the class (14 PP; a Carrier 8; another AFV one SMC; a truck none), counted apart from Passenger PP; unload takes either role. Riders are allowed for the Russians from 1942 and every nationality from 1943 (D6.2); a game with no scenario year refuses them and says why. |
| R65.17 | When do Riders Bail Out, and what follows? | Pass 65; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-23 ("start on Pass 65 without my intervention"), open to a veto. One Rules member decides D6.21's and B13.43's Bail Out: before woods, orchard, a building (but a friendly Vehicular-Sized Entrance), rubble, a Water Obstacle, or bocage (but a Breach), unless on a road hexside, in Bypass, or in a stream; and when a turreted AFV changes its TCA. Riders use no SW, fire at half their FP or a quarter once the vehicle moved or is in Motion, and take +1 to their CC DR (D6.22). A pin or break makes them Bail Out (D6.23). The event `rider-bailed-out` carries the cause, the NMC DR, and each SW's dr; the unit is beneath the vehicle, its movement ended, with a fire counter if unbroken, and FFNAM applies. |
| R65.18 | How is a Carrier played? | Pass 65; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-23 ("start on Pass 65 without my intervention"), open to a veto. A vehicle of class carrier is never BU, and its crew, HS, and Passengers are always CE and Vulnerable, stunned or shocked included (D6.84). Its inherent crew is the nationality's 2-4-7 HS, placed on dismount, Abandonment, and Survival, unless it is armed with a FT or a 76mm mortar or tows a Gun (D6.82, D6.83). Its capacity is 4 Passenger PP (none with a mortar, FT, or 6 FP AAMG as MA) and 8 Rider PP (D6.81). An inherent Carrier HS is worth 2 VP (p. 100). |
| R65.19 | Which counter is the crew that leaves a vehicle? | Pass 65; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-23 ("start on Pass 65 without my intervention"), open to a veto. One member (`GamePlanner.LeavingCrew`) places it on every path (Survival, Abandonment, Recall, dismount): the vehicle nationality's `asl:vehicle-crew` definition, or the Carrier's HS. It replaces `CrewCounter`'s and `AbandonEvents`' lookups of the first infantry crew. An older game's recorded `instance-created` keeps the infantry crew it placed. |
| R65.20 | How are a crew's own acts played? | Pass 65; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-23 ("start on Pass 65 without my intervention"), open to a veto. Voluntary Abandonment (D5.41) is an MPh action of an unmoved vehicle with no fire counter, with D5.411's self-destruction as a choice (a non-burning wreck with no CS, or every MG and the MA malfunctioned). Re-crewing (D5.42) by Infantry in the Location at the start of its MPh, spending all its MF, by a new containment role, keeping its id, its Stun +1, and its Armor Leader (D3.43); barred in the Player Turn the old crew left and for a Recalled crew into its own AFV (D5.341). MG Removal (D6.631) as an option of Abandonment for a MG of 3 FP or less, taken as the nationality's LMG, the vehicle Disabled until the LMG returns. D5.8's fire counter for a leaving crew is a Rules member. |
| R65.21 | How are the TC, Crew Survival, and Brew Up built? | Pass 65; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-23 ("start on Pass 65 without my intervention"), open to a veto. The Immobilization TC's first cause on the mortar path and the IFT's Vehicle line; its second cause (D5.5) a Rules member that recomputes a Direct Fire ordnance shot's To Kill result on an Original DR of 5 at an already Immobile target, a Burn, an Eliminated, or an automatic Shock calling for the TC. One Crew Survival member over every path, with Shocked and Abandoned among the vehicle's facts; Survival on the IFT's Vehicle-line kill of an armed vehicle; Hazardous Movement for survivors for the phase (D5.6); the red CS#'s -1 at each burn test (D5.7); Brew Up's Survival on every destruction path that does not burn (D9.2). |
| R65.22 | How is a vehicle captured and used? | Pass 65; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-23 ("start on Pass 65 without my intervention"), open to a veto. An Abandoned AFV is captured at the end of the CCPh, automatically with no enemy Personnel present, or by a capture attempt in vehicle CC at -1 with A11.61's CE and Immobile DRM; the event `vehicle-captured` names the captor's side. A Temporary Driver runs a captured unarmed vehicle for an MMC captor (A21.21). A Temporary crew enters by re-crewing, under A21.22's penalties (halved MP, the start dr, captured MG and ordnance, red MP). A SMC crew fires the AAMG only while CE, is Wounded on a Stun, and is eliminated on a Recall (D5.343). A manned captured vehicle holds Control for its captor (A26.12), lifting ruling R24.5 for a vehicle captured by the new event; older records keep R11.16's state. |
| R65.23 | How are wrecks attacked and Scrounged? | Pass 65; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-23 ("start on Pass 65 without my intervention"), open to a veto. A non-burning wreck is a target of ordnance on the Vehicle Target Type, of the IFT, and of a FT or MOL, as its vehicle; only a Burn matters, and it places a Blaze (D10.1). Scrounging (D10.5 to D10.52) is an RPh action of an unbroken, non-berserk MMC not in Melee, at a non-burning wreck or an Abandoned vehicle not Scrounged or Disabled: a dr of 1 to 3 takes that many qualifying SW by Random Selection (a BMG not fixed, an AAMG, a mortar MA), a MG becoming the nationality's LMG; the unit and its SW are TI, and the vehicle is marked Scrounged. (The LMG's dm form is amended by R65.30.) |
| R65.24 | What does the pass move on? | Pass 65; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-23 ("start on Pass 65 without my intervention"), open to a veto. To pass 100: unloading in Bypass, and fire from a CAFP. To pass 105: an unarmored Target Facing under D5.311. To pass 75: A11.52's capture of Passengers, rearming with scrounged small arms, and captured vehicles' VP. To pass 70: CC against a wreck, and a Carrier HS recombining without a TC. To the backlog: Cavalry and motorcycle Riders, a Rider's PP against a towed Gun's ammunition, and Bailing Out in Bypass. Each has a row in the backlog's section 71.1. |
| R65.25 | How does the pass keep its reads of units aboard cheap? | Pass 65; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-23 ("start on Pass 65 without my intervention"), open to a veto. `ObjectIndex` gains the PRC of each vehicle, built in its single pass over the state, and every Passenger, Rider, and crew-inside read of the pass goes through `MemoOf(state)`, with no per-unit `Aboard`, `Passengers`, or `At` in a loop. `KeepPassengers` keeps its once-per-event scan. Measured in the Studio on a copy of `dl-tractor-works-02` at revision 1316 against the pass's base; a rise beyond the noise of three runs is a fault to find. |
| R65.26 | Where do D5.311 and A7.308, which increment 2b left, go? | Pass 65; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-23 ("start on Pass 65 without my intervention"), open to a veto. To a small increment 2k after 2j, or to the backlog if the session's end came first. Both were built in 2k: A7.308 for the IFT, with ruling R25.3's one-vehicle refusal lifted where the attack carries `prc`, and D5.311's crew attacked as unarmored. |
| R65.27 | Does a Passenger's Prep Fire bar its vehicle's own PFPh fire? | Pass 65; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-23 ("start on Pass 65 without my intervention"), open to a veto. D.3 (p. 192) bars only the vehicle's MPh move and its MPh and AFPh fire. The build marks the vehicle Prep Fired, the path the projector, the movement planner, and the fire bars already read, which also bars that PFPh fire. Kept: the vehicle fires its own weapons before its Passengers in the PFPh; D.3's exact reading is a backlog row. |
| R65.28 | May a crew that mans no Gun be fired at? | Pass 65; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-23 ("start on Pass 65 without my intervention"), open to a veto. Yes: it is an ordinary Infantry target, alone or with other units (A1.123), on the IFT and the ordnance Infantry Target Type. A Gun's crew keeps ruling R8.3's reading. The manning is the fact `gunCrews`, taken from the record at replay. It only turns refusals into plans. Built in increment 2k. |
| R65.29 | May a crew that mans no Gun be advanced against and fought in CC? | Pass 65; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-23 ("start on Pass 65 without my intervention"), open to a veto. Yes, as ordinary Infantry (A1.123): the CC package counts it a MMC, and Casualty Reduction eliminates it. A Gun's crew keeps ruling R24.3's reading. It only turns refusals into plans. Built in increment 2k. |
| R65.30 | Is a scrounged MG dismantled? (amends R65.23) | Pass 65; decided under the user's word of 2026-10-23 ("start on Pass 65 without my intervention"), open to a veto. No: it becomes the nationality's LMG, assembled. D10.52 says "in its dm form if possible", and A9.8 gives a LMG no dm form; the engine also refuses to assemble a LMG. A mortar MA would be dismantled, but no catalog vehicle has one. |
